# Hazard log

| Item | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Product | Harbour |
| Manufacturer | Thapsus Cargo Ltd |
| Standard | [DCB0129](https://adultsocialcare.standards.nhs.uk/published-standards/clinical-risk-management-its-application-in-the-manufacture-of-health-it-systems) |
| Process | [clinical-risk-management-plan.md](clinical-risk-management-plan.md) (risk matrix in section 3.2, acceptability in 3.3) |
| Opened | 11 October 2026, Phase 0, with the day-one hazards from master prompt section 5.2 and the rules in section 10.3 |
| Clinical approval | Not yet reviewed. All ratings below are engineering proposals awaiting `[CSO name - human action]` |

## How to read and maintain this log

- IDs are permanent and referenced from code and tests. Never renumber or reuse an ID. Add new hazards at the end with the next number, using the `hazard` skill.
- **Initial risk** is the risk with no Harbour-specific controls (a generic digital record built without these mitigations). **Target residual risk** is the rating expected once every listed control is implemented and verified. A hazard moves to `controlled` only when the CSO has seen the verification evidence and accepted the residual risk.
- Ratings use severity (Minor, Significant, Considerable, Major, Catastrophic), likelihood (Very low, Low, Medium, High, Very high) and the resulting risk level 1 to 5 from the DCB0129 matrix in the plan.
- **Controls** are grouped as Design, Test and Training/user documentation. Each control says when it lands. "Built in Phase 0" means the control exists in the Phase 0 foundations release; everything else is planned.
- **Verification** lists the automated tests that prove the controls. Every test name contains the hazard ID so `grep -rn "HZ-004" apps packages` finds the evidence. Test names below are the planned titles; keep them when the test is written. Maestro flow files are named `hazard-HZ-NNN-<slug>.yaml`.
- A PR labelled `clinical` that adds, changes or removes a control must update the relevant entry here in the same PR (the CI docs gate enforces this).
- "Deployer control" marks a control that the tenant must put in place; these are carried into the [DCB0160 deployment template](dcb0160-deployment-template.md).

## HZ-001 Wrong-person record selection

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-001 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `people` module (lead engineer); `medicines` module from Phase 2 |
| Source | Master prompt 5.2 (day-one hazard), 10 (header on every screen), 10.2 (wristband or photo confirm) |

**Description**: a user opens, reads or writes to the wrong person's record. They record a note, chart entry or medication administration against the wrong person, or act on the wrong person's care plan, allergies, diet or resuscitation status.

**Causes**

- Same or similar names at one location (for example a couple sharing a family name).
- Mis-tap on a list on a small screen; the list re-orders while scrolling because sync delivers changes.
- The app advances automatically to the next person after a save.
- Missing, old or poor-quality photo.
- A shared device left open on the previous user's or previous person's screen.
- A room move not yet recorded.
- A notification deep link that opens a different person from the one named in the notification.
- Duplicate person records created by data entry or import.
- Choosing from search results a person with the same name, a former resident, or the near match for a mistyped NHS number.

**Effects**

- Medication given to the wrong person: overdose for one person, omission for the other.
- Care given contrary to the person's allergies, resuscitation decision, IDDSI diet level or moving and handling plan.
- An inaccurate legal record; a confidentiality breach.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Point-of-care header on every person screen shows photo, full and preferred name, date of birth, room, allergies and communication needs (master prompt 10). The offline person table carries photo and communication needs (`packages/sync`) | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Every Care mode form pins a compact identity bar (photo, name, allergies, communication needs, resuscitation status) while it scrolls, names the person in its title, and shows Save only once the person has loaded | Built in Phase 1 (mobile, clinical reviews C7 and round 3 M4) |
| Design | The web record shows the point-of-care header above every tab of the person's record (overview, care plan, assessments, risk assessments, notes, charts, body map, advance care, activities and visits, history), with the resuscitation status and IDDSI levels (HZ-015, HZ-016). Each tab renders the header itself, so every tab move reads it again (HZ-002); its data is checked against the API's schema when it arrives, and a person who cannot be read safely shows a warning instead of details that may be wrong | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web) |
| Design | A missing photo shows a labelled placeholder ("No photo on record", icon plus words), never a silhouette that could be mistaken for a photo; the photo's date is shown and an old photo prompts an update | Phase 1 |
| Design | "Similar name" flag (icon plus words) in the header of every active person at a location who shares a family name or has a similar name with another | Phase 1 |
| Design | No automatic advance after saving: the app confirms the save naming the person and returns to that person or the list | Phase 1 |
| Design | Lists do not re-order under the user's finger while sync applies changes | Phase 1 |
| Design | Notification deep links carry tenant and person IDs; the target screen re-validates both before rendering | Phase 1 |
| Design | Probable duplicate check on create and import (same NHS number, or same date of birth and similar name) | Phase 1 |
| Design | eMAR requires positive identification for each administration: wristband barcode scan or photo confirmation | Phase 2 |
| Design | Shared and kiosk devices lock after 30 seconds by default and return to user sign-in, not to the last person ([kiosk.md](../compliance/kiosk.md)) | Phase 1 |
| Design | The room on the header comes from the dated bed history, which never puts two people in one bed; a death or discharge recorded against the wrong person is corrected with a reason (and a fresh sign-in for a death), cancelling the exit workflow and the death notification drafts ([people.md](../specs/people.md) rules 2 and 6) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 8 API) |
| Design | Search results show care roles the photo, name, preferred name, date of birth, NHS number, location, room and status (icon and word); names-and-rooms roles (such as the administrator) see the photo and age instead of the date of birth and NHS number, so two people of the same name can still be told apart. People come first, whole-word matches before prefix matches, then people receiving care before those being assessed or who have left, then name and date of birth; two people of the same name in the results are each flagged with a same-name warning. People who have left are hidden unless the user may and does ask for them; an NHS number is matched exactly, and a mistyped, 9- or 11-digit or dotted one is refused, never matched approximately ([platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rules 1, 3, 6 and 7; ADR-0070 keeps fuzzy name matching out) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Go-live waits until every current person has a photo on record and every active shared or kiosk device is enrolled in device management (onboarding items `people_ready` and `shared_devices_enrolled`, [platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rule 26). `GET /v1/onboarding` reads the people from their records. Harbour does not yet record a device's mode, so the device item is unknown and stays open ("Harbour does not record this yet"), blocking go-live until the kiosk work records modes and enrolment; the sample data item does the same until sample data mode (fail closed, #86 security review H1; platform-completions.md section 10e) | Phase 1 (domain rules and the checklist route built; device modes planned) |
| Design | The round view (`GET /v1/locations/{id}/round`) carries the point-of-care header (photo, names, allergies, communication needs, resuscitation status and the prescribed IDDSI food and drink levels, each `not_recorded` until recorded; HZ-016) on every stop, and a stop is shown only with the header of the person it is about; the handover returns the same header for each person on the items the reader may see | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (API; IDDSI levels added in review round 2) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1, Phase 2 |
| Design | On the web handover, each person's items sit under the compact point-of-care header (`PersonHeaderCompact`: photo, full name and room as a heading, allergies, resuscitation status, IDDSI food and drink levels, never blank, and communication needs, each labelled in every state); each item's link and note button name the person; items about someone the reader cannot see say so, never name them (#67 review round 1) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 5 web) |
| Design | On the phone's round (PR #123), each task card names the person as they are known and their communication needs by name, and each "Done", "Not done" and "Record the entry" button says the person in its spoken label; a task about someone whose record is not on the phone cannot be marked at all. The card does not show the photo, room, allergies, resuscitation status or IDDSI levels, which the person's record does, one tap away: whether that suffices is H-395 (#123 clinical review, M2 and M3) | Built (round screen) |
| Design | Personal details (PEOP-001, the web record's personal details tab and `PATCH /v1/people/{id}/details`): names, date of birth (1900 to today) and NHS number change only with `people.person.update` where it reaches the person and the version the form was opened at; an NHS number must pass the modulus 11 check and be unique in the organisation (a number already on another record is refused, never merged: in words when the caller can see that record; when they cannot, without saying whose or where, audited as denied without the number, limited to three such refusals per member an hour, counted before the number is looked up so parallel requests cannot pass the limit together, and a task asks the owner to look for a duplicate record, #119 review M1 and round 2); a person with no NHS number needs a local identifier; every earlier version stays in the record's history, and the header is read again on the next page | Built in Phase 1 (PEOP-001 web) |
| Training and user documentation | Care worker guide: check photo and date of birth before every administration and entry. Deployer control: photos taken at admission and refreshed under the tenant's policy | Phase 1 |
| Design | The web admission tab (PEOP-007) offers only beds free today in rooms in use, re-checked by the API for the first day; shows the room and bed now with every move before it, a move recorded after its day flagged in words; and the header's room follows the history after an admission or a move. Enquiries are recorded on the web with names and a date of birth only, after a prompt to search first so a returning person keeps one record; an enquiry's id sent again is answered only for the caller's own enquiry with the same details, never with anyone's record, and the same id with other details is refused so the form starts a new enquiry (PEOP-005 and PEOP-007 web) | Built in Phase 1 (web) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

## HZ-002 Stale offline data shown as current

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-002 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `packages/sync` and `apps/mobile` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 5.2, 17.1, 17.2 |

**Description**: the mobile app shows information that has changed on the server since the device last synced (a new allergy, a stopped medicine, a changed resuscitation decision, a new risk, a dose already given by a colleague) and the user treats it as current.

**Causes**

- No connectivity (basements, rural home care, lifts) for long periods.
- A sync stream filter that fails to deliver an update to a device that should receive it.
- The app left in the background; a device used after days off.
- The user does not notice the device is offline.
- PowerSync service outage.
- An assignment change not yet reflected on the device.
- A server restore to a point in time earlier than data the device already holds ([restore runbook](../runbooks/restore.md)).
- On the web, a header drawn once and kept while the person moves between tabs (a shared layout), or a page left open, so a colleague's change (a withdrawn DNACPR, a new allergy) does not show.

**Effects**

- A stopped medicine given, or a medicine given despite a newly recorded allergy.
- A new care-plan instruction (for example a changed IDDSI level) missed.
- A dose given twice because a colleague's administration is not yet visible (see HZ-004).
- Resuscitation attempted or withheld contrary to a newly recorded decision.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Sync status banner on every Care-mode screen showing online or offline state, the time of the last successful sync and the number of queued changes | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | The web point-of-care header is read again from the API on every tab move, every two minutes while the page is in view and when the person comes back to the window; it says when it was read ("Checked at 14:05") and has a "Check again" button. The interval is a product constant (`HEADER_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS`), not a rate-table figure | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web) |
| Design | Stale-data warning: when the last successful sync is older than the threshold the banner switches to a warning state (icon plus words, for example "Last updated 3 hours ago. Information may be out of date"). The threshold is a named constant in `packages/sync`; the CSO confirms its value `[CSO decision - human action]` | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Reference data for assigned people (care plan summary, allergies, medicines, photo, communication needs) is never evicted while the assignment stands (`NEVER_EVICT_TABLES` in `packages/sync`) | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Safety-critical panels (allergies, medicines, resuscitation decision) show when that data was last confirmed from the server | Phase 1 |
| Design | Last-given time for every dose shows its source (this device, or synced from the server at a stated time) | Phase 2 |
| Design | High-priority changes (new allergy, medicine stopped, resuscitation decision changed) travel on a priority sync stream and a push that prompts the app to sync; the push content carries no personal data | Phase 2 |
| Design | Sync stream filters are tested separately from RLS, because logical replication bypasses RLS (shared with HZ-011) | Phase 0 gate |
| Design | Offline, search covers only what the device has synced and labels its results "This device only, last synced at HH:MM" ([platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rule 7; ADR-0070) | Phase 1 (mobile step) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1, Phase 2 |
| Training and user documentation | Care worker guide explains the banner states. Deployer control: critical changes (allergy, medicine stopped, resuscitation decision) are also handed over verbally at shift change; the nightly downtime pack (Phase 1) is kept where connectivity is poor | Phase 1 |
| Design | On the web home page (INSI-001, `feat/web-dashboards`, docs/specs/insights.md): the dashboard says when its counts were worked out (taken before the reads); it is read again when the person returns to the tab after more than five minutes, measured on the device's monotonic clock from when the counts arrived (never the device's wall clock against the server's), and "Read the counts again" reads it now. No background timer | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Justification for level 3**: offline operation is itself a safety control (continuity of care where connectivity is poor), and a device cannot know about changes it has not received. Harbour makes the age of the data visible and prioritises critical changes; the remaining risk depends on deployer controls (verbal handover). Further reduction would mean blocking offline use, which creates a larger hazard. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/ui-native/src/logic/sync-banner.test.ts`: `warns that offline data may be out of date past the threshold (HZ-002)`; stale warning kept until caught up (threshold 15 minutes, ADR-0029; CSO to confirm) | Passing (Phase 0) |
| `apps/web/e2e/care-record.spec.ts` (Playwright with axe, WCAG 2.2 AA): `hazard HZ-002 hazard HZ-015 a colleague's change to the CPR decision shows in the header after one tab move` (a second browser context records and then withdraws a DNACPR decision; the first sees each change after one tab move, and after "Check again") | Passing (local run; CI e2e-web on the PR) |
| `packages/sync`: `hazard HZ-002 banner state is stale when the last successful sync exceeds the threshold` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `packages/sync/test/care-streams.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-002 reference data for assigned people is never evicted while the assignment stands` (care profile, active care plan, risk assessments, assessment results and advance care records stay whatever the window) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/ui-native`: `hazard HZ-002 allergy, medicines and resuscitation panels show when data was last confirmed` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `apps/mobile` Maestro `hazard-HZ-002-offline-banner.yaml`: go offline, pass the threshold, banner shows the stale warning and the last-sync time | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `packages/sync`: `hazard HZ-002 priority stream delivers allergy and medicine-stopped changes before routine data` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/sync-summary.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-002 the sync status on Today` (last successful sync as UK date and time, changes waiting to upload, the stale warning, never synced, demo mode); `screens.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-002 Today shows the last successful sync and the changes waiting to upload` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/web/e2e/dashboard.spec.ts`: the manager journey reads the counts again and sees "Counts worked out at" | Passing (Phase 1, web) |

## HZ-003 Missed medication administration

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-003 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `medicines` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 5.2, 10.2, 10.3; [NICE SC1](https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/SC1/chapter/1-Recommendations); [CQC time-sensitive medicines](https://www.cqc.org.uk/guidance-providers/adult-social-care/time-sensitive-medicines) |

**Description**: a scheduled dose is not given, or not recorded, and the omission is not noticed in time. The highest risk is for time-critical medicines (for example Parkinson's medicines, insulin, anti-epileptics).

**Causes**

- The dose does not appear on the round (scheduling or frequency expansion error; see also HZ-009).
- The person is not assigned to anyone for the round.
- The carer is interrupted mid-round; the round is closed with doses outstanding.
- Medicine not available and no stock escalation.
- A new or changed prescription not yet on the MAR (see HZ-005).
- Reminder or escalation not delivered (offline device, failed job) or ignored (HZ-008).
- The person's rounds stay stopped after the people lifecycle stopped them: a discharge or death
  recorded in error is corrected, or a return from hospital is recorded, and the rounds (with
  medicines rounds from Phase 2) the exit or the absence cancelled are not brought back (slice 8
  review, clinical finding H1).
- Rounds whose times passed before a late return or correction was recorded stay cancelled with
  nothing caught up, and the review task does not say which rounds were missed; or rounds that
  ended long before the return are counted (slice 8 review round 3, clinical finding M2).
- A correction or the migration that coded existing tasks brings back tasks a different exit, or
  a manager, stopped, because it matched reason text a user can type (round 3, security finding
  L5).
- A missed round is caught up beside a later occurrence that is open again (paracetamol due at
  08:00 and 12:00 both due at 12:10 after a correction), or a missed dose is made due again,
  which is a treatment decision (round 4, clinical finding C1).
- A stay longer than the 48-hour materialisation horizon loses the rounds after it, and a
  correction made more than 49 hours after the exit adds an extra overdue round (round 4, C5).
- A death or discharge corrected after its exit workflow has closed restores nothing (round 4,
  C6).
- A care plan action in the medicines domain makes rounds that are ticked off as if they were
  doses; a plan changed while the person is away leaves the old schedule's paused rounds to be
  brought back on their return; the hourly sweep makes a second series for an action whose rounds
  the lifecycle stopped (slice 5 build).
- Medicines support that lives in other plan domains (creams under skin, thickeners under
  nutrition), or in the medicines domain, never appears on the round, so staff read the round as
  the whole of the person's care and the support is missed until the eMAR exists (slice 5 review
  round 2, C-M7).

**Effects**: deterioration from an omitted time-critical medicine (rigidity and swallowing difficulty in Parkinson's, hypo- or hyperglycaemia, seizures), untreated pain or infection.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Notification centre with escalation chains used by the medicines rules | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Round view by time slot lists every due dose for assigned people; a dose stays outstanding until an outcome is recorded (given, refused, omitted with coded reason, not available, self-administered, given by family, withheld with reason) | Phase 2 |
| Design | The app refuses to record an administration without a selected person, a dose and an outcome (master prompt 10.3) | Phase 2 |
| Design | Late warnings with time-critical rules; time-critical medicines escalate at an earlier threshold to the team leader, then the nurse in charge or on-call | Phase 2 |
| Design | A round cannot be closed with outstanding doses without a reason; outstanding doses appear in the auto-compiled handover | Phase 2 |
| Design | On-device reminders for due doses that work offline | Phase 2 |
| Design | A dose due for a person not assigned to any staff member on the shift alerts the shift lead | Phase 2 |
| Design | "Not available" creates a stock and ordering task | Phase 2 |
| Design | Expired medicines competence blocks administration (manager override logged) | Phase 2 |
| Design | The tasks and series the people lifecycle stops carry a code (`task.cancel_code`, `task_series.inactive_code`: paused while away, ended at exit) and, when an exit stopped them, that exit (`stopped_by_exit_id`). Migration 0023 codes existing rows only from the lifecycle event that stopped them (the outbox event written in the same transaction), never from reason text. A return brings back the rounds paused while the person was away; correcting a discharge or death recorded in error brings back only what that exit ended and starts its series again ([people.md](../specs/people.md) rules 1 and 2). Whether rounds restart without a person confirming them is for the CSO (H-89) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Of the tasks brought back (`planTaskRestore`), only those whose window ends at or after the return count. A task an entry answered while it was stopped is restored as done, never due again (`restore_code` answered_while_stopped; HZ-004; H-91). One still in its window reopens. Of those whose window passed, the latest occurrence of each series (and each one-off task) is caught up once, due now with a fresh window of its own length and its escalation reset (`restore_code` caught_up), unless a later occurrence of the series is done or open again, so two turns or doses never fall due minutes apart; a missed `medicines.*` task is never made due again, because giving a late dose is a treatment decision ([medical-device boundary](../compliance/medical-device-boundary.md)): it is listed as missed for the nurse in charge. The others stay cancelled, coded as missed (H-92, H-131). One urgent review task goes to the nurse in charge and lists every round, its due time and whether it was caught up (`task.details.missedTasks`, `listMissedRounds`): missed medicines rounds first and never dropped, then the rest newest first, at most 50 in all and the rest counted, so the latest rounds and every missed dose are always listed (review round 5, M2). Shown on screen, in UK time, on the web task list (`/tasks`, HZ-006) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review rounds 3 to 5) |
| Design | Before planning, a return or correction makes the rounds that would have fallen due since the series stopped making tasks (`fillStoppedGap`), so a stay longer than the 48-hour materialisation horizon loses no round and a correction long after the exit adds no extra overdue one (review round 4, C5). It looks back to 00:00 UK time seven days before (`restoreLookbackStart`); when a series had rounds before that, they are not made, and the review stores the date (`task.details.missedTasksNotListedBefore`) and says "Rounds before dd/mm/yyyy are not all listed: check the record." (review round 5, M2). Whether seven days is the right limit is for the CSO (H-137) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review rounds 4 and 5) |
| Design | A correction finds the latest exit of that kind whatever its state, so a death or discharge corrected after its exit workflow has closed still restores the status the person had and what that exit stopped, and the exit is named for withdrawing its notifications; whether such a correction needs more (a second person, the Registered Manager) is for the CSO (H-135) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review round 4) |
| Design | A correction returns the person to the status recorded on the exit (active, on leave or in hospital, `exit_record.status_before`) from when that status began; back to on leave or in hospital, what the exit ended waits for the return, paused, and nothing is due while the person is away (HZ-008; H-93) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review round 3) |
| Design | Downtime pack medicines section (OPS-028): until the eMAR exists it names where the location keeps the paper MAR (set per location); with the eMAR it lists the scheduled doses still due while the pack is in use, time-critical first and in time order, never doses already given, and every PRN medicine with when it was last given ([operations.md](../specs/operations.md) rules 1 and 7) | Domain interface built in Phase 1 slice 11; data in Phase 2 |
| Design | Care plan rounds (care.md rule 14): an action in the medicines domain cannot generate tasks (refused when written and at publication), because medicines rounds come from the medicines record (H-151); ending a series for a plan change also takes the pause or exit code off its cancelled rounds, so a return or correction never brings back a schedule the plan dropped; a series the lifecycle stopped stays with its action, and the sweep never makes a second one; a stay in hospital pauses the plan's rounds and the return brings the same rounds back | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 |
| Design | A medicines action says "Not a round: see the medicines record" on the plan (`CarePlanActionItem.notice`) and on the person's stop in the round view (`RoundStop.notRounds`), so the round never reads as the whole of their care; a person with no task on the round has no stop, so the round view shows no medicines notice for them (their plan still does); whether medicines support written in other domains needs the same, and whether rounds must say medicines are not on them until the eMAR, is for the CSO (H-151) | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (review round 2, C-M7) |
| Design | The web task list (`/tasks`) never lets old open tasks hide today's: the API returns the window's own tasks first, then open tasks due before it, the most recent first, and counts those left out (`earlierOmitted`; `earlier` in the `platform.task.list` audit); the page lists the window's tasks and "Open from earlier" apart, 20 at a time with "Show more", says how many older tasks are not listed, and says when the window itself has more than 1,000 tasks. Tests: `apps/api/test/tasks.test.ts` `hazard HZ-003 lists the window's own tasks first when more than 1,000 older open tasks exist, and counts the older ones left out`; `apps/web/src/lib/tasks.test.ts` `hazard HZ-003 a long task list stays quick and keeps today's tasks in sight`; `apps/web/e2e/tasks.spec.ts` | Built (web, Phase 1 slice 9) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 2 |
| Design | On the web round (slice 5 web part 2): each task's time state (still to come, due now, overdue, overdue and escalated) in an icon and words, urgent and catch-up tasks marked, the plan action as its plan words it with the earlier-plan notice (HZ-024), and each stop's actions that are not rounds ("Not a round: see the medicines record"); a view that cannot list every open task says so; marking a task not done needs a reason; a task that needs a record links to the record form it names (the chart, notes, body map or activities), never done from the round; every stop carries the compact point-of-care header (photo, name and room, allergies, resuscitation status, IDDSI levels, communication needs; HZ-001) and every action names the task and the person; the page reads the round again when a task's state can next change, or at least every 5 minutes, and says when it was read (#68 review round 1; stale-page policy for the CSO, H-270); while a "not done" reason is typed the re-read waits (so the task neither changes under the form nor leaves the list with the reason), and the reason is sent against the version seen when the form was opened, or, if it changed, the form says "This task changed while you were typing. Check it before saving." (#68 review round 2; `RoundTaskActions.test.tsx`, HZ-003) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 5 web part 2) |
| Training and user documentation | eMAR training and competency assessment; deployer control: tenant policy on time-critical medicines and round allocation | Phase 2 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Justification for level 3**: giving a medicine is a human act that Harbour cannot perform. Harbour makes every outstanding dose visible, escalates time-critical ones and blocks closing a round silently; the remaining risk sits with staffing and practice, which the tenant controls. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-003 expands every supported frequency into the correct doses across a 28-day cycle` (table-driven) | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-003 a dose with no recorded outcome stays outstanding after its window` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-003 time-critical medicines escalate at the earlier threshold` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-003 a round cannot close with outstanding doses without a reason` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-003 refuses an administration without person, dose and outcome` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-003 an overdue time-critical dose escalates to the nurse in charge` | Planned, Phase 2 |

| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-003 brings back the rounds and point-of-care tasks the death ended, and cancels the exit task` (the series starts again; the safety follow-ups were never stopped) and `hazards HZ-003 and HZ-023 record the return: rounds resume, ...` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/db/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-003 codes the tasks the lifecycle cancels, and only cancelled tasks carry a code` and `hazard HZ-003 codes only what a lifecycle event stopped, with the exit that stopped it (backfill)` (migration 0023 codes existing rows from the event that stopped them; a task a manager cancelled with the same words is left alone) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/tasks.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-003 and HZ-004 tasks brought back after a return or a correction` (table-driven: a round answered offline restored as done at 10:05 and 10:10; a round still in its window; a window closed before the return; the latest missed round of each series caught up once; a series whose later occurrence was done) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-003 restores a round an offline entry answered as done, never due again, when a death recorded in error is corrected (10:05, 10:08, 10:15, 10:20)`, `hazard HZ-003 codes the rounds missed during a late-recorded return, catches each series up once and lists them for review` and `hazards HZ-003 and HZ-008 correct a death recorded in error while the person was in hospital back to in hospital, with rounds still paused` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/people/lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-003 and HZ-008 correct ...` (table-driven: back to active, on leave or in hospital as recorded on the exit; any other status refused; an exit with no recorded status returns to active) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/tasks.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-003 does not catch up a series whose later occurrence is open again (turns at 08:00 and 12:00, corrected at 12:10; C1)`, `hazard HZ-003 makes only the dose still in its window due (paracetamol ...)`, `hazard HZ-003 never makes a missed medicines dose due again: it is listed for the nurse in charge (C1)`, `hazard HZ-008 gives a caught-up round a fresh window as long as its own, so it does not escalate at once`; `packages/domain/src/people/exit.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-001 and HZ-003 cancel a death recorded in error while returns are awaited, never once closed` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `slice 8 review, round 4`: `hazard HZ-003 never makes a missed medicines dose due again after a late return: it is listed (C1)`, `hazard HZ-003 makes the rounds a stay longer than the 48-hour horizon would have had, from the return, and catches up once (C5)`, `hazard HZ-003 adds no extra overdue round to a correction made long after the exit (C5)`, `hazard HZ-003 restores the status and the rounds when a death is corrected after its exit workflow has closed (C6)`; the round 3 test of a late return now expects a turn missed beside a later one to stay missed | Passing (Phase 1) |

## HZ-004 Duplicated medication administration

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-004 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `medicines` module and `packages/sync` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 5.2, 6 (conflict model), 10.3, 17.1 |

**Description**: the same dose is given twice, by the same or different staff, or is recorded twice so that later dosing decisions rest on a false record.

**Causes**

- Two devices offline both record the same scheduled dose.
- Last-given time not visible; PRN minimum interval or 24-hour maximum not enforced.
- Upload retries create duplicate rows (non-idempotent writes); a double tap on save.
- Stale data (HZ-002), clock drift (HZ-010) or daylight-saving errors (HZ-009) make an interval look elapsed.
- A database restore loses acknowledged administrations and staff re-record or re-give ([restore runbook](../runbooks/restore.md)).
- A round answered offline before a death recorded in error, or before a move, is reopened by the correction or the return as if never done, so it is done again (slice 8 review round 3, clinical finding H1).
- A second entry for a round already done, or an entry by someone who may not complete the round, is kept with nothing to show it was not linked and nobody to review it (round 3, clinical finding M4).
- A medicines entry refused for a closed round, or one not the author's, leaves no trace on the server; two entries sent at once both complete the round; a second entry for an answered cancelled round is not reviewed; a replayed medicines entry is refused; a user gives themselves a task to complete it (round 4, C2 to C4, S-L5, S1).
- Two rounds of one care plan action due at once because their windows overlap (hourly checks,
  a clock-change night), so an entry may answer either and one is done twice; a new plan version
  cancels and remakes rounds staff already hold on their devices (slice 5 build).
- Windows worked out from the clock face rather than real time: on the night the clocks go
  forward 01:00 and 02:00 are the same instant, so two rounds fall due together with windows cut
  to nothing (slice 5 review round 2, C-M1).
- The author's permission for an entry is judged when the device uploads, not when the care was given: a carer acting up under a time-boxed grant gives a dose offline, the grant ends before the upload, the entry is kept as a conflict, and the round stays open and overdue as if nothing was done, inviting a second dose (round 6, clinical finding M).

**Effects**: overdose (opioids, insulin, paracetamol, anticoagulants), hypoglycaemia, respiratory depression, death.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Point-of-care events are append-only with client-generated UUIDs and idempotent inserts, so a retried upload cannot create a second row (conflict model, ADR-0001) | Pattern built in Phase 0; applied to administrations in Phase 2 |
| Design | Duplicate administration within the minimum interval is blocked and needs an override reason (master prompt 10.3) | Phase 2 |
| Design | Last-given time, by whom and from which source, shown for every PRN and regular dose | Phase 2 |
| Design | PRN maximum in 24 hours enforced from the PRN protocol | Phase 2 |
| Design | A MAR row locks once synced; only referenced correction events are allowed | Phase 2 |
| Design | On sync, two administrations for the same scheduled dose from different devices go to a conflict review screen for the nurse in charge; nothing is merged silently | Phase 2 |
| Design | Save disabled after the first tap until the result returns; the confirmation names person, medicine and dose | Phase 2 |
| Design | Intervals measured in elapsed UTC time, drift-corrected where drift is known (HZ-009, HZ-010) | Phase 2 |
| Design | A task the people lifecycle stopped, which an entry answered while it was stopped, is restored as done when the return or correction is recorded, never reopened (HZ-003; H-91) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review round 3) |
| Design | Whether an entry's author may complete the task it names is judged when the care was recorded (`entryPermissionTime`): the permission the task names, held at its location at the entry's `occurredAt` (never later than the server's clock, which the clock-drift limit already bounds, and never earlier than the task was made, since a device can only name a task it holds, so an entry backdated to an earlier grant completes nothing). A grant that ended before the upload still counts; a revoked grant never counts, whenever the care was recorded. Holding the permission when the entry arrives also counts. Done or skipped by hand on the task route is judged at the server's time. A refused completion is audited only by the upload that saved the entry. The CSO confirms the rule (H-138, with H-132) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review round 6) |
| Design | What an entry did to the task it names is stored on the entry (`task_outcome`: completed, task cancelled, task already closed, not linked, medicines conflict; `checkTaskLink`). An entry for a round already done or skipped, for a cancelled round an earlier entry already answered, or one its author may not complete, is kept, flagged and listed on one review task for the nurse in charge (`care.entry_review`). For a `medicines.*` task in any of those cases the entry is kept as `medicines_conflict`, never completing the task, with a separate urgent conflict review (`care.entry_conflict_review`), and the user is told "Tell the nurse in charge now. Only the person who gave a medicine records it."; whether to refuse such an entry instead is for the CSO (H-132). Both reviews stay open when the person dies or leaves after the entry: they look back at what was recorded, so an exit recorded after them never cancels them (`KEPT_AT_EXIT_TASK_KINDS`; review round 5, M1). Every entry view shows the outcome as an icon and words | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review rounds 3 to 5) |
| Design | Planning an entry's link locks the task row until the entry is written, so of two entries sent at once only one completes the round and the other is planned against the closed round (reviewed, urgently for medicines); an update that still finds the task changed raises the review rather than doing nothing. A replayed upload returns the stored entry before anything is planned again (round 4, C3, S-L5) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review round 4) |
| Design | A task that needs a permission (a clinical check, a medicines round) is given only to someone who holds that permission at its location, whoever assigns it, themselves included, because being the assignee lets someone complete it; the refusal is audited (round 4, S1). A task about a person created or scheduled with an assignee is checked the same way, so the rounds a series makes never go to someone who could not complete them. At completion, by the task route, by an entry or by recording a return check, the assignee completes the task only while they could still be given it (`assigneeMayComplete`), so an acting-up grant that has expired or been revoked no longer lets them mark it done or skipped and silence its escalation; the refusal is audited and an entry is kept and reviewed instead. General tasks and an owner's investigation actions are exempt, as are the Care mode sync tasks the escalation gives to a Registered Manager elsewhere when no one at their location can act (H-142; nobody else can be given one without its permission) (round 5, S1 follow-on) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review rounds 4 and 5) |
| Design | Care plan rounds (care.md rule 14): each round's window comes from the real instants of the round and its neighbours (`roundTimes`), never the clock face: each side is under half the real gap and at most the tenant's figure, so no instant is inside two windows and no window is ever nothing; consecutive occurrences under 10 minutes apart on the clock face are refused when the action is written, for every frequency (`roundGapProblem`: within a day, across midnight, from one weekday to the next, across the cycle; a weekly Monday 23:55 and Tuesday 00:00 is refused), and any two times a clock change brings under 10 minutes apart are one round (01:00 and 02:00, 01:30 and 02:30, or 01:05 and 02:00 on the spring night); the windows are the same however the range is cut, so it does not matter when the rounds are made (materialisation and the lifecycle's gap filling use the same rule). A new plan version carries an unchanged action's series and its rounds forward (same ids, same state), so no round on a device is cancelled and made again | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (window rule corrected in review round 2, C-M1) |
| Design | An entry a manager hands over for its owner (HZ-040) is judged as the owner's: the round it names completes only on the owner's permission when the care was recorded, never the sender's. It is taken only from a sender who has used the device, while the device's hold for that owner is open and within the count it reported, timed while the owner used the device (and before any revocation), about someone in the owner's care within the offline window, and it keeps its sender as "Sent by" (`handed_over_by`), so a manager cannot record care in a carer's name or complete their rounds unseen ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 6; #25 review round 2, H1; H-158) | Built in Phase 1 (Device integrity) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 2 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: one staff member allocated per person per round; conflict review is part of the nurse-in-charge role | Phase 2 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**. A duplicate now needs two staff on two offline devices giving the same dose to the same person in the same window despite round allocation, and it is detected and escalated on sync.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-004 blocks a second administration within the minimum interval unless an override reason is given` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-004 enforces the PRN maximum in 24 hours` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-004 replaying the same client UUID does not create a second administration` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-004 two devices recording the same scheduled dose create a conflict review task` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/db`: `hazard HZ-004 medication_administration has no UPDATE or DELETE grant` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/mobile` Maestro `hazard-HZ-004-offline-conflict.yaml`: two devices record the same dose offline; the nurse in charge sees the conflict on sync | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/tasks.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-004 and HZ-006 what an entry does to the task it names` (table-driven: completes an open round; keeps late evidence for a cancelled round; flags a round already done or skipped, and one the author may not complete, for review; refuses a medicines task not the author's, already done or already skipped; refuses another person's task) and `hazards HZ-003 and HZ-004 tasks brought back after a return or a correction` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `care.md rule 13: an entry and the task it names (HZ-004, HZ-006)`: `hazard HZ-004 keeps an entry for a round its author may not complete, unlinked and flagged, with a review for the nurse in charge`, `hazard HZ-004 keeps a second entry for a round already done, flagged, on the same review`, `hazard HZ-004 refuses an entry for a medicines task its author may not complete` and `... a medicines task already done (a second dose)`; `hazard HZ-003 restores a round an offline entry answered as done, never due again, ...` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/db/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-004 and HZ-006 an entry names its task only with what happened to it` (every entry table: a task and an outcome together, or neither) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/web/src/components/care/LifecycleBadges.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-004 an entry for a round already recorded, for review` and `hazard HZ-004 an entry its author could not link to the round, for review` (words and an icon) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/tasks.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-004 keeps a medicines entry ...` (by someone who may not complete it, for a task done or skipped, a second entry for an answered cancelled task: `medicines_conflict`, urgent), `hazard HZ-004 keeps a second entry for a cancelled round that already has evidence, for review (C4)`, `never completes a task except with the outcome completed`; `packages/domain/src/platform/access.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-004 and HZ-005 counts only the roles and grants for that location or the whole tenant` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-004 keeps an entry for a medicines task its author may not complete, never completing it, with an urgent conflict review (C2)` and `... a medicines task already done (a second dose?) ...`, `hazard HZ-004 lets only one of two entries sent at once complete a round; the other is reviewed (C3)`, `hazard HZ-004 sends a second medicines entry sent at once to the urgent conflict review (C3)`, `hazard HZ-004 reviews a second entry for a cancelled round that an entry already answered (C4)`, `hazard HZ-004 returns a replayed medicines entry as it was stored, raising nothing again (S-L5)`, `hazards HZ-004 and HZ-005 never gives a task to someone who cannot complete it, through the assign route itself (S1)`; `apps/web/src/components/care/LifecycleBadges.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-004 a medicines entry that could not complete its task tells the user to act now` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-004 keeps the urgent conflict review open when a death is recorded after it` (M1); `hazards HZ-004 and HZ-005 stop an assignee completing a clinical task once their acting-up grant has expired, by the task route or by an entry (S1 follow-on)` and `... has been revoked ...` (done and skipped refused and audited; the entry kept as `not_linked` with the refusal audited); `apps/api/test/tasks.test.ts`: `refuses, and audits, a one-off task given on creation to someone without its permission (S1)` and `refuses, and audits, a series whose rounds would go to someone without their permission (S1)` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/tasks.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-004 and HZ-006 when permission for an entry is judged` (when the care was recorded; no earlier than the task was made; no later than the server's clock); `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-004 completes a medicines round with an entry recorded under a grant that ended before upload` (the assignee's dose and another's), `hazard HZ-004 refuses an entry recorded before a grant was revoked: a revocation counts from when it is made`, `hazard HZ-004 does not let an entry backdated to an earlier grant complete a task made after it ended`, `hazards HZ-004 and HZ-005 let a care worker acting up complete a task by the task route and by an entry, reading their own roles and grants under row-level security` and `audits a refused completion once, for the upload that saved the entry, when a duplicate upload races it` (round 6) | Passing (Phase 1) |

| `apps/api/test/device-integrity.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 refuses a sender who never used the device: the device sends its report as the sender first (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 takes no more entries than the hold reported, however much the sender reports (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 refuses a hand-over once the device reports nothing waiting for the owner (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 refuses an entry timed before the owner first used the device (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 refuses an entry about someone never in the owner's care, though the manager may see them (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 refuses a hand-over id reused for another owner (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 takes a hand-over from a revoked device only for entries recorded before the revocation, the owner's own as well as a manager's` | Passing (Phase 1, Device integrity, #25 review round 2) |

## HZ-005 Unit or dose transcription error

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-005 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `medicines` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 5.2, 10.2; [NICE SC1](https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/SC1/chapter/1-Recommendations); [dm+d standard](https://standards.nhs.uk/published-standards/dictionary-of-medicines-and-devices) |

**Description**: the dose, unit, strength, form, route, frequency or timing is entered or transcribed wrongly onto the MAR from a prescription, discharge letter or pharmacy label. Typical errors: milligrams for micrograms, millilitres for milligrams with oral liquids, misplaced decimal points and tenfold errors, "u" read as zero, a weekly medicine entered as daily, the wrong strength or a look-alike product selected.

**Causes**: free-text entry; ambiguous abbreviations; adjacent similar options in a picker; trailing zeros and missing leading zeros; import mapping errors; manual transcription from discharge summaries; changes passed on verbally; the medicines check after a return from hospital recorded by someone who may only record movements, not reconcile medicines (slice 8 review round 3, security finding M2).

**Effects**: overdose or underdose, wrong medicine, harm up to death.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Medicines selected only from dm+d; strength and form come from dm+d, never free text | Phase 2 |
| Design | Structured dose: a number plus a unit from the units permitted for that dm+d product; "micrograms", "nanograms" and "units" always written in full; leading zero enforced (0.5, never .5); trailing zeros never displayed (5, never 5.0) | Phase 2 |
| Design | Non-daily schedules (for example once weekly) show the day name on every MAR row and in the round view | Phase 2 |
| Design | A new or changed MAR entry is "Not yet checked" until a second competent user, different from the transcriber, verifies it against the source document, as NICE SC1 recommends for transcription | Phase 2 |
| Design | The source document (prescription, discharge letter, label) is attached to or referenced from each MAR entry | Phase 2 |
| Design | Imported medicines arrive as "Not yet checked"; the import dry run reports unmapped units | Phase 2 |
| Design | Pharmacy MAR import replaces manual transcription where a pharmacy is integrated | Phase 5 |
| Design | No dose calculation or dose-range advice is offered (medical-device boundary) | All phases |
| Design | A return check (the discharge letter, medicines, allergies, resuscitation decisions, IDDSI levels, skin) is recorded only by someone holding the permission its task requires (`care.plan.write` for the clinical checks) or by its assignee while they could still be given it (round 5, S1 follow-on), and a check's task is given only to someone who holds that permission (HZ-004); checking belongings back in stays with `people.movement.record`, with or without a task. A refusal is audited after its transaction ([people.md](../specs/people.md) rule 10) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review rounds 3 and 4) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 2 |
| Training and user documentation | Transcription competency; deployer control: tenant transcription and second-check policy | Phase 2 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**, re-assessed when pharmacy import lands in Phase 5.

**Justification for level 3**: until pharmacy data is imported electronically, a person reads a source document and enters it. Structured entry and the second check reduce but do not remove that human step. Dose-range checking would cross the medical-device boundary. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-005 rejects a dose whose unit is not permitted for the dm+d product` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-005 formats doses with a leading zero, no trailing zero and micrograms in full` (table-driven) | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-005 a new or changed MAR entry stays not yet checked until a second competent user verifies it` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-005 the checker must be a different user from the transcriber` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-005 non-daily schedules show the day on every MAR row` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-005 imported medicines are created as not yet checked` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-005 and HZ-015 let only someone who may complete a return check's task record it` (a team leader, who records movements but not care plans, is refused the medicines, resuscitation and allergy checks, each refusal audited, and checks belongings back in; a check's assignee without its permission is refused and audited, and records it under an acting-up grant; the nurse in charge records the medicines check) | Passing (Phase 1) |
