## HZ-010 Device clock drift producing wrong recorded times

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-010 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `packages/sync`, `apps/api` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (device and server time), 10.3 (drift over 5 minutes) |

**Description**: a device clock is wrong (changed by the user, drifted, or reset after the battery ran flat), so times recorded on it are wrong: administration times, note times, visit check-ins; and interval checks on the device use the wrong "now".

**Causes**: manual time change; automatic time disabled; long periods offline; shared tablets without network time; deliberate back-dating.

**Effects**: wrong next-dose timing (overdose or omission), wrong chronology in the legal record, late entries not flagged, wrong notification deadlines, false visit times.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | A time typed on the phone that is later than the device clock by more than the tenant's tolerance (`sync.clock_drift_flag_minutes`, read from the rate table and cached; none until the device has read it) is refused, never moved to an earlier day (clinical review round 3, H1) | Built in Phase 1 (mobile) |
| Design | Held entries never act on the device clock: a clock earlier than when the hold started, or than the last time the server was reached, changes nothing, and a clock that jumped forward can only bring the escalation to a manager sooner, never a deletion (HZ-040; security review round 3). At the server, a hold report's times are never later than the server received it, so a fast device clock can bring the manager's task forward but never push it later (security review round 5, S-M1; clinical review round 5, L-3), and a hold's start is never earlier than the device's last report for that hold said, nor than the server first heard from the device, so a backdated start cannot make a new task due at once (security reviews round 6, N2 and round 7, M1). Tests: `held-limit.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 does not act on a clock earlier than the hold's start or the last server time`, `hazard HZ-010 treats a clock that jumped forward as a reason to escalate, nothing more`; `api.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 takes a hold's start no later than the server received the report (round 5, S-M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 raises one task for the Registered Manager at the reporter's location, due when the hold escalates, never moved later, and closed at zero (round 5, C-H1 and S-M1)` (an earlier start than the last report's is taken as that report's) | Built in Phase 1 (mobile and API) |
| Design | Every event records device time with its offset and the server-received time | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Drift between device and server is measured at each sync and held in local sync bookkeeping (`sync_meta` in `packages/sync`) | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Drift over 5 minutes is flagged on the device and on every record written while drifted (master prompt 10.3) | Phase 1 |
| Design | A record whose device time is later than its server-received time, or far earlier, raises a manager review flag; a note written more than 2 hours after the event carries the late-entry flag | Phase 1 |
| Design | The history view shows both device and server times | Phase 1 |
| Design | The web sends the device's clock reading with every entry (`x-harbour-sent-at`), so the API flags an entry from a browser whose clock is wrong; the entry shows "Device clock differed" in words and an icon | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web) |
| Design | Interval checks on the device use a drift-corrected clock when drift is known | Phase 2 |
| Design | A held device's start is never earlier than the server first saw the device: the earliest first seen in `device_binding`, written at each sync token and report and filled from the audit trail for devices seen before ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 1) | Built in Phase 1 (API, Device integrity) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1, Phase 2 |
| Training and user documentation | Care worker guide: never change the device time. Deployer control: MDM policy enforces automatic network time on shared devices ([kiosk.md](../compliance/kiosk.md)) | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/shared/time.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 clock drift` (5-minute tolerance) | Passing (Phase 0) |
| `apps/web/src/components/care/EntryBadges.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 says when the device clock differed from the server's`; `apps/web/src/lib/uk-time.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 hazard HZ-017 refuses a time later than now, beyond the clock tolerance` | Passing (Phase 1 slice 9) |
| `packages/db`: append-only event columns keep device time, device offset and server-received time | Built in Phase 0 |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-010 every uploaded event is stamped with server-received time` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `packages/sync`: `hazard HZ-010 drift over 5 minutes between device and server is flagged` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-010 a device time later than server-received time creates a review flag` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-010 a note written more than 2 hours after the event carries the late-entry flag` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `packages/domain` (medicines): `hazard HZ-010 the minimum-interval check uses the drift-corrected clock` | Planned, Phase 2 |
| `apps/mobile/src/sync/connector.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 hazard HZ-017 sends the time it was written and the time it was sent apart`, `hazard HZ-010 stamps each retry with the time it is sent` (`deviceTime` is the time written; `x-harbour-sent-at` is stamped as each request leaves) | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/mobile/src/components/when-field.render.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-009 hazard HZ-010 hazard HZ-017 WhenField on the autumn clock change`: `hazard HZ-009 hazard HZ-017 names the time, normalised, in the question and in both options`, `has no default, and records the zone chosen`, `asks nothing for a time that happened once`; `apps/api/test/api.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 takes a hold's start no later than the server received the report (round 5, S-M1)` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/time.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 when the event happened`: `hazard HZ-010 refuses a time later than now` (with the default day), `hazard HZ-010 never rolls a later time back to yesterday`, `hazard HZ-010 with a tolerance of %i minutes from the rate table reads %s as %s`; `notes.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 refuses a time still to come today rather than dating it yesterday`, `hazard HZ-010 takes the tenant's clock tolerance from the entry context`; `sync/clock-tolerance.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 the clock tolerance for typed times comes from the rate table` (cached for offline starts, none until known, a figure in the wrong unit or out of range ignored); `sync/held-limit.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 does not act on a clock earlier than the hold's start or the last server time`, `hazard HZ-010 treats a clock that jumped forward as a reason to escalate, nothing more` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/api/test/device-integrity.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-010 never takes a hold's start earlier than the device's earliest binding` | Passing (Phase 1, Device integrity) |

---

## HZ-011 Cross-tenant or out-of-scope record exposure

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-011 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]`; data protection lead consulted |
| Engineering owner | `packages/db`, `packages/sync`, `apps/api` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 4 (access model), 5.3 (tenant isolation); [ADR-0002](../decisions/0002-tenancy-and-row-level-security.md); [Postgres row security](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-rowsecurity.html); see also HZ-073 (the same scope read the other way: safety work hidden from the person who must act) |

**Description**: a user sees, or a device receives, records belonging to another tenant, or records in their own tenant outside their scope (location, team, assigned people, time window), or an external professional or agency worker keeps access after it should have expired.

**Causes**: a handover item about nobody (an incident with no residents, the safety tasks and alerts it raises), or a note on one, shown to every reader of the handover, an agency worker included (slice 5 review round 3); a missing `tenant_id` predicate; RLS disabled or bypassed (owner role, `BYPASSRLS`, `SECURITY DEFINER` functions); `app.tenant_id` leaking between pooled transactions; a PowerSync stream filter error (logical replication bypasses RLS); cache or search keys without the tenant; a search index, query or result count that crosses the tenant or the user's scope; a break-glass search used to browse, or to find where a person at risk lives; an agency worker with no shift recorded given open-ended access; guessable or long-lived file URLs; export jobs (see HZ-070); operator impersonation; scope grants that do not expire; stale token claims after a role change; a permission used with the scope of another of the user's roles (before ADR-0071 the widest scope of any role applied to every permission, so a care worker at one home who also viewed finance across the organisation read and wrote care notes at every home, and a grant applied where the user's roles reached rather than where it was given).

**Effects**: disclosure of special category data and resulting distress; safeguarding risk (for example disclosure of the whereabouts of a person at risk); a wrong-person decision if an out-of-scope record is mistaken for the right one (HZ-001); a reportable personal data breach.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | `tenant_id` on every row; RLS enabled with `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` on every tenant table; the application connects as a non-owner role without `BYPASSRLS` and sets `app.tenant_id` per transaction | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Cross-tenant isolation test suite proving every table denies cross-tenant access | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Sync streams filtered by the token's tenant, location and assignment claims, tested on their own | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Every view of a care record, export and permission change written to the append-only, hash-chained `audit_event` table | Built in Phase 0 for person views, lists, history, break-glass, refusals and sync tokens; extended per module |
| Design | Attribute-based scoping (location, assigned people, time window) enforced in the API and in RLS | Built in Phase 0 (team scope with rotas, Phase 2) |
| Design | Data scope is per permission ([ADR-0071](../decisions/0071-data-scope-is-per-permission.md)): each permission reaches only the people the roles and grants carrying it reach (a grant at its location, or the whole organisation only when marked so; a location role with no location only when marked so, otherwise refused); a request runs under the scope of the permission it requires, the narrowest of several, and sees no one when it requires none, except the listed routes that every member uses (the task list and task moves, document lookups), which run in `people.person.read`'s scope, and a static test checks every other route asks for a scope before the database. The people a user is assigned to count only for a permission a role scoped to assigned people carries, and break-glass people only for the read permissions break-glass opens (`people.person.read`, `care.plan.read`, `care.note.read`; never a write, a discharge or death, money, ReSPECT or an admission, nor signing a document, which needs its category's update permission; H-168), in the API and in row-level security (`app.scope_assigned`, `app.scope_break_glass`); no tab of such a record on the web offers a form, and each says it is for reading only, as the API lists what reaches the person without break-glass (review rounds 2 and 3). A grant with no location that was live before the change stops the migration rather than being widened to the whole organisation (review round 2, S2-M2). Each family of sync streams follows its own read permission, assigned people included only when that permission reaches them; a document about a person is read with its category's permission; the audit trail is whole only across the organisation; a second permission on one record (completing a task, a status change, a workflow move, a return check, a task an entry completes, managing an incident) counts only where it reaches that record; alerts and handover views use the scope of the permission the recipient would open the record with (review round 1) | Built in Phase 1 (fix/platform-scope-per-permission, review round 1) |
| Design | Break-glass access with typed justification, alerting the Registered Manager and quality lead, audited with the reason, lapsing after an hour; a weekly report by user, person and reason flags repeated use for the Registered Manager and data protection lead | Built in Phase 0 (weekly report job, storage and API built in Phase 1 slice 8; screen with the web work) |
| Design | Short-lived presigned file URLs with tenant-prefixed keys; remote wipe and device revocation | Phase 1 (device revocation built with Device integrity part 2) |
| Design | Device integrity tables ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6): `device_binding`, `device_session`, `device_discard_entry`, `handed_over_entry`, `refused_entry` and `request_quota` carry `tenant_id` with forced row-level security and the tenant policy. Bindings, sessions, hand-overs, deletion lists and copies are written only in the system's view, and a quota row only in a view acting as its member. A member reads their own bindings, sessions, hand-overs and deletions; tenant-wide roles read the organisation's; a location role reads only those of staff with a role assignment at one of its locations, as the API narrows them, and never another member's quota (#25 review round 2, low). The API lists a location role's devices after narrowing to its staff, and a location role revokes only a device every member bound to it is its staff (any device it reaches nobody on reads as not found). A refused entry's copy is read like the care record it would have been, the person in the reader's scope, and is taken only about someone its author cared for. None of these tables syncs to devices | Built in Phase 1 (Device integrity) |
| Design | Time-limited grants expire automatically (agency workers at shift end plus `access.agency.post_shift_hours`, and an agency worker with no shift recorded gets no access at all; external professionals at their expiry) | Phase 1 (professionals; the agency window rule is built in the domain and search refuses after it), Phase 2 (agency, with rota) |
| Design | Search runs under the same forced RLS as the records (ADR-0070); the plan allows only the kinds of record the user can read, in their scope, while their access lasts, and only current people unless `platform.search.archived` is held and used; results are filtered again and counted only over what may be seen, so an out-of-scope match looks like no match, and a disagreement raises a security alarm ([platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rules 1 to 7) | Phase 1 (domain rules built; index and routes with the schema and API step) |
| Design | Security-definer functions run with their owner's rights, so their execute rights are their access control: PUBLIC executes none, and each only the roles it is meant for (ADR-0002). Migration 0061 left `app.family_can_see` and `app.family_item_shown` executable by every login role (PUBLIC's default right, revoked after the owner change and so not at all), so any role could ask whether a sharing decision would show a family an update, yes or no; migration 0068 revokes it as the owner. Every definer function's callers are listed in a test, and a new one fails until it is listed; migrations apply in journal order, checked so none is skipped | Built in Phase 1 (#97) |
| Design | Break-glass search: step-up (a sign-in with the second step within the window), MFA, a reason code and a typed reason, a specific query (an NHS number, or at least two name words) matched on whole words of names only (no prefixes, no room labels), identity fields only with location and room withheld until the record is opened, at most 10 people. The search itself alerts the Registered Manager of each location whose people were shown and the data protection lead; `break_glass.search_max_per_day` searches per user in 24 hours, after which further searches are refused and the data protection lead is told (rule 9). Searches made at once are serialised per user, so none passes the limit; an alert that cannot be delivered (no Registered Manager at a home shown, no active data protection lead) is audited; the count shown never exceeds the 10 people listed, and more matches ask for a narrower search (HZ-001); the weekly break-glass report lists searches by user and reason | Phase 1 (built) |
| Design | Search audit events record the kinds searched and left out, the counts, the person scope, location ids, when the user's access ends and the opaque ids shown; people shown count as views of their care records. Never the words typed, which are usually a name; a break-glass search refers to its break-glass record, which holds the typed reason, instead of copying it (rule 8) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Operator support access only after the tenant owner approves a ticketed request with step-up; requests and decisions written to the tenant's audit chain | Request and approval built in Phase 0; read-only impersonation session, Phase 1 |
| Design | Shift handovers (slice 5): each item is its own append-only row (`handover_item`) that row-level security shows only to those who can see its person, and an item about nobody only to those who see the handover whole (`app.handover_whole`: the reader's scope for `care.handover.manage`, the permission a handover is read with, is the organisation's or includes its location; another role's reach never counts, ADR-0071), as `app.incident_in_scope` and `app.task_visible` restrict such records; a note on an item follows that item's visibility (`app.handover_item_visible`), a note on the whole handover reaches every reader of it, and a note on an item takes its person from the item and needs an item the writer can see; the API's view of what each member of staff can see (`staffCanSee`, under each member's own scope for `care.handover.manage`, assigned people only when a role scoped to them carries it) applies the same rule; a reader limited to their own people (anyone whose scope for `care.handover.manage` is neither the organisation's nor includes the location) sees counts over their items only and only their own acknowledgements, and learns nothing about items they cannot see (no hidden count, no version); only the system's view writes a handover or its items | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (review round 2, S-M1 and S-M2) |
| Design | The round and the handover on the phone (slice 10 part 2): the `*_open_tasks` streams carry the open tasks the round view shows (`care.plan.read` under `app.task_visible`: about the people in that scope, about nobody at the locations it reaches whole, or about nobody and given to the reader); the `*_handovers` streams carry the handovers, items, notes and acknowledgements the handover views show, with their own claims for `care.handover.manage` (`handover_location_ids`, `handover_assigned`). A reader limited to their people receives only the handovers they may open (where one of their people lives) and in them only the items and notes about their people and the handover's own notes, never an item about nobody or a note on one; every reader receives only their own acknowledgements with item keys, and a whole reader who acknowledged as who and when only (`handover_staff_acknowledgement`), since keys name people and what happened to them (#33 review round 1, M1 and L1). No refresh time or version (the API shows those to whole readers only). Every query is pinned to the token's tenant and window and reads no connection parameters | Built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2; the tables are published by migration 0052, which `packages/db/test/sync-publication.test.ts` checks for every table a stream reads |
| Design | Invitations (PLAT-004, STATUS item 20): sending, listing and withdrawing an invitation need `platform.user.manage` and reach only the members it reaches (`memberReached`, as the admin sign-out does: a location role only members whose roles and grants are all at its homes, never one with an organisation-wide role), although `invitation_scope` lets a location role read every invitation in the database (#48 review); anyone else reads as not found, audited. Sending also needs step-up and every permission of the member's roles where they apply (`app.may_invite`). Opening and accepting a link need no session, are throttled per client address and per token, and go through the security-definer functions with the token as a bound parameter only; an existing sign-in is never given a password through a link | Built in Phase 1 (invitation routes) |
| Test | See Verification; annual external penetration test `[Thapsus - human action]` | Phase 0 onwards |
| Training and user documentation | Tenant role-design guidance (least privilege); deployer control: periodic access review by the tenant | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Release note: some staff whose roles give different permissions at different homes, and administrators in the task list, see fewer people; deployer control: Registered Managers check role bundles so each role carries the permissions its holders need where they work | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

| `apps/api/test/invitations.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-011 sending, listing and revoking within reach (#48 review)` (a location role is refused, as not found, for a member at another home or with an organisation-wide role, for send, list and withdraw; a member whose roles go beyond the sender's is refused), `hazard HZ-011 opening and accepting a link with no session`, `hazard HZ-011 the first owner's invitation by an operator`, `hazard HZ-011 the nightly sweep` | Passing (Phase 1, invitation routes) |

## HZ-012 Validated assessment tool calculated incorrectly

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-012 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module and the forms engine in `platform` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 5.2, 10.1; [RCP NEWS2](https://www.rcp.ac.uk/improving-care/resources/national-early-warning-score-news-2/); [BAPEN MUST](https://www.bapen.org.uk/must-and-self-screening/must-calculator); Waterlow scoring card `[authoritative source to be confirmed by CSO - human action]`; [medical-device boundary](../compliance/medical-device-boundary.md) |

**Description**: a NEWS2, MUST or Waterlow score (and later Braden, Abbey, PAINAD and the other tools in master prompt 10.1) is calculated, banded or displayed wrongly, or the wrong version or scale is used (for example NEWS2 SpO2 Scale 2 for a person who has no clinical decision for it).

**Causes**: transcription errors in scoring tables; boundary errors (less than versus less than or equal); unit conversions (weight, height, temperature); rounding of BMI; a missing value treated as zero; a form that pre-selects an answer (for example "Air" and "Alert" on the NEWS2 form), so an unanswered question is scored as normal; a tenant editing a locked template; new confusion not scored as the published value; supplemental oxygen not scored; MUST weight-loss percentage over the wrong period; Waterlow multi-select categories summed wrongly; mixing observations taken at different times.

**Effects**: under-scoring leads to missed deterioration (for example sepsis), missed malnutrition referral or pressure-ulcer prevention not started, causing severe harm or death; over-scoring leads to unnecessary escalation.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Validated tools are locked templates implemented exactly as published, with the publisher, edition and source cited in code and on screen; tenants cannot edit scoring | Phase 1 |
| Design | The screen shows every parameter's sub-score, the total and the published banding table, so the user can check the arithmetic | Phase 1 |
| Design | Missing inputs produce no total ("Score incomplete: 2 values missing"), never a partial total | Phase 1 |
| Design | Nothing is pre-selected on the web forms for validated tools; an unanswered question is refused at its own field (NEWS2 air or oxygen, and consciousness). Without the care profile the SpO2 scale is unknown, so vital signs are not offered | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web) |
| Design | NEWS2 SpO2 Scale 2 is available only when a registered clinician has recorded that decision and target range on the person's record | Phase 1 |
| Design | The result links to the tenant's escalation policy; Harbour adds no recommendation of its own. Every stored NEWS2, MUST, Waterlow, Braden and falls result is returned with `escalationPolicy`: the organisation's own policy for its tool and band (title, reference and document, configured per tenant and optionally per location in `escalation_policy_link`), or `not_configured`, never nothing ([care.md](../specs/care.md) rule 5; [medical-device boundary](../compliance/medical-device-boundary.md) rule 4). The web shows it on every assessment result and NEWS2 entry, and on the NEWS2 result shown straight after saving (#26 review round 3): the title and reference, linking the policy document, or "No escalation policy is configured for this result. Follow your organisation's procedures and tell the nurse in charge." with an icon, never nothing (#26 review round 2, C6). The not-configured line shows on every band, NEWS2 0 included, where the RCP's own response is routine monitoring; whether to show it only for bands that need a response is for the CSO (H-73; medical-device boundary rule 4; HZ-008). The lookup order is for the CSO (H-163) | Built in Phase 1 (API, care API follow-ups; web, #26 review rounds 2 and 3); the phone planned (H-73) |
| Design | Each stored score records the tool edition and the code version that calculated it | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b: the server runs the tool by key and stores the version, answers, score, band and workings; the client never sends a score |
| Design | Waterlow and Braden stay off for real tenants until their licence terms are confirmed (H-25): `care.tool.waterlow` and `care.tool.braden` flags, off by default, on in the demo tenant | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | Fixed units (kg, cm, degrees Celsius) with visible conversion for stones and pounds | Phase 1 |
| Design | The CSO checks each tool implementation against its published source before release, including a spot-check of the NEWS2 reference vectors (the points for every parameter, worked by hand from RCP chart 1) `[CSO - human action]` (human action H-129) | Phase 1 |
| Design | On the phone NEWS2 shows the formula, the published bands, every parameter's points and the RCP chart 4 clinical response, with the source and a link to it; with no SpO2 scale on the device no total is shown and the server scores the readings on upload ([medical-device boundary](../compliance/medical-device-boundary.md) section 5 rules 1 to 3) | Built in Phase 1 (mobile, clinical review C6) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | User guide per tool with the published source; deployer control: tenant escalation policy linked in configuration | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 scores every parameter band boundary as published by the RCP` (table-driven, every boundary value) | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 adds 2 for supplemental oxygen and scores new confusion as published` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 applies SpO2 Scale 2 only when a clinician decision is recorded` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 returns incomplete when any parameter is missing` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 refuses impossible or malformed values rather than scoring them` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 clinical risk follows RCP chart 2 at every threshold` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 MUST BMI, weight-loss and acute disease scores match the BAPEN worked examples` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 MUST bands the exact BMI and rounds it only for display` (18.45, 18.49, 18.5, 19.99, 20.0, 20.01) | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 MUST gives no total for missing or impossible values` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 Waterlow totals and risk bands match the published scoring card` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 Waterlow gives no total for missing or contradictory answers` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 Braden totals and risk bands match the published form` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-012 Braden gives no total for missing or impossible subscale scores` | Passing |
| `packages/domain/src/care/validated-assessment.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 the tool runs on the server by its key` (MUST, Waterlow, Braden and the NG249 pathway stored with tool version, score, band and workings; the falls pathway with no score and no band; no result for incomplete answers) | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/care-planning.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 refuses Braden where its licence flag is off (H-25), and allows it in the demo tenant` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (platform): `hazard HZ-012 tenants cannot edit locked validated templates` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `apps/web/e2e/care-record.spec.ts` (Playwright with axe, WCAG 2.2 AA): `hazard HZ-012 a nurse runs MUST and sees the API's score, band, workings, follow-ups and source` (the web sends answers only; the test checks each step's points in the workings, the total, the band in the publisher's words and the source link); `hazard HZ-014 vital signs show the NEWS2 result and the RCP response the API returns` (saving with air or oxygen and consciousness unanswered is refused at those fields) | Passing (local run; CI e2e-web on the PR) |
| `packages/domain/src/care/news2.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 matches the reference vectors worked by hand from RCP chart 1` (`NEWS2_REFERENCE_VECTORS`: the points for every parameter as well as the total, so two errors cannot cancel out; every band and chart 4 threshold, both SpO2 scales, a single 3, and the boundaries respiration rate 25, systolic 219 and 220, temperature 35.1, pulse 41 and 50, scale 2 on air, and scale 2 on oxygen at 92 to 97; clinical review round 3). The CSO spot-checks the vectors (H-129) | Passing |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 NEWS2 on the device against the reference vectors (C6)` (the same vectors from typed readings); `NEWS2 on the device (HZ-012, HZ-014)`: no total without the person's SpO2 scale on the device, the readings kept for the server; the RCP chart 4 clinical response kept with the score; nothing scored until every reading; `components/news2-panel.render.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 the NEWS2 panel shows its workings and the published response (C6)` (rendered: the score, the RCP wording, every parameter's points, the formula, bands, policy and source) | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/assessments.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 names the publisher, edition and source the API calculates from`; `hazard HZ-012 words every MUST and Waterlow band in the catalogue, never by its code` (against the domain's band types); `apps/web/src/lib/assessment-answers.test.ts`: `answer options match the API's contract`; `apps/web/src/lib/chart-entry.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 hazard HZ-014 refuses vital signs until air or oxygen and consciousness are chosen` | Passing (Phase 1 slice 9) |
| `apps/web` Playwright: `hazard HZ-012 the score screen shows the full published banding table beside the band` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `packages/domain/src/care/escalation-policy.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 the link is the organisation's own policy, never a Harbour recommendation` (only what the organisation configured, the band as stored; every band each tool's results carry, none for the falls pathway); `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 shows only the organisation's own policy, and says none again once removed` (another organisation's link never shows) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/web/src/components/care/ObservationList.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 says no escalation policy is configured, with an icon, when it is %s` (not configured, and not returned); `apps/web/e2e/care-record.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 a nurse runs MUST and sees the API's score, band, workings, follow-ups and source` (no policy linked for MUST in the demo: the result says so, with axe) | Passing (#26 review round 2) |

## HZ-013 Statutory notification missed or late

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-013 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `safety` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 4 (`safety.notification.submit`), 11.1; [CQC Registration Regulations 2009 Reg 18](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/uksi/2009/3112/regulation/18); [RIDDOR](https://www.hse.gov.uk/riddor/when-do-i-report.htm); [Care Act 2014 s.42](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2014/23/section/42) |

**Description**: a notifiable event (CQC death or other incident notification, RIDDOR report, safeguarding referral, UKHSA outbreak report, duty of candour notification) is not identified, not drafted, not submitted, or submitted late.

**Causes**: the incident is not recorded; a classifier question is answered wrongly or the rule is wrong or out of date (for example after the June 2026 Supreme Court judgment on deprivation of liberty); no holder of `safety.notification.submit` is available and no acting-up grant is in place; the deadline clock starts late or from the wrong time; a notification job fails silently; alert fatigue (HZ-008); an incident recorded offline is not synced (HZ-006); a discharge or death cancels the person's safety, statutory and exit tasks along with their rounds (slice 8 review, clinical finding H2); the drafts of a death recorded in error and corrected stand in for a later real death, or a notification already submitted for it is never corrected with the regulator (H5); the LeDeR question is taken as "no" when nobody answered it (M3); an incident's draft for a death recorded in error is counted for a later real death, or stays submitted with nobody asked to correct it (round 3, clinical finding L9).

**Effects**: a person remains at risk of abuse; the regulator cannot act; an outbreak spreads; the family is not told what happened; regulatory enforcement against the tenant.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Notification centre with escalation chains | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Incident classifier with structured questions produces each required output (master prompt 11.1); rules are table-driven, versioned and dated with their source regulation and nation, not hard-coded | Phase 1 |
| Design | Deadline clocks start from the recorded event or awareness time; deadlines come from the rate table ([rates.md](../specs/rates.md)), not literals in code; clocks show on the incident, the Registered Manager dashboard and the notification centre, and escalate before breach to the Registered Manager, then the Nominated Individual | Phase 1 |
| Design | Submission requires `safety.notification.submit` with step-up authentication; when no holder is available the system prompts an acting-up grant | Phase 1 |
| Design | An incident recorded offline is classified on sync with its clock started at the recorded event time and a flag if the clock is already running late | Phase 1 |
| Design | A notification decision cannot close without submission evidence or a recorded "not notifiable" reason | Phase 1 |
| Design | Weekly digest of open notification decisions to the Registered Manager and Nominated Individual | Phase 1 |
| Design | An "unsure" answer drafts a provisional notification with its clock; an unanswered question raises a task due at the event time; outside England the classifier says notifications are not checked rather than giving a partial answer (`packages/domain/src/safety/classifier.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | An incident cannot close while a notification is a draft or ready, a classifier question is open or the candour record is open (`incidentCloseProblems`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The classifier runs on the server whenever an incident is created, synced, changed or answered, with tenant-wide scope so it always reads every person on the incident (ADR-0033); it stores its version and reasoning, writes one draft per incident and kind (per person for deaths, LeDeR, the medical examiner pack and missing people) with clocks from `occurred_at`, and a draft that a later answer no longer supports is flagged, never closed, until a holder of `safety.notification.submit` marks it not required with a reason and step-up (`apps/api/src/services/safety.ts`) | Phase 1 (built) |
| Design | An overdue draft alerts the active holders of `safety.notification.submit` at its location once, through the tenant's `safety.notification.overdue` escalation chain (by default the Nominated Individual after `statutory_notification.escalation_minutes`, H-69), until it is submitted or marked not required; with no active holder it stays unalerted, is tried again on every run and is audited at most once a day (`safety.notification.unrouted`, `no_active_submitter`), counted in the operator's daily signal (ADR-0071 review round 5, C5-M3; HZ-073); closing an incident counts drafts about people the closer cannot see | Phase 1 (built) |
| Design | An exit ends only rounds (series tasks) and point-of-care tasks (`care.*`, `medicines.*`), coded `ended_at_exit`; incident reports, safeguarding decisions, DoLS expiry, every `safety.*` and `people.*` task and the exit checklist task stay open | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Each death notification draft belongs to its exit record (source key `exit:<exit id>:<kind>`) and the exit checklist follows only that exit's drafts, so a death recorded in error never stands for a later one. A correction flags that exit's drafts no longer indicated, never closing them, and gives the holders of `safety.notification.submit` one urgent task to tell the regulator that a submitted notification was made in error. An incident's draft that counted for that death is withdrawn the same way, and neither counts for a later death. What to do with submitted notifications is for the CSO (H-88) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (incident drafts: review round 3) |
| Design | Recording a death needs an explicit answer on learning disability or autism (LeDeR); the exit record has no default. Whether each exit notification is statutory comes from the safety module's `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`, so the checklist and the notification agree. The clocks and the statutory labels for LeDeR and the medical examiner are for the CSO and DPO (H-87) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | The web incidents list shows each incident's status as an icon and a word (one the web does not know warns) and how many questions about statutory notifications are still to answer; when the API's limit of 500 is reached it fetches the open and under-review incidents besides and says older incidents are not listed. The incident page shows the same count as a warning chip. A reader who cannot see everyone on an incident is told part of it is hidden, and sees no questions or notifications (`apps/web/src/lib/incidents.ts`). | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 1 built) |
| Design | The web incident page shows each statutory notification on the incident, before the people on it, with an overdue chip at the top that links to them: its clock as an icon and a word (overdue for how long, due, submitted, or not required in the not-applicable style; an unknown clock or ending warns), its status (draft, ready to submit) as its own chip, its deadlines in the law's terms from the classifier's outputs ("Without delay: the clock started ...", RIDDOR's second deadline; "Due time not known" warns), who it is about for one person (named, or a numbered person not shown), its recipient, and on a draft still open its warnings with a next step (provisional: confirm or mark not required, if in doubt notify; waiting for answers, without inviting a not-required decision; no longer indicated; a Harbour target, made as soon as possible all the same for a safeguarding concern or a missing person). Questions still to answer (worded for who can answer them, `safety.incident.manage`), kinds waiting for them, checks that decide whether one is needed (RIDDOR's absence check, the safeguarding decision guide, an outbreak's links, the candour decision) with when (left out once the incident is closed, and "Check whether this was done: ..." once their date has passed, since the stored tasks never clear), outputs drafted on another record, "not checked yet" and "not checked outside England" all warn; "no notification found" is said only when none of these is there. Kinds, recipients and statuses come from the catalogue, checked against the domain's `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`; RIDDOR's threshold comes from the rate table (`apps/web/src/lib/incidents.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/safety/IncidentNotifications.tsx`; #49 review round 1; wording for the CSO, H-257 to H-259) | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 2 built) |
| Design | The web statutory notification pages (`/notifications/statutory` and `/notifications/statutory/{id}`, the page the overdue alert links to), for holders of `safety.incident.manage` as the API allows (anyone else is told in words that the page is not for them): the list puts what is still to do first (an unknown clock, then overdue, then due soonest) above those submitted or not required, each with its clock and status as icons and words, who it is about (people the reader cannot see numbered consistently across the list, and the list says so), its due time, location, recipient and warnings, and reads the unfinished (draft, ready) and finished (submitted, not required) notifications separately, so old finished ones never push an unfinished one off the list; when an unfinished read reaches the API's limit of 1,000 it says unfinished notifications may be missing and never says there is nothing to do (#52 review). Who a notification is about shows their communication needs (AIS). A holder of `safety.notification.submit` without `safety.incident.manage`, whom the overdue alert sends here, is told who can act (H-260). The notification's page shows its clock, status and warnings, every deadline in the law's terms (kept once done), the record it was drafted on named and linked with that record's status (#49 review round 2), why Harbour drafted it, what it must include and its sources (the API's English, marked as such; a source is linked only on https) and how it ended. Both pages, and the incident page, say when the clocks were worked out and to reload for an update (#49 review round 1); on the incident page each notification links to its own page for a holder of `safety.incident.manage` (`apps/web/src/lib/statutory-notifications.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/(shell)/notifications/statutory/`). Read only; the forms follow | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 3 built) |
| Design | The web records an incident (`/incidents/new`, from the incidents list or a person's record where `access.reaching` lists `safety.incident.report`, ADR-0071) and offers an incident's open questions on its page: every one to a holder of `safety.incident.manage`, the falls questions to the person who raised it; a blank question stays open, "not sure yet" is always an answer, and saving classifies the incident again (HZ-090) | Phase 1 (slice 9) |
| Design | The web closes the loop (slice 9, part 4b; HZ-091): the incident page offers a holder of `safety.incident.manage` "start the review", "send it back to open" with a reason and "close" with a reason, never "close" to the person who raised it, and while a notification is a draft or ready or a question is open it lists what to do first (each notification linked) instead of a form the API would refuse. The notification page prepares it (one answer per line of required content, ready only when every line is answered), records it as submitted only once ready (the time chosen every time, the regulator's reference) and decides "not required" with a reason, each only where its permission reaches the person it is about (`access.reaching`, ADR-0071), with step-up for the last two; a refusal is said in words a manager can act on (`apps/web/src/lib/incident-review.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/safety/`) | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | The approvals list (`GET /v1/workflows`, `/approvals`, PLAT-011) shows each statutory notification's own due time and the candour clocks. The safety module writes them onto the workflow instance whenever it starts or changes them: `upsertDraft`, the candour record and each candour step, through `setWorkflowDeadline` and `candourStateDeadline`. The engine keeps that deadline across moves (`deadlineOnRecord`). Only the states that carry such a deadline count: a notification's draft and ready, and candour's threshold, verbal due and written due. An instance in one of them without a deadline yet reads "Deadline kept on the record: open it to check" as a warning and is listed first, never "No deadline". Any other state, such as candour enquiries open, reads "No deadline". `workflow-deadlines:backfill` fills older instances. Since #130 review round 2, a statutory notification is submitted, or marked not required, only by someone holding `safety.notification.submit` at its home, not by the raiser of its incident at another home | Phase 1 (built 31/10/2026; #130 review) |
| Design | Safeguarding log and outbreak register feed the same clocks | Phase 2 |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1, Phase 2 |
| Training and user documentation | User guide states that the classifier supports, and never replaces, the Registered Manager's judgement. Deployer control: tenant notifications policy and acting-up arrangements for absences | Phase 1 |
| Design | On the web home page (INSI-001, `feat/web-dashboards`, docs/specs/insights.md): the statutory notifications tile counts drafts and ready ones still to submit, says the overdue ones apart (a clock the web does not know counts as overdue) and gives the soonest due time; if either read fails the whole tile says "Could not load", never a lower count | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Justification for level 3**: Harbour cannot ensure an incident is recorded, and submission is a human decision by a named role. Harbour makes the obligation and its deadline visible and escalates; further reduction is impractical without automating a regulated judgement. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

## HZ-014 Deterioration score or screening trigger not acted on

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-014 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (NEWS2, MUST); [RCP NEWS2](https://www.rcp.ac.uk/improving-care/resources/national-early-warning-score-news-2/); [BAPEN MUST](https://www.bapen.org.uk/must-and-self-screening/must-toolkit/) |

**Description**: a NEWS2 score or MUST result that calls for a response (for example NEWS2 of 5 or more, or a single parameter scoring 3; MUST medium or high) is calculated correctly but the nurse in charge is not told, or the follow-up (repeat observations, food record, dietitian referral) is not scheduled.

**Causes**: the result is shown only on the screen where it was entered; no task or alert is raised; the alert goes to someone off shift; the follow-up interval is wrong; offline entry delays the alert (HZ-006); alert fatigue (HZ-008).

**Effects**: deterioration goes unnoticed until the person is seriously unwell; malnutrition progresses; avoidable hospital admission or death.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Every validated result stores the published response band; bands that call for action raise a task and an urgent notification to the nurse in charge on shift, escalating through the location's chain until acknowledged | Phase 1 |
| Design | Follow-ups are generated from the band: repeat observation frequency for NEWS2, a food record for MUST medium risk, a dietitian referral task for MUST high risk; intervals come from the rate table where they are defaults | Phase 1 |
| Design | Stored MUST results schedule the next screening (`must.care_home.screening_interval_months`) and weighing (weekly at medium or high risk, otherwise monthly, from the rate table) as tasks, one open task of each kind at a time; a recorded weight answers the weight check; a correction raises only the follow-ups the original did not | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | Results entered offline raise the task on the device immediately and the alert on sync, with the original time | Phase 1 |
| Design | Handover always includes NEWS2 above the low band (an aggregate of 5 or more, or a single parameter scoring 3) and MUST follow-ups raised in the shift (HZ-018) | Phase 1 |
| Design | Go-live waits until a working deterioration escalation chain exists and every validated tool switched on links the tenant's escalation policy (onboarding items `escalation_chains_set` and `escalation_policies_linked`, [platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rule 26) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Every validated result says which of the organisation's escalation policies applies to its band: the location's own policy for the band first, then the location's for the tool, then the organisation's for the band, then for the tool (`escalationPolicyFor`), resolved when the result is read; with none, "No escalation policy is configured for this result", so a missing policy is visible rather than silent. Only holders of `quality.policy.manage` change links, a location role only for its locations (API and restrictive row-level policies); each change is audited and versioned; who may change a link is read from the roles and grants that carry the permission, never a scope another role widens (#26 review round 2, S1). The rule for the onboarding item `escalation_policies_linked` reads the links: a tool is linked only when every band, at every location, finds a policy (`validatedToolsWithLinks`). `GET /v1/onboarding` calls the rule (slice 13, section 10e of platform-completions.md), so the go-live check reads the links; a tool switched on at any one location counts as in use even when the organisation-wide setting is off (#26 review round 3; [care.md](../specs/care.md) rule 5; H-163). The NEWS2 result shown and focused straight after saving vital signs names the policy too, as the saved entry does (#26 review round 3) | Built in Phase 1 (API, care API follow-ups; web, #26 review rounds 2 and 3); the onboarding rule and the checklist route that calls it built (slice 13); the phone planned (H-73) |
| Design | An urgent result alert goes only to someone who can open the person's record (the database's own person scope, read for each member as at sign-in), on the staff and with a role in force: the nurse in charge at the location; with no one there who can, the Registered Managers across the organisation, then other Registered Managers whose scope covers the person, then the holders of `care.plan.write` whose `people.person.read` reaches the person and whose `care.plan.write` reaches the person (each from the roles and grants carrying it, ADR-0071). A manager at another home who cannot open the record is never chosen; see also HZ-073. The alert names the home (location name only) so someone told about a home not their own knows where to go. The route is worked out before the entry is written and kept on it (`flags.alertRoute`: `nurse_in_charge`, `fallback`, `no_one`), and the web says from it who was told, never "the nurse in charge was told" when they were not. With no one who can see the person, nothing is counted as raised and `care.alert.unrouted` is audited with a coded reason (`news2_threshold`, `iddsi_level_not_recorded` and so on), never the result in words. The sync tasks keep their own fallback, where assigning the task is what lets its taker see it ([care.md](../specs/care.md) rule 6; #26 review rounds 2 and 3, C2 and item 1). The order and the skips are verified by `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 hazard HZ-016 an urgent alert's fallback order, and the staff it skips (#26 review round 4)` | Built in Phase 1 (#26 review rounds 2 to 4) |
| Design | A drink offered and refused adds nothing to the day's intake or the shortfall, so a refusal never hides a shortfall; the fluid day reports how many drinks were refused and the amount offered ("2 drinks offered and refused (450 ml), not counted in the intake."), and a refused drink's amount reads as offered ("200 ml offered") on the web and the phone. Whether the offered amount is shown beside the total is for the CSO (H-167). Verified by `packages/domain/src/care/fluid-balance.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 a drink offered and refused is never counted as drunk (#26 review round 3)`; `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 fluid balance: a drink offered and refused is never counted as drunk (#26 review round 3)`; `apps/web/src/lib/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 says beside the totals what was offered and refused, never as drunk (#26 review round 3)` | Built in Phase 1 (#26 review round 3) |
| Training and user documentation | User guide: the score supports clinical judgement and the published response, never replaces it; deployer control: escalation policy and on-shift nurse in charge set per location | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Justification for level 3**: Harbour raises, routes and escalates the response, but acting on it is a clinical decision by the staff on shift; further reduction would mean automating a clinical judgement, which the medical-device boundary rules out. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

| `apps/web/src/components/care/ObservationList.test.ts`: `the NEWS2 result shown after saving (#26 review round 3, item 3)` (`hazard HZ-012 shows the policy the save returned beside the response`; `hazard HZ-014 says none is configured when the save returned none`), `hazard HZ-014 says who a NEWS2 entry's urgent alert reached (#26 review round 3)`; `apps/web/src/lib/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 hazard HZ-016 says who an alert actually reached: %s (#26 review round 3)` (table-driven: the nurse in charge, the fallback, no one, an alert saved before the route was kept, no alert); `apps/web/e2e/care-record.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 vital signs show the NEWS2 result and the RCP response the API returns` (the policy on the result focused after saving, and "The nurse in charge was told." on the entry, with axe); `apps/api/test/care.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-012 and HZ-014 NEWS2 vital signs` (the alert names the home); `packages/domain/src/care/fluid-balance.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 a drink offered and refused is never counted as drunk (#26 review round 3)` (table-driven: a refused drink adds nothing to the intake and is reported as offered; every drink refused leaves the whole target short; a drink not refused counts in full; a refused drink outside the day is neither counted nor offered); `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 fluid balance: a drink offered and refused is never counted as drunk (#26 review round 3)` (`leaves the intake and shortfall as they were and reports the amount offered`, on a fixed day so it passes at any hour); `apps/web/src/lib/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 says beside the totals what was offered and refused, never as drunk (#26 review round 3)`; `apps/mobile/src/care/entry-view.test.ts` (a refused drink reads "150 ml offered") | Passing (#26 review round 3) |

## HZ-015 Resuscitation decision missing, out of date or not visible

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-015 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (end of life); [ReSPECT](https://www.resus.org.uk/respect); hazard-log candidate list |

**Description**: staff cannot see, at the moment they need it, whether the person has a ReSPECT plan, DNACPR decision or advance decision to refuse treatment, or they see one that has been withdrawn or replaced.

**Causes**: the document is filed but not flagged; an old version is shown; the record is not available offline or in the downtime pack; the hospital transfer pack omits it, or prints it on a later page behind a long medicines list (slice 8 review round 3, clinical finding M5); a check of resuscitation decisions made in hospital recorded by someone who may not review the care plan (round 3, security finding M2). With only an advance decision to refuse treatment held, page 1 reads "not recorded" in bold and the refusal is missed (round 4, C7); with a ReSPECT or DNACPR form in force as well, page 1 prints the CPR decision and says nothing of the advance decision (round 5, M3).
**Causes**: the document is filed but not flagged; an old version is shown; the record is not available offline or in the downtime pack; the hospital transfer pack omits it; on the web, a header kept from an earlier tab after a colleague withdrew or recorded a decision (HZ-002); a status the screen does not know shown as if it were "Attempt CPR".

**Effects**: resuscitation attempted against the person's wishes, or not attempted when it should have been; distress to the person and family.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The point-of-care header shows the current resuscitation status (word and icon) with the date and clinician; a missing record shows as 'Not recorded', never as blank | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web: a warning chip that says to check the paper record; records that disagree are the strongest warning; an overdue review keeps the decision and says it still stands); mobile in slice 10 |
| Design | The person summary every point-of-care header reads carries the resuscitation status in force from an active ReSPECT or DNACPR record, with the clinician and dates, or `not_recorded`; records in force that disagree are flagged; a passed review date never hides a decision | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b (screens in slice 9) |
| Design | Go-live waits until every current person has a resuscitation status, either a decision in force or a recorded "no decision in place"; the full export's current summary gives the decision in force, or `conflicting` with every record in force ([platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rules 10 and 26) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Only one active record per kind; recording a new one supersedes the old; review dates raise tasks | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b (database partial unique index; hourly review job) |
| Design | The web header is read again on every tab move and says when (HZ-002); the resuscitation status is checked against the API's schema, and one that cannot be read, or that this version does not know, shows "CPR decision could not be read" as the strongest warning, never "Attempt CPR". Withdrawing a ReSPECT plan or DNACPR decision says first that the header will then show "CPR decision not recorded" unless another record is in force | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web) |
| Design | Advance care records are reference data on the device (never evicted while assigned), in the downtime pack and in the hospital transfer pack | Phase 1 |
| Design | The transfer pack prints the CPR decision in force by the same rule as the header (`resuscitationStatus`), from ReSPECT and DNACPR records only; an ADRT or advance care plan alone leaves it "not recorded" and still prints. Records that disagree print together as a conflict on the first page and need the named acknowledgement at hand-over; an overdue review is shown on the first page | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | The identity block on page 1 of the pack carries a "CPR decision:" line in bold, copied from the stored pack and never resolved: the decision in force with its record and date, "not recorded", "no decision in place" with who confirmed it (or that who confirmed it is not recorded), or that the records disagree (said once, in sentence case). With no ReSPECT or DNACPR but an advance decision to refuse treatment it reads "CPR decision: not recorded on a ReSPECT or DNACPR form. An advance decision to refuse treatment is held (date): read it before acting." With an advance decision in force, every other variant adds "An advance decision to refuse treatment is also held (date): read it before acting." (round 5, M3). A long medicines list cannot push it to a later page. The wording is for the CSO (H-94, H-133) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review rounds 3 to 5) |
| Design | The return check on resuscitation decisions made in hospital is recorded only by someone who may complete its task (HZ-005) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review round 3) |
| Design | On the downtime pack, every active ReSPECT and DNACPR record prints, newest first, with its clinician and dates. Records that disagree mark the section itself as conflicting, with the words "Resuscitation records disagree. Every record in force is listed here. Harbour does not choose between them: check the signed forms and follow the home's policy." A passed review date is noted and never hides the decision ([operations.md](../specs/operations.md) rule 5; wording for the CSO to confirm, H-100) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the original signed form stays with the person as the home's policy requires; Harbour shows the record, it is not the legal document | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| `apps/mobile/src/care/person-summary.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 resuscitation status in the header` (not recorded with a warning, never blank; DNACPR and ReSPECT decisions as word and icon with the form and clinician; an overdue review keeps the decision and is spoken; records that disagree flagged; withdrawn and unreadable rows ignored); `queries.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 resuscitation status from the device's records` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `packages/sync/test/care-streams.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 only advance care records in force, and active risk assessments` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/care/advance-care.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 a missing resuscitation record shows as not recorded`; `hazard HZ-015 the current resuscitation status` (latest decision, conflicts flagged, withdrawn and superseded records ignored, overdue review keeps the decision) | Passing |
| `packages/db/test/care-planning.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 only one advance care record of each kind is active` | Passing |
| `packages/contracts/src/contracts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 needs a resuscitation status on every point-of-care summary` | Passing |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/onboarding.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 no resuscitation status`, `hazard HZ-015 a recorded 'no decision in place' counts` (person readiness) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/care-planning.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 advance care records and the resuscitation status` (a DNACPR shows on the person summary and list; superseding and withdrawal) | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 prints the current resuscitation record in the pack` (reads the printed PDF: the CPR decision in force and the DNACPR with its clinician and date) and `hazard HZ-015 prints ReSPECT and DNACPR that disagree, and an overdue review, on the first page` (both records printed, the hand-over refused without an acknowledgement) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/people/transfer-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 keeps an ADRT beside the CPR decision but never as one`, `hazard HZ-015 prints ReSPECT and DNACPR that disagree as a conflict, with both, never resolved`, `hazard HZ-015 shows a resuscitation decision past its review date`; `apps/api/test/pdf.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 prints ReSPECT and DNACPR that disagree with both records, never resolved`, `hazard HZ-015 prints an ADRT alone as no CPR decision` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/pdf.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 says on page 1 that an ADRT is also held when the CPR line is from ...` (a DNACPR form in force, records that disagree, no decision in place, a pack stored before the decision in force was kept; and nothing said of one when none is held) (M3) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/downtime.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 hazard HZ-007 hazard HZ-001 builds the pack again after a new DNACPR, an allergy or a room swap` (the DNACPR in force on the person's page) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 part 3a-1) |
| `packages/domain/src/people/pre-admission.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 requires the resuscitation and advance care section` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/people/transfer-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 no resuscitation record`, `only a withdrawn resuscitation record`, `prints only the active resuscitation record` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/pdf.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 prints an ADRT alone as no CPR decision, and says on page 1 to read it`, `hazard HZ-015 says on page 1 when the CPR decision is ...` (now also: no decision in place with nobody recorded as confirming it), `hazard HZ-015 prints ReSPECT and DNACPR that disagree with both records, never resolved` (the conflict said once) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/pdf.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 prints the CPR decision in force on page 1, however long the medicines list` (25 long medicines) and `hazard HZ-015 says on page 1 when the CPR decision is ...` (none recorded, records that disagree); the two HZ-015 pack tests in `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts` read the line from page 1; `hazards HZ-005 and HZ-015 let only someone who may complete a return check's task record it` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 resuscitation status on the downtime pack` (`prints the decision in force`; `marks a conflict between ReSPECT and DNACPR in the section itself, and prints every record in force`; `prints two records that agree without a conflict`; `notes a decision past its review date without hiding it`; `puts safety-critical gaps and conflicts first on the front sheet`) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/care-record.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-015 a missing decision reads as a warning, never blank or calm`; `a DNACPR decision shows the word, icon, source and clinician`; `an overdue review keeps the decision and says it still stands`; `records that disagree are the strongest warning`; `a status that could not be read, or is unknown, is a warning, never 'Attempt CPR'`; `keeps the person but marks a resuscitation status it cannot read` | Passing (Phase 1 slice 9) |
| `apps/web/e2e/care-record.spec.ts` (Playwright with axe, WCAG 2.2 AA): `hazard HZ-001 hazard HZ-015 the header shows the resuscitation status, allergies and IDDSI levels on every tab, and 'not recorded' as a warning`; `hazard HZ-015 a DNACPR decision recorded on the advance care tab shows in the header on every tab`; `hazard HZ-002 hazard HZ-015 a colleague's change to the CPR decision shows in the header after one tab move` | Passing (local run; CI e2e-web on the PR) |
| `apps/web/e2e/pre-admission.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-007 hazard HZ-015 hazard HZ-016 the manager records an enquiry, assesses and decides with reasons and a condition` (the resuscitation section is never offered "not applicable" at pre-admission) (PEOP-005 and PEOP-007 web) | Passing (local run; CI e2e-web on the PR) |
