## HZ-016 Wrong IDDSI diet or fluid texture displayed or recorded

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-016 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (food chart with IDDSI); [IDDSI framework](https://www.iddsi.org/framework); hazard-log candidate list |

**Description**: the person's prescribed IDDSI food or drink level is shown wrongly, or food or fluid of a different level is recorded as given without challenge.

**Causes**: levels shown as numbers only; the levels missing from the header of a round or a handover (slice 5 review round 2, C-M3); the prescribed level is out of date after a speech and language therapy review; the food chart accepts any level silently; a form that pre-selects the prescribed level, so the record says the right texture was given without anyone choosing it; a drink level left optional when one is prescribed; a viewer who cannot read the care profile told "No level prescribed". Added in slice 11: food and drink levels merged on a printout, so a missing drink level hides behind a recorded food level; a stored code that is not an IDDSI level printed as if it were one.

**Effects**: choking, aspiration pneumonia, death.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Go-live waits until every current person has IDDSI food and drink levels recorded ([platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rule 26) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The prescribed food and drink levels show with their IDDSI number, name and colour in the header and on the food and fluid charts | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web: the header, and beside the level chosen on the food and drink forms, which ask for a reason on a mismatch); mobile in slice 10; the API's point-of-care header (`PersonSummary.iddsiFoodLevel` and `iddsiDrinkLevel`, `not_recorded` until recorded) on every round stop and handover in slice 5 (review round 2, C-M3) |
| Design | Recording a level that does not match the prescribed level needs an override reason and raises an alert to the nurse in charge | Phase 1 |
| Design | The web food and drink forms pre-select no level: the texture given is chosen every time, with the prescribed level shown beside it. A drink level is required when one is prescribed, and the API requires it too: from the web it refuses a drink with no level while a drink level is prescribed, naming the prescribed level (food always carries its level, which the contract requires). A viewer who cannot read the care profile is told the prescribed level is not available to them; the header says so too | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web); the API check in the care API follow-ups |
| Design | A change of prescribed level records who changed it, on whose advice, and shows as new for 72 hours | Phase 1 |
| Design | Eating and drinking (IDDSI levels) is a safety-critical section of the hospital transfer pack: printed with the level's number and name, and "not recorded" on the first page when missing. Whether it stays safety-critical is for the CSO (H-86) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | The downtime pack shows the food level and the drink level as two separate safety-critical sections, each with number, name and colour. A missing level reads "not recorded" with "check before giving food or drink"; a stored code that is not an IDDSI level reads "This could not be read from the record. Check the record before giving food or drink." and never stops the pack ([operations.md](../specs/operations.md) rules 1 and 6) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: kitchen and dietary profiles are kept in step (Phase 3 catering links them) | Phase 1 |
| Design | With a drink level prescribed, a drink is recorded with its level; with no care profile on the device the header and the chart say "Food and drink levels are not on this device. Check with the nurse in charge.", and a level, or a drink recorded with no level, is accepted only once the carer confirms they checked it with the nurse in charge | Built in Phase 1 (mobile, clinical reviews C3 and round 3 M3) |
| Design | From a device (a request naming `x-harbour-device`) the server keeps a food or drink entry it would refuse from the web for its level, because the food or drink was already given and the record of it must not be lost (clinical review C3): a drink with no level while one is prescribed, a different level with no reason, or a prescribed level stored that is not an IDDSI level. The entry is flagged (`flags.iddsiProblem`: `level_not_recorded`, `reason_missing`, `prescribed_unreadable`) and, when it may not be safe (no level while one is prescribed, a level more solid or thinner than prescribed, an unreadable prescription), the nurse in charge is alerted as for an unsafe level. A code that is not an IDDSI level is still refused. The web shows the flag in words with an icon on the saved entry, in place of the reason line ([care.md](../specs/care.md) rule 6; H-160) | Built in Phase 1 (care API follow-ups) |
| Design | Every IDDSI problem of a kept entry is shown in words with an icon, never nothing: no level; a thinner drink or more solid food than prescribed with no reason, said plainly to be possibly unsafe; a safer level with no reason; a prescribed level that could not be read. Who the alert reached is said from what happened, kept on the entry (`flags.alertRoute`): "The nurse in charge was told.", the fallback when no nurse in charge could open the record, or "No one who can open this record could be told. Tell the nurse in charge or a manager now."; an alert kept before the route was recorded says "Check that the nurse in charge knows.", never that anyone was told (#26 review round 3). The phone, which cannot know offline who the server will reach, says of an unsafe level "When this is sent, the nurse in charge or a manager will be told. Tell them now if you can." (#26 review round 4). The alert reaches only someone who can open the record, and is audited when no one can be told (HZ-014). A drink recorded as refused needs no level and raises nothing, on the web, the phone and the server (#26 review round 2, C1 and C2). The level given is compared with the prescription when the entry reaches the server, not when it was given (H-166) | Built in Phase 1 (#26 review rounds 2 and 3) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/handover-summary.test.ts`: `words both texture alerts for food or a drink, and says they may not be safe (#123 clinical review, M5)`; `packages/domain/src/care/handover.test.ts`: food rows in `reads the trigger of a care result from its observation's flags` | Passing (PR #123) |
| `apps/web/src/components/care/ObservationList.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 says a different level was kept with no reason, instead of an empty reason` (the fallback said, never "the nurse in charge was told"), `hazard HZ-016 says plainly that a thinner drink kept with no reason may not be safe` (no one could be told), `hazard HZ-016 says when the prescribed level could not be read, never nothing` (an alert saved before the route was kept says to check); `apps/web/src/lib/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-014 hazard HZ-016 says who an alert actually reached: %s (#26 review round 3)` | Passing (#26 review round 3) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/onboarding.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 no IDDSI drink level`, `hazard HZ-016 no IDDSI food level` (person readiness) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/care/iddsi.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 a drink needs its IDDSI level when a drink level is prescribed` (`says which level is prescribed when a drink has none`); `hazard HZ-016 an entry recorded on a device is kept and flagged, never refused for its level (C3)` (the problem and the alert for each case; `still refuses a level that is not an IDDSI level, from a device too`) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 a drink needs its IDDSI level when a drink level is prescribed` (`refuses a drink with no level from the web, saying which level is prescribed`; `keeps a drink with no level from a device, flagged, and tells the nurse in charge (C3)`; `keeps an unsafe level with no reason from a device, flagged and alerted, and refuses it from the web`; `takes a drink with no level when none is prescribed, with no flags`) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/web/src/components/care/ObservationList.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 says a drink was kept without its level though one is prescribed`, `hazard HZ-016 says a different level was kept with no reason, instead of an empty reason`; `apps/web/src/lib/refusals.test.ts`: `recognises hazard HZ-016 a drink without its level while a drink level is prescribed` (the API's refusal at the level field) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 says every IDDSI problem of a kept entry: %s` (table-driven: no level, a thinner drink, more solid food, a safer level, an unreadable prescription); `apps/web/src/components/care/ObservationList.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 says plainly that a thinner drink kept with no reason may not be safe (#26 review round 2, C1)`, `hazard HZ-016 says when the prescribed level could not be read, never nothing (#26 review round 2, C1)`; `apps/web/src/lib/chart-entry.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 asks no level for a drink refused, since none was given`; `apps/mobile/src/care/charts.test.ts`: `asks no level for a drink refused, since none was given (#26 review round 2)`; `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 asks no IDDSI level of a drink refused, and raises nothing`, and the C2 alert tests under HZ-014 | Passing (#26 review round 2) |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-016 a food entry at a different IDDSI level needs an override reason` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-016 recorded IDDSI levels are checked against the prescribed level` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-016 IDDSI levels show number, name and colour` | Passing |
| `apps/web/src/lib/care-record.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 food level 5 shows number, name and colour` (and the other levels, and no level prescribed); `apps/web/src/lib/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 shows IDDSI levels with number, name and colour` | Passing (Phase 1 slice 9) |
| `apps/web/e2e/care-record.spec.ts` (Playwright with axe, WCAG 2.2 AA): `hazard HZ-016 food at a texture other than the prescribed level is refused without a reason` (the header shows the prescribed level; the form shows it beside the choice with nothing chosen; saving without a level is refused at the level; the API's refusal is shown in the catalogue's words at the level and the reason, then the entry with its reason is saved) | Passing (local run; CI e2e-web on the PR) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 IDDSI levels on food and drink` (the prescribed level beside the choice; food more solid or a drink thinner than prescribed refused without a reason and marked unsafe; a softer level refused without a reason; saved with a reason and the alert flag); `person-summary.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 prescribed IDDSI levels in the header` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/charts.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 food and drink levels on the device (C3)` (a drink needs its level when one is prescribed; a level is blocked without a profile until confirmed), `hazard HZ-016 asks the same confirmation for a drink with no level when the profile is not on the device (round 3 M3)`; `person-summary.test.ts`: `warns that the levels are not recorded when the profile is not on the device (C3)`; `person-header.render.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-016 says the food and drink levels are not recorded when the profile is not on the device (C3)` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |

## HZ-017 Late or retrospective entry mistaken for a contemporaneous record

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-017 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (late-entry flag), 13.4 (downtime forms and back-entry workflow), 18 (times); [NMC record keeping](https://www.nmc.org.uk/standards/code/read-the-code-online/) |

**Description**: a note, chart entry or incident written long after the event, or backdated, reads as if it were written at the time, so decisions or investigations rely on it wrongly.

**Causes**: only one time stored; device clock wrong (HZ-010); offline entries synced days later; edits overwrite the original. Added in slice 11: care written on paper during an outage and entered hours later (back-entry) without saying so; the paper time mistyped, or outside the outage it belongs to; the same paper form entered twice by two staff (a fluid chart double-counted); paper forms never entered at all. Added in the slice 11 review: a paper time written in the hour the clocks skip or repeat (HZ-009); a paper record typed in through the normal care screen, so it looks contemporaneous; the same event recorded on paper and on a device that stayed online (paper against digital); the person who entered the forms also signing off the reconciliation.

**Effects**: misleading care history; unsafe decisions based on charts that look complete; investigations and inquests misled.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Every entry stores the event time, the device time and the server-received time; entries recorded more than the rate-table late-entry threshold after the event are flagged late everywhere they appear | Phase 1 |
| Design | Entries are append-only; a correction supersedes the original with a reason and both remain readable | Phase 1 |
| Design | Future event times beyond the clock-drift tolerance are refused | Built in Phase 1 (mobile, clinical review round 3 H1; HZ-010) |
| Design | A correction keeps the original entry's instant exactly by default; "When" is today unless the carer chooses an earlier day within the offline window, and says which date and time will be recorded (with BST or GMT on a clock-change day). A time still to come today is refused, never dated yesterday on its own: yesterday only through the day choice | Built in Phase 1 (mobile, clinical reviews C4 and round 3 H1) |
| Design | Paper times (`resolvePaperTime` in `packages/domain/src/operations/outage.ts`, using `resolveRecordedUkTime`): the date and time written on the form are kept as written; a time in the skipped spring hour is refused, a time in the repeated autumn hour must say which one, and a time ahead of the clock is refused ([operations.md](../specs/operations.md) rule 11; HZ-009) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | Back-entry from paper (`checkBackEntry`): the resolved paper time is the event time, the late-entry reason is `downtime` and the entry names its declared outage at the person's location; a paper time outside the outage window is refused unless a reason is given, which is shown with the entry; every back-entry carries the words "Written on paper ... during the outage ... Entered in Harbour ... on the device, received ..." (the device time and the server-received time) wherever it appears (operations.md rule 12) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11; columns and routes with the schema and API step) |
| Design | Through the normal route (`checkEntryAgainstOutages`): any care entry whose event time falls inside a declared outage at its location, but which was written after the outage ended or past the late-entry threshold, must carry back-entry details or say why it is not from paper; entries made at the time on a device that kept working offline are allowed (operations.md rule 13). The database does not check this: it depends on the API applying it on every care write route and on the sync upload (#38 review round 1) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | In the database (`app.entry_outage_guard`, `app.outage_guard`, migration 0053; #38 review round 1): a back-entry's time is the instant its paper date and time give in UK time (the occurrence chosen in the repeated hour, never the skipped hour); a time outside the outage window `[start, end)` needs a reason, checked on every entry; a cancelled outage takes no entries and a reconciled one only corrections of entries citing it, back-entries or not from paper (#38 review round 2: a wrong value is always correctable); a back-entry is corrected once, a further correction superseding the correction, so each paper record has one current version, and the outage is locked for share so a cancel or reconciliation at the same moment waits and counts the entry; a correction stays a back-entry of the same outage, and cannot make an entry not entered from paper a back-entry; superseded entries do not count for the cancel and window checks; the reconciler entered and sent none of the original back-entries; closing may set the true end before back-entries already entered, and reconciliation then waits until each one outside the window with no reason has been reviewed (H-207); a correction of the start or end may not leave outside a back-entry that was inside | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 part 2) |
| Design | Possible duplicates (`possibleDuplicateBackEntries`, `checkOutageReconciliation`): entries for the same person and kind within `downtime.duplicate_match_minutes` of each other are grouped, paper against paper and paper against digital, unless their form references differ; an outage cannot be reconciled until each group is reviewed and a manager says what was checked; the reconciler cannot be anyone who entered records for that outage; the summary counts forms entered and entries without a form reference; overdue back-entry escalates after `downtime.back_entry_due_hours` (operations.md rules 10 and 14) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | A task done or not done on the phone is recorded as completed when the move reaches Harbour, not when the care was given: the phone keeps the move's device time in its own row only, which errs towards a task looking late in the meantime, never on time when it was not. Taking the time of care from the phone needs task columns for the time of care, the time received and a late flag (a migration), a late label wherever the task shows, a lower bound at the task's creation or its window, and a rule for which shift's handover the outcome belongs to (STATUS item 15 follow-up; CSO decision H-309) | Open (Phase 1 slice 10 part 2) |
| Design | On the web (slice 11 web, `feat/web-downtime`): a back-entered note, observation, body map mark, activity or visit shows "Entered from a paper record" (icon and words) with the API's label wherever its entry badges appear (`EntryBadges`), or, when the reader cannot see the outage, the date and time written on the paper; an entry written later and not from paper shows "Written later, not from paper" with its reason, beside the late-entry flag | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web) |
| Design | On the web, the outage page offers closing, correcting (with a reason), cancelling (with a reason) and reconciling only in the state the API accepts and only to holders of `operations.downtime.manage` who work at the location; nothing is chosen for the person (no time is filled in); reconciling asks the manager to tick each possible duplicate group and each back-entry outside the window with no reason, and to say what was checked, and shows the API's blockers in its words; a manager who entered or sent a paper record is told someone else must reconcile (H-203). Entries are listed by reference only, as the API returns ids (HZ-092) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web) |
| Design | On the web care record forms (notes, chart entries, body map marks, activities, visits; slice 11 web part B): "When" offers "From a paper downtime form" only while the person's location has an open or closed outage and only where the form itself is offered (`access.reaching`); it asks which outage, the date and time written on the form (nothing filled in; the repeated autumn hour asks which), the form's reference, and why when the time is outside the outage (at least `MIN_BACK_ENTRY_REASON_LENGTH` characters); the entry's time is the paper time. When the API asks why an entry for a time in an outage is not from paper, the form asks for that reason; correcting a paper note whose time is now after the outage's true end asks why (H-208) and sends the paper details again with it; a second correction is refused in words that say to reload and correct the current version | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web part B) |
| Design | A vital signs reading entered from paper says, before saving, that it is from the time written on it, and its NEWS2 result says first "This reading is from {paper time}. Check it was escalated on paper, and check the person now.", so an urgent response is never read as for a reading taken now (#64 review round 1; wording H-268). A record kept for review after a reconciled outage starts the form again with a new id; a recent reconciled or cancelled outage is said beside "When" (give the form to the nurse in charge) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web part B) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| `packages/db/test/care-record.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 notes and observations cannot be updated or deleted by the app` | Passing |
| `packages/db/test/care-record.test.ts`: corrections supersede; replayed device ids are ignored | Passing |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/notes.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 late and corrected notes` (event time and time written kept apart; a correction supersedes with a reason, never an edit); `entry-view.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 corrections and late entries in lists`; `queries.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 a note written on the device and its correction read back as one current entry`; `sync/care-upload.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 sends a correction with the entry it supersedes and the reason` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/notes.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 a correction keeps the original's event time by default (C4)`, `hazard HZ-017 dates a note yesterday only when the carer chooses yesterday (C4)`; `time.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 earlier days within the offline window (C4)`, `hazard HZ-017 a correction keeps the original instant unchanged (C4)` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |

## HZ-018 Safety-critical item omitted from shift handover

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-018 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (tasks and rounds), 15.1 (handover); see also HZ-073 (an item hidden from a reader by their access scope) |

**Description**: the incoming shift is not told about a fall, a raised NEWS2, a refusal, a new wound, an open incident or an overdue task from the outgoing shift.

**Causes**: handover written from memory; compiled list editable so items are removed; items recorded offline sync after the handover is compiled; nobody acknowledges it; the configured shift pattern does not match the real rota (an event just before the configured start belongs to the wrong shift); a fall or refusal recorded only in free text or in a chart field the compiler does not read; an urgent alert or a skipped task outlives the shift it was raised in; a late addition arrives after the receiving shift has gone home; the handover is compiled in the view of whoever asked, so items about people outside their scope are missed; a stored handover is edited so an item disappears; an urgent alert is listed once per person it reached, or cannot be tied to a location; a member of staff is asked to acknowledge, or told about, items about people outside their scope, so the requests are ignored (HZ-008) (slice 5 build); one acknowledgement, even the outgoing staff's own before the shift ended, clears "unacknowledged" for everyone and nobody is told when the incoming shift never reads it (C-M5); staff told to "record anything new on the person's record" after a handover closes, which no handover reads unless it is a fall or a refusal (C-M6); a late addition that no acknowledger can see reaches nobody (C-M4); one organisation's failure stops the sweep for every other, and a missed run leaves a shift without a handover (slice 5 review round 2); one acknowledgement by someone who can see only part of the handover clears it for the whole incoming shift; late-addition notices go to outgoing staff who read it back before going home, and every refresh of a shift still running notifies the nurse in charge; a location with no nurse in charge hears nothing when a handover goes unacknowledged (slice 5 review round 3).

**Effects**: missed observation or treatment; repeated harm; delayed escalation.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Handover compiles automatically from the shift's records by rule (falls, NEWS2 above the low band, incidents, urgent alerts raised in the shift or still unacknowledged, new body-map marks, care notes tagged "Include in handover" (the fixed `handover` tag, item kind `flagged_note`), overdue, skipped and unfinished tasks, MUST follow-ups, refusals from every chart that records one and from fixed note tags); compiled items cannot be removed, only annotated. Which notes should reach the handover beyond these is for the CSO (H-155; master prompt 10.1 says "auto-compiled from the shift's notes") | Phase 1 (the `handover` tag added in slice 5 review round 2, C-M6) |
| Design | Shift patterns are set per location to the minute with two or more shifts; the defaults are flagged for confirmation before go-live | Phase 1 |
| Design | Items that sync after compilation are appended to both affected handovers and marked as late additions; the incoming staff are notified while on shift, otherwise the nurse in charge on duty | Phase 1 |
| Design | Each incoming member of staff acknowledges the handover; an acknowledgement records the keys of the items seen, so a late addition makes it pending again and shows only the new items; unacknowledged handovers show on the nurse-in-charge view | Phase 1 |
| Design | The handover is stored per location and shift and always compiled in the system's view of the organisation, never the requester's, so nothing is missed because of who asked (who asked is audited). It is compiled on request (never for a time before the offline window, and at most `HANDOVER_COMPILE_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE` times a minute per user) and by a worker job every 5 minutes, which compiles and locks every shift that ended within the offline window if nobody has, so a missed run or an outage loses none, and locks a handover when its shift ends; open handovers are refreshed each run, and locked ones from the offline window (`mobile.offline_window_days` and a day) when an entry that happened in their shift arrives after their last refresh, read back 10 minutes because an entry's recorded time is when its transaction began (an entry committed just after a refresh it began before is never missed). Sources are read at the location: open tasks due by the end of the shift, MUST follow-ups raised in it, tasks staff skipped or cancelled in it, vital signs by their stored NEWS2 band, refusals, fixed note tags, body-map marks, incidents with their people, and urgent alerts ([care.md](../specs/care.md) rule 8 as built in slice 5) | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 |
| Design | Items are append-only rows (`handover_item`: no UPDATE or DELETE grant, `append_only` triggers, a unique key and position per handover), behind `checkHandoverKeepsItems`; only the system's view may insert or update a handover or its items (restrictive policies on scope `tenant`); a trigger refuses any update that changes the organisation, location, shift or first compilation, soft-deletes a handover or unlocks a locked one; every refresh is versioned in `record_version` | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (items as rows and system-only writes in review round 2, S-M2) |
| Design | Urgent alerts name the person and location they concern (`notification.person_id`, `location_id`; migration 0028 fills them for earlier alerts from their source); each alert is one item however many people it reached (`alertConditions`), counted acknowledged from the first acknowledgement, with escalation copies and the handover's own notices left out | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 |
| Design | Acknowledgements are append-only, take a client id (a retry is recorded once), carry the server time and are only ever one's own (row-level security). The reader sends the seen token of the view they were shown; the server records the keys of the items they could see and refuses an old token (409), so nobody acknowledges an item they were not shown. Each reader sees, and is asked to acknowledge, only the items about people in their scope (`viewerAcknowledgement`); who is still pending is worked out over each member's own scope with the database's own person-scope policy. Until rotas say who comes on (Phase 2), the incoming staff are those who acknowledge: a handover counts as acknowledged by the incoming shift only when every item is covered by at least one acknowledgement made at or after its shift's end, by the keys each acknowledgement recorded (`incomingAcknowledged`), so one narrowly scoped acknowledgement, the outgoing staff's read-back, or an item added since never clears it, and access that ends later does not undo it. Once the shift has ended without that it is marked unacknowledged, and after `handover.acknowledgement_grace_minutes` the nurse in charge on duty gets one acknowledgeable alert per handover (`care.handover.unacknowledged`, within a day of falling due; with no nurse in charge, the longest-serving Registered Manager anywhere, else the longest-serving holder of `care.handover.manage`; with no one, audited once with `told: 0`; H-153) | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (incoming acknowledgement and the alert in review round 2, C-M5; seen token, S-M1; coverage by recorded keys and the fallback in round 3) |
| Design | Late additions are announced once per refresh and recipient (`lateAdditionNotices`), never before the receiving shift starts: while it runs, to the staff who acknowledged on it (at or after its start), about what they can see; to the nurse in charge on duty (clinical leads and team leaders at the location, else the Registered Manager; assignments in force, staff still employed) about anything no acknowledger can see, always about a fall, a raised NEWS2, an incident or an urgent alert, and about everything once the receiving shift has ended; urgent with push only for such an item the recipient can see, a warning in the app otherwise (`lateAdditionSeverity`, HZ-008), with a title that names nobody | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (nurse in charge added in review round 2, C-M4) |
| Design | Notes by the outgoing staff on the handover or one of its items until the shift ends or it is locked (`handoverOpen`, `checkHandoverNote`); a note on an item takes that item's person (a trigger) and follows their scope. Once closed, the refusal says: "This handover has closed. Add a care note tagged "Include in handover" instead: the next handover carries it, and if you time it within this shift it is added to this handover too." | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (advice corrected in review round 2, C-M6) |
| Design | One organisation's failure in any worker sweep never stops the others: its transaction rolls back, the failure is logged by opaque ids and error code, and audited as `platform.job.tenant_failed` | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (review round 2) |
| Design | On the phone (slice 10 part 2): the handovers compiled for shifts ending on a day of the offline window sync with every item and note the reader may see, including those about a person who has since died or been discharged (their record leaves the device, the handover item does not). A shift nobody has compiled yet has no handover on the phone, and one whose `locked_at` is empty is not final: the screen is to say so, and it must not ship until the gate in STATUS is met (each item's words or source on the device, the expected shift known on the device, H-300, H-303). Acknowledging offline will send the seen token over the items the phone showed, which the server refuses (409) when its view differs, so nobody acknowledges an item they were not shown | Streams built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2 (the tables are published by migration 0052, which `packages/db/test/sync-publication.test.ts` checks for every table a stream reads); offline acknowledgement and the screen to follow |
| Design | Each handover item carries a worded summary, compiled with it from its source and kept with the append-only item (`handover_item.summary`, migration 0071): a catalogue key (`handover.summary.*`, one per kind) and its parameters, which are codes (a NEWS2 band and score, a chart, an incident type, an alert kind and, for a care result, why it was raised: the NEWS2 threshold or the IDDSI problem, a mark's type, view, region and side) and, for a task, its title, the care plan action it carries out, whether it was from an earlier plan when compiled, and the reason it was not done. Never whether an alert was acknowledged: an item never changes once compiled, so that would soon be untrue (#50 review round 1, clinical M1 to M3). The key and codes are never English and no field holds a person's name, but a task's title, plan action and reason not done are staff's free text and may mention one; so a summary is shown only to those who may read its sources (ADR-0071). Each app words it from its own catalogue (the phone: `mobile.` + key; the Welsh entries are English placeholders, listed for translation), so the phone words incidents, alerts and skipped tasks it does not hold (#123 clinical review, L4). An item without a summary, or with one the phone cannot read, is worded by its kind, never blank (STATUS screens gate item (a); H-300) | Built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2 (data and words; the screen to follow) |
| Design | The phone learns the shift pattern from its sync token (`handover_shifts`: each nation's day and night start times from the rate table, where the handover family reaches; ADR-0074), so the handover screen can work out offline which handover the phone should hold and say "not compiled yet", "not final" (`locked_at` empty) or that it cannot tell when a nation's figures are missing (left out of the token, and logged for the operator). Until that screen merges (`feat/mobile-handover-screen`) nothing on the phone reads the claim (STATUS screens gate item (e); H-303; #123 clinical review, L5) | Built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2 (token; the screen in its own PR) |
| Design | On the web (slice 5 web, `feat/web-rounds-handover`): a location's handover page shows the shift running now (compiled, or a button to compile it) and the last week's; one handover lists its items by person, most urgent first, each kind in an icon and words with its time and a link to its record, marked when added since it was compiled or new to the reader; notes on each item and on the whole handover while it is open (a UUIDv7 per note, so a retry is recorded once); who has read it and who has new items to read; "I have read this handover" sends the token of the handover as shown, and when items were added since, the page reloads with them and says so; a reader limited to their own people is told so; the incoming shift's missing acknowledgement is a warning chip (icon and words) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 5 web) |
| Design | On the web home page (INSI-001, `feat/web-dashboards`, docs/specs/insights.md): the handover tile counts ended shifts' handovers the incoming shift has not acknowledged, or with a late addition still to be seen, as "To do" (how long the incoming shift has is the tenant's to set: H-410); a view limited to the reader's people says "Not known", never "every one acknowledged"; for one location it says whether the shift running now has a handover | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| --- | --- |

| `packages/domain/src/care/handover.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 items synced after compilation are appended as late additions` (items are never removed; an acknowledgement covers the items seen, including one made in the same instant as a late addition; late additions reach the nurse in charge once the receiving shift has ended) | Passing (domain) |
| `packages/domain/src/care/handover.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 refusals are read from every chart that records one`; shifts to the minute, two or three per day, with no gap or overlap across both clock changes | Passing (domain) |
| `packages/domain/src/care/handover.test.ts` (slice 5): `hazard HZ-018 the shift a handover compiles at the end of` (day, night, the 13-hour nights when the clocks go back in 2026 and 2027 and the 11-hour night when they go forward in 2027; open for notes until the shift ends or it is locked), `compiles the 11-hour night when the clocks go forward in 2027, including its last hour` and `the 13-hour night when the clocks go back in 2027`, `hazard HZ-018 an urgent alert is one item however many people it reached` (acknowledged from the first acknowledgement; escalation copies and every handover notice left out), `hazard HZ-018 each member of staff acknowledges the items they can see` (late additions about other people do not ask again; every acknowledgement of their own counts by its keys, never another person's), `hazard HZ-018 the incoming shift has acknowledged only when its acknowledgements cover every item` (table-driven: nobody; only the outgoing read-back; every item after the end; at the end; an item missed; a carer's own items only, which no longer clears it; two people who together cover every item; the outgoing read-back never filling an incoming gap; an empty handover still needing an incoming acknowledgement; overtaken by a late addition nobody incoming has acknowledged; the alert due from the grace period for a day, never once acknowledged), `hazard HZ-018 puts a note on the handover only when it is tagged "Include in handover"`, `hazard HZ-018 notes add to a handover; they never replace an item` (`names the tag that reaches the next handover when this one has closed`) | Passing (Phase 1 slice 5) |
| `packages/db/test/handover.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 a handover can only be added to` (moving the shift or changing the first compilation refused; an item twice or two in one place refused; a late addition appended to a locked handover; versioned; never unlocked), `hazard HZ-018 only the system's view writes a handover or its items`, `hazard HZ-018 acknowledgements are each person's own and append-only` (items, notes and acknowledgements: no update or delete, even for the owner role), `hazard HZ-018 alerts name the location and person they concern` (another organisation's person refused; migration 0028's backfill, re-run) | Passing (Phase 1 slice 5) |

another view's token refused with 409; a limited reader sees only their own acknowledgement), `hazard HZ-018 appends a late addition and asks again, for the new item only, those who can see it, notifying nobody while its own shift runs` (an old token refused; an addition about someone outside the care worker's scope neither asks them nor moves their counts or token; no notice while the handover's own shift runs), `hazard HZ-018 takes notes on an open handover and its items, never on an item out of scope`, `hazard HZ-018 the worker locks the last shift's handover and appends entries synced late` (unacknowledged once the shift has ended; notes refused once closed, naming the tag; a fall recorded offline in the shift appended and told to the nurse in charge when nobody has acknowledged; acknowledged on the incoming shift, then overtaken by a refusal the acknowledger is told of; nothing written when nothing is new; an entry whose transaction began before the last refresh still appended), `hazard HZ-018 compiles and locks every shift in the offline window, so a missed run loses none`, `hazard HZ-018 alerts the nurse in charge once when nobody on the incoming shift has acknowledged by the grace period`, `hazard HZ-018 with no nurse in charge at a location, alerts the longest-serving Registered Manager, else audits that nobody could be told`, `hazard HZ-018 one organisation's failure never stops another's sweep, and is audited`, `hazard HZ-018 a care note tagged "Include in handover" reaches the handover; an untagged one does not`, and in `refuses a shift that has not started, ...` a time before the offline window | Passing (Phase 1 slice 5) |
| `packages/sync/test/task-handover-streams.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 keeps an item about someone who died in the shift, though their record leaves`, and the other tests under `hazard HZ-011 hazard HZ-018 the handover follows the handover views (care.handover.manage)` | Passing (the sync-rules engine); the tables are published by migration 0052, which `packages/db/test/sync-publication.test.ts` checks for every table a stream reads |
| `packages/domain/src/care/handover.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 words a %s item from %s as %s` (every kind from its source), `gives every compiled item a summary, and a late addition keeps its own`; `packages/db/test/round-gate-data.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 a handover item's summary is a catalogue key with its parameters` (free English, another namespace or missing parameters refused; append-only with it); `apps/api/test/rounds-and-handover.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 words each item from its source as a catalogue key and parameters, kept with the item` (no item without one, no person's name); `packages/sync/test/task-handover-streams.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 sends each item's worded summary to whole and limited readers alike`; `apps/mobile/src/care/handover-summary.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-018 a handover item's words on the phone, from the server's summary` (every kind, English and Welsh entries for every key, worded by kind when the summary is missing or unreadable); `apps/api/test/api.test.ts`: `issues a manager a token for their location's people` (the shift pattern claim) | Passing (Phase 1 slice 10 part 2) |

| `packages/domain/src/platform/access.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-011 hazard HZ-018 a permission reaches no further than what its records show (ADR-0071; #50 review round 1, S-M1)` (care plans too: `handovers without care plans`); `apps/api/test/scope-per-permission.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-011 hazard HZ-018 handovers reach no further than incidents, notes, the person and care plans (ADR-0071; #50 review round 1, S-M1; #123 security review, M1)` | Passing (#50 review round 1, security M1; #123 security review, M1) |

## HZ-019 Body-map side or site recorded wrongly

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-019 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (body map and skin) |

**Description**: a wound, pressure ulcer or bruise is recorded on the wrong side or site, because the drawing is mirrored (front and back views) or tapped imprecisely on a small screen.

**Causes**: front and back views mirror left and right; side shown only by position on the drawing; no confirmation in words.

**Effects**: wrong site treated or checked; deterioration of the real wound missed; safeguarding concern misjudged.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Side and region are chosen in words as well as on the drawing, and must agree before saving; front and back views label the person's left and right | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web: front and back drawings labelled with the person's left and right, a list of the same areas for keyboard and screen reader users, the side confirmed in words, and the API's refusal shown word for word); mobile in slice 10 |
| Design | Wound photos are linked to the mark, with a ruler prompt | Phase 1 |
| Design | The body map is read against the body's midline, with a narrow centre band over the head and trunk only, so a mark on a leg or an arm is always on a side; three buttons choose the same zones for screen readers | Built in Phase 1 (mobile, clinical review C5) |
| Design | The server works out the drawn side itself whenever a place is sent: every screen places marks on one drawing in its own units (`BODY_MAP_DRAWING`), each mark carries where it was placed (the point, or the zone chosen with a button), and `sideFromDrawing` reads the person's side from the place and the view. A mark with no place and no side drawn, a point off the drawing, a point and zone that disagree, a side in words that disagrees, or a side a device worked out that is not the server's, is refused; the place and side are stored with the mark. The web sends each area's spot (spots moved so each reads as its own side) and the phone the point tapped or the zone chosen; an entry queued before is sent as the zone its drawn side names ([care.md](../specs/care.md) rule 7; H-162) | Built in Phase 1 (care API follow-ups) |
| Design | A phone from before the server read the drawing sends only the side it worked out: the server reads that side as the zone it names on the view (`zoneForPersonSide`, as the current phone does for queued entries), still checks the side in words against it, and flags the mark `drawn_from_device_side`, so those marks are kept rather than refused and the record says the place came from the phone. A refused mark that may be harm (a pressure ulcer of category 2 or worse, or with no category or one that cannot be read, since it may be worse; a bruise, a skin tear, a laceration, a burn or a wound) makes its review urgent at once, with the nurse in charge told; only a category 1 pressure ulcer waits as routine (#26 review rounds 2 and 3, C4; H-162) | Built in Phase 1 (#26 review rounds 2 and 3) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-019 a mark whose drawn side and stated side differ cannot be saved` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-019 body map sides are the person's own left and right` | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/care.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 body map` (a mark whose stated side differs from the drawing is refused; a category 3 ulcer prompts an incident and the safeguarding guide) | Passing |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/body-map.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 the side on the drawing and in words` (front and back views; a mark drawn on one side and described as the other refused with both sides named; the midline only for central areas); `sync/care-upload.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 sends the place drawn, the side drawn and the side chosen in words, and reads booleans` so the API repeats the check | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/body-map.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 the drawing is split at the body's midline (C5)` (a mark on the person's left leg reads left on both views; no centre band between the legs; the band only over the head and trunk; taps mapped from the frame to the drawing) | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `apps/api/test/incidents.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 links a mark recorded for the incident and refuses one for someone not on it` (marks keep the body map's checks; linked pressure ulcers reach the classifier) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/body-map.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 match the API's regions and the sides each allows, on both views`; `hazard HZ-019 places the person's left on your right on the front view and on your left on the back`; `hazard HZ-019 reads the midline and either side of it the same way as the domain, on both views`; `apps/web/src/lib/refusals.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 says a side mismatch in the catalogue's words, at the side and the area` (the rule run in the domain package) | Passing (Phase 1 slice 9) |
| `apps/web/e2e/care-record.spec.ts` (Playwright with axe, WCAG 2.2 AA): `hazard HZ-019 a body map mark whose side in words differs from the drawing is refused` (a tap on the front view's knee on your right, the person's left, with "Right" chosen in words, is refused, with the error at the side question and the body area list; the list then records the person's right knee); `keyboard only: move between tabs and record a body map mark with the area list and the side in words` | Passing (local run; CI e2e-web on the PR) |
| `packages/domain/src/care/body-map.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 the server works out the side from the place drawn and the view` (`takes the point and the zone together only when they agree`; `reads the drawing in its own units, with a narrow band over the trunk only`) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `packages/db/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 the drawing on a body-map mark` (`keeps the point, zone and side worked out together`; a partial or impossible drawing refused by `body_map_mark_drawing`) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 the server works out the side of a body-map mark from the drawing` (`reads the person's left on the viewer's right of the front view, and stores what was drawn`; `refuses a side in words that disagrees with the side the drawing shows, on either view`; `never takes the side a device says it drew: one that disagrees with the drawing is refused`; `takes the zone chosen with the buttons, and refuses a point and a zone that disagree`; `refuses a mark with no place on the drawing`) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/body-map.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 sends the API a place on the shared drawing that reads as each spot's own side, on both views`; `apps/mobile/src/care/body-map.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 sends the point tapped, which the API reads as the same side the device did`, `hazard HZ-019 asks for the drawing again when the point and the zone disagree`; `apps/mobile/src/sync/care-upload.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 sends the place drawn, the side drawn and the side chosen in words, and reads booleans`, `hazard HZ-019 sends an entry queued with only the side drawn (%s view, %s) as its zone of the drawing` | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 marks from phones not yet updated, and refused marks that may be harm (#26 review round 2, C4)` (`reads a mark sent with only the side the phone worked out as that side's zone, and flags it`, the side in words still checked; `makes the review of a refused bruise urgent at once`; `reviews a refused copy of %s urgently: %s (#26 review round 3)`, table-driven: an observation, a pressure ulcer with no category, one with a category that cannot be read, category 2, category 1 (routine), a bruise, a rash, a care note; `makes the review of a refused pressure ulcer with no category urgent at once (#26 review round 3)`), and `refuses a mark with no place on the drawing and no side drawn`; `packages/domain/src/care/body-map.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-019 body map sides are the person's own left and right` (each side read back to its zone by `zoneForPersonSide`) | Passing (#26 review round 2) |

## HZ-020 Deprivation-of-liberty authorisation lapses unnoticed

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-020 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.4; [Mental Capacity Act 2005 Schedule A1](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2005/9/schedule/A1); [DHSC interim guidance after the June 2026 Supreme Court judgment](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/changes-to-the-definition-of-deprivation-of-liberty/uk-supreme-court-2026-judgment-on-what-constitutes-a-deprivation-of-liberty) (updated 22 September 2026) |

**Description**: an urgent or standard authorisation expires, or a review required by the 2026 legal change is not done, while the person is still deprived of their liberty.

**Causes**: expiry dates kept outside the record; no reminder; the legal-change review not tracked; the CQC outcome notification not drafted.

**Effects**: unlawful deprivation of liberty; the person's rights not protected; regulatory action.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | DoLS register with expiry clocks from the rate table; tasks at 28 and 7 days before expiry (configurable) to the Registered Manager | Phase 1 |
| Design | The outcome of an application drafts the CQC notification (HZ-013) | Phase 1 |
| Design | Tenant switch for the 2026 legal change turns on the review-of-existing-authorisations workflow and lists every authorisation awaiting review | Phase 1 |
| Design | Authorisation dates are checked against Schedule A1 (urgent period, one extension request, extension period, standard period) before they are saved; the register shows status and days left; a reminder that would fall before an urgent authorisation starts fires at its start (`packages/domain/src/care/mca/dols.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The legal-change review records the DHSC guidance's multifactorial assessment, objection and consent signs, and cannot close without the local authority referral when the person is no longer deprived of liberty or there is doubt | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The API stores the register and checks it with the domain rules; a known outcome drafts the CQC notification and publishes `care.dols.outcome_known`; an hourly job raises each expiry reminder once (task, alert to the Registered Manager, `care.dols.expiring`); each review step goes through the workflow engine | Phase 1 (built) |
| Design | The web record's consent, capacity and DoLS tab shows each authorisation's status from the API as an icon and a word ("Ending soon", "Expired"; an expired one that a later granted authorisation names as the one it follows reads "Renewed" in a neutral tone, H-251); its start; its end as the last day in force when it ends at 00:00 UK time ("In force until the end of 20/10/2026", H-250), else the date and 24-hour time; the days until the last day in force, "Today is the last day in force" only on that day; the reminders and where the CQC notification stands, in the catalogue's words; and, for one granted before the June 2026 judgment and still in force with no review, "Review after the legal change: not started" as a warning with the next step (H-253). An unknown status, outcome, type or review state reads "could not be read" as a warning, never as a fact. The DoLS expiry alert's link (`/people/{id}/dols`) opens the tab | Phase 1 (slice 9, built; review round 1) |
| Design | On the web home page (INSI-001, `feat/web-dashboards`, docs/specs/insights.md): the "DoLS expiry reminders" tile counts open `care.dols_expiry` reminders on the round, overdue ones apart, and links to the round where each opens the person's DoLS register; none open is said neutrally ("None open"), never with the all-clear tick, and the tile says an authorisation already ended shows on the person's register, not here. A location-level register read (ending, expired, review awaiting) is a follow-up | Phase 1 |
| Design | A location's list of DoLS needing attention (`GET /v1/locations/{id}/dols`) brings together, for the people the reader may see, requests still waiting for an outcome (which have no end date and so no reminder), authorisations inside the first reminder window and authorisations past their end with nothing recorded since, ending soonest first. It feeds the role dashboards (INSI-001) | Phase 1 (API built 31/10/2026) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

## HZ-021 No access to essential information during an IT outage

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-021 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `operations` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 13.4 (nightly downtime pack); hazard-log candidate list; see also HZ-073 (information hidden by access scope rather than an outage) |

**Description**: during a power cut, network failure or Harbour outage, staff cannot see allergies, resuscitation status, IDDSI levels, care plan summaries, PEEPs or contacts.

**Causes**: no downtime pack; the pack is out of date; it is printed but not where staff can find it; no way to back-enter records made on paper. Added in slice 11, for the out-of-date pack: an allergy, resuscitation decision or IDDSI level changed after the pack was made; the nightly job failed, or a kiosk's cache was not refreshed; the outage lasts longer than the pack's life; an old printed pack is kept beside the new one; a kiosk builds the pack from a cache synced long ago; a person admitted, or a section recorded for the wrong person (HZ-001), after the last pack; resuscitation records that disagree; PEEPs and the MAR not yet held in Harbour. Added in the slice 11 review: a pack used during an outage that says nothing about what has changed since; a device clock behind the pack's time, so an old pack reads as current; food and drink levels merged so one hides the other; a stored value that cannot be read; the paper MAR or PEEPs kept somewhere other than the pack says; the kiosk's cached pack opened by someone who should not see every person at the location, or left on a withdrawn device. Added in #32 review round 1 (clinical M1): a stored pack is hidden from the location's holders once someone on it moves away (it follows every person it names), so during an outage the nurse in charge may find no pack to print until a new one is built (H-200). Added in round 5 of the Care mode review (C-L9): a care worker's access withdrawn, by mistake or by a role edit that lands mid-shift, removes the care record from their phone at once (allergies, resuscitation decisions, IDDSI levels and care plans; only unsent entries are kept), so that worker is without essential information until access is restored. Whether the phone should keep the care record for a while after access is withdrawn is a CSO decision (human action H-145); until then the downtime pack and colleagues' devices are the fallback.

**Effects**: wrong care given during the outage (an allergen given, resuscitation against a recorded decision, food or drink of an unsafe texture); records lost; evacuation without PEEPs.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | A downtime pack per location is generated nightly (allergies, resuscitation status, IDDSI levels, care plan summaries, PEEPs, contacts) and cached on kiosk devices; each page shows when it was generated | Phase 1 |
| Design | Every person receiving care at the location gets a page (on leave and in hospital included), never left out for an incomplete record; the safety-critical sections (identity, allergies, resuscitation, IDDSI food, IDDSI drink, communication, evacuation, medicines due) are never blank: missing ones read "not recorded" with what to do (`assembleDowntimePack` in `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.ts`; [operations.md](../specs/operations.md) rules 1 and 3) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11; served through the API, slice 11 part 3a-1) |
| Design | A stored pack is served only while nothing it was built from has changed since its information's time (the people, their status, room and identity, allergies, advance care records, eating and drinking, contacts, care plans, risks, photo documents and the places, soft deletes included, compared on the database's clock); otherwise it is built again on the spot, one build at a time per location. If that build fails, the latest pack is served with a header line saying it could not be rebuilt and when it was made, and the failure is audited (#45 review round 1, H1 and clinical M1; operations.md section 13) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 part 3a-1) |
| Design | Until PEEPs and the eMAR are in Harbour, the evacuation and medicines sections say where this location keeps the PEEPs and the paper MAR (location settings `downtime.mar_location` and `downtime.peep_location`; the domain port refuses an empty place, but every location is seeded with general wording, "the paper MAR chart" and "the person's PEEP in the fire file", which prints until the places are changed. Built in the schema (slice 11 part 1): only a holder of `platform.location.manage` at the location changes or confirms the places, and a confirmation is recorded (`downtime_places_confirmed_at`, cleared whenever a place changes without one). Built in part 3a-1: an unconfirmed place prints as not set ("where it is kept is not set in Harbour: ask the nurse in charge"); the task to confirm it comes with part 3b (whether the seeded wording may print before confirmation is H-201). With the eMAR, the medicines section lists doses still due and PRN last-given times (HZ-003) (`paperMarMedicinesPort`, `fireFileEvacuationPort`; operations.md rule 7) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11); data in Phase 2 and 3 |
| Design | Freshness on every page: each pack carries its generation time, the time its information was read and a "use until" time (`downtime_pack.valid_hours`, 36 hours so one failed nightly run still leaves a pack in date; CSO to confirm, H-101). Every page's header, worked out when printed or shown, says one of four things: current ("Information as at ... Use until ..."); out of date, in words, with what to check; during a declared outage, "Latest available pack: information as at X. Anything recorded since Y is on paper or not shown here: check the downtime forms and ask the nurse in charge about changes.", Y being the earlier of X and the outage's start (#45 review round 1, clinical M2); or that the device clock is behind the pack, so the time cannot be trusted. A kiosk-built pack counts from its last sync (`downtimePackFreshness`, `downtimePageHeader`; operations.md rule 2) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11; the API serves and prints it with this header, #45 and #46; kiosk with the mobile steps) |
| Design | A section from another person's record is withheld with words saying so, never printed (HZ-001); resuscitation records that disagree mark the section as conflicting and print every record in force (HZ-015); food and drink levels print separately and an unreadable value says to check the record (HZ-016) (operations.md rules 4 to 6) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | The kiosk's cached pack is encrypted under a key released only to a PIN session of a holder of `operations.downtime.read`; every print and open is queued as an audit event; it is evicted when the device key is revoked and when its retention ends (`kiosk-cache.ts`; operations.md rule 8; [kiosk.md](../compliance/kiosk.md) section 3.5) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11; device storage with the mobile step) |
| Design | Back-entry workflow for paper records made during downtime, flagged as late entries (HZ-017) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: business continuity plan says where the printed pack is kept and who prints it, and that a superseded printed pack is shredded when the new one arrives | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: until the eMAR and PEEPs are in Harbour, the paper MAR and the fire file are kept current and where the location settings say | Phase 1 |
| Design | On the web (slice 11 web): the location's downtime page opens the pack (its freshness as an icon and words, the API's notice, the header lines every printed page carries, when it was made and how many pages and warnings it has) and prints it as a PDF streamed from the API and never stored by the web app (`no-store`, a download); both need a fresh sign-in and count towards the hourly limit, so the pack is never prefetched; over the limit, or when no pack can be built, the page says what to use instead (the last printed pack and the nurse in charge) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Residual risk while the MAR and PEEPs are pointers only**: until Phase 2 (eMAR) and Phase 3 (PEEPs), the pack cannot show medicines due or evacuation needs; it can only say where the paper copies are. The risk then rests on the paper MAR and the fire file being current, which is a deployer control. The CSO decides whether that residual risk is acceptable for go-live or needs a further control (H-102). `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-021 the downtime pack has a page with every section for every person receiving care at the location` (people away included, discharged, deceased and other locations left out, incomplete records never left out, room order) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-021 missing safety-critical information reads not recorded, never blank` (photo, allergies, resuscitation, IDDSI food and drink, communication) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-021 every page says when its information is from and whether it is out of date` (`says out of date in words at the moment it expires` and later; `during a declared outage says it is the latest available and that anything since is on paper`; `flags a device clock behind the pack instead of calling it current`; `marks a pack built on a kiosk from an old sync as out of date from the start`) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `keeps a pack in date for longer than the nightly interval, so one failed night still leaves a pack`; `points to the location's own MAR and PEEP places until medicines and PEEPs are in Harbour`; `refuses a placeholder with no place set` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `says an ADRT is held, and never not recorded, with ...` (an ADRT alone, beside a DNACPR, withdrawn); `hazard HZ-009 says BST or GMT beside a time on the day the clocks change`; `apps/api/test/downtime.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-001 names on every page's footer the person whose content is on it, a continuation page included` (and the identity instruction on each person's page while photos are not printed) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 part 3a-2) |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-001 information from another person's record is withheld, never printed`; `hazard HZ-015 resuscitation status on the downtime pack` (conflicting section listing every record in force); `hazard HZ-016 an unreadable IDDSI code says check the record and never stops the pack` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/operations/kiosk-cache.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-021 the kiosk opens its cached pack only in a PIN session of a downtime reader`; `hazard HZ-021 the kiosk evicts its cached pack on revocation and at the end of retention`; `hazard HZ-021 the kiosk queues an audit event for every print and open` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/db/test/downtime.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-011 hazard HZ-021 a downtime pack follows its location and its people` (the people taken from the pack's content whatever the caller says; append-only for the application and the owner, no delete or truncate); `hazard HZ-021 seeds every location with the default places, unconfirmed, and refuses a blank one` (`downtime.mar_location`, `downtime.peep_location`: changed and confirmed only by a holder of `platform.location.manage`, in their own name; a changed place unconfirmed again) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 schema) |

| `apps/api/test/downtime.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-021 the downtime pack contains every required section for every current resident`; `prints the header worked out now on every page, and each person's page names them` (part 3a-2); `hazard HZ-001 names on every page's footer the person whose content is on it, a continuation page included`; `hazard HZ-015 prints that an ADRT is held beside the CPR decision`; `refuses HEAD on the pack`; `builds the pack again when someone's status or the places change`; `is only for holders of operations.downtime.read where the location is`; `builds once when two people read at the same time, and reuses the pack when asked to build`; `serves the latest pack, saying it could not be built again, when a build fails`; `needs a fresh sign-in to build on request, but not to read`; `limits how often a member opens packs`; `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `during an outage that ... says paper began at the earlier time` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 part 3a) |
| `apps/api/test/downtime.test.ts` (#47 review round 1): `asks the Registered Manager to act when a location is left with no pack in date`; `keeps a failed rebuild from holding back the event, and logs it`; the rebuild after a bed move through the real route in `hazard HZ-021 rebuilds the pack when someone on the latest one has moved`; the places event in `builds the pack again when someone's status or the places change`; `packages/db/test/downtime.test.ts`: `keeps a rebuild the system's and an on-demand pack a member's, and never names a document (migration 0055)` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 part 3b) |

| `apps/mobile` Maestro `hazard-HZ-021-kiosk-downtime-pack.yaml`: print the cached pack offline with the freshness words | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `apps/web/src/lib/downtime.test.ts`: `the downtime pack's freshness (HZ-021)` (each state in an icon and words, an unknown state warns; a refused print goes to sign in again or back with what happened); `apps/web/e2e/downtime.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-021 the manager opens the pack with its freshness in words and prints it, never kept` (step-up with a passkey, axe, the PDF as an attachment with `no-store`) | Passing (unit; web journeys with axe in CI on every pull request and on `main`; run 38938760784 on `d77b416`) (Phase 1, slice 11 web) |
