## HZ-022 A person's information shown to a family member or professional without a sharing decision

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-022 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]`; data protection lead consulted |
| Engineering owner | `people` module (sharing rules); `comms` module (family feed, Phase 1 slice 12; family app mode, Phase 4) |
| Source | Master prompt 9.2 (family and professional sharing), 15.1 (sections stay closed until a sharing decision exists); [Caldicott principles](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/the-caldicott-principles) (principle 7); Phase 1 plan candidate hazard ("family feed showing a section without a sharing decision") |

**Description**: a section of a person's record (updates, photos, care plan, health summary, medicines, finance, About Me) is shown to a family member, representative, external professional or share-link holder when no sharing decision in force covers that section for that recipient: there was never a decision, it has expired, consent was withdrawn, an attorney's authority has lapsed, sharing was suspended during a safeguarding enquiry, or the person has died or left the service.

**Causes**: a feed that is open by default; a decision that covers "the person" rather than named sections; decisions with no expiry; a withdrawal or suspension not applied at once (including on devices that synced earlier); a decision for one recipient applied to another (contact ids confused, a shared family account); a lasting power of attorney or deputyship accepted without evidence or after it ended, or a health and welfare attorney treated as having authority while the person has capacity; share links that outlive their decision; sharing left open after a death. In the family feed (slice 12): an update composed for one person shown in another person's feed; updates from before a decision started, or from a gap after a withdrawal, shown once a later decision exists; an update taken down still shown to people who never saw it; a property and financial affairs attorney shown care updates, or a welfare attorney shown finance; a photo shared without consent or a best-interests decision, or one in which other residents can be recognised; another resident named, or a phone or NHS number written, in a shared note or caption; a visit booking refusal that reveals a hospital stay, a safeguarding suspension or a restriction; a staff role holding the family feed permission. Found in review: an update from a suspension period shown once the suspension is lifted; a welfare attorney's or representative's decision still honoured after the person regains capacity; a deputy acting outside the sections their order covers; an appointee shown finance statements; another resident's name written in lower case, with a zero-width space or with lookalike Cyrillic or Greek letters; a phone number written with brackets or points, or an email address with a full-width at sign; a photo attached to another kind of update, or a care plan update with no section; a family member given a person assignment, which would open every staff table through `app.person_visible`; a portal permission in a staff role or grant, or a staff permission in a portal session.

**Effects**: a person's whereabouts, health or money disclosed to someone who may harm them (safeguarding risk, financial abuse); a person with capacity overridden; distress and loss of trust; a reportable personal data breach; a family member relying on information they should no longer see.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Closed by default: every section is hidden from every family member, professional and share link until a sharing decision in force covers that section (and, for the care plan, that domain) for that recipient (`checkSectionVisibility` in `packages/domain/src/people/sharing.ts`) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (family feed in slice 12) |
| Design | Every decision records its basis and is checked: no consent from a person assessed as lacking capacity for it; an authority needs evidence, must cover the section, and a welfare authority applies only when the person lacks capacity; a best-interests decision must be recorded; direct care only for professionals and clinical sections | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Every decision expires; withdrawal, suspension (for example during a safeguarding enquiry) and the end of an authority close the section at once; suspensions are stored as periods (`isSharingSuspendedAt`); a stored decision that breaks the rules is never honoured | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Sharing closes when the person is discharged, dies or is archived; correcting a discharge or death does not reopen it without a manager | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | About Me share links carry About Me only, store only a hash of the token, and cannot outlive their decision | Phase 1 (links issued and revoked in slice 8; the link viewer with the portal) |
| Design | Every view by a family member, professional or link holder is audited with the decision that allowed it (`buildFamilyFeed` names the decision for each entry; `GET /v1/family/feed` writes one `feed_view` per entry and a `comms.feed.viewed` audit event with ids only) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 12, part 1) |
| Design | The family feed adds nothing to the sharing rules: each update belongs to one section (`FEED_ITEM_SECTIONS`) and is shown only when `checkSectionVisibility` finds a decision in force for that person, section, care plan domain and family contact; an update about another person is never shown; only decisions in force and not suspended when the thing happened count (`decisionInForceAt`), so earlier updates, gaps after a withdrawal and suspension periods stay closed even after a suspension is lifted; a care plan update with no section, and a photo on another kind of update unless the photos section is open and the photo basis holds, are hidden (`checkFeedItemVisibility` in `packages/domain/src/comms/feed-visibility.ts`); the schema step adds CHECK constraints for both | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Attorneys, deputies and appointees see only what their authority covers through the same rules: welfare authorities, a representative's consent and a best-interests decision only while the current capacity finding says the person lacks capacity for the decision (presumed otherwise, `capacity_regained`); deputies only within the sections their order covers (`orderSections`); property and finance authorities the finance placeholder; appointees the benefits section only; a decision naming a section outside the authority is never honoured (`packages/domain/src/people/sharing.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Photos need their own basis (`feedPhotoBasis`): the person's consent or a representative's with authority, or a best-interests decision after an assessment of the photos decision; a refusal or an overdue review stops them; only photos where nobody else can be recognised are composed, and photo updates are taken down when the basis ends | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Composition normalises staff-written words first (Unicode NFKC, invisible format and direction characters removed, lookalike Cyrillic and Greek letters folded) and refuses words naming another resident (full names with given, preferred or known-as first names, and surnames, in any case), an email address (including a full-width at sign) or a run of ten or more digits across spaces, points, hyphens or brackets; it strips staff surnames in any case when the tenant asks; visitors are never named; withdrawn updates disappear for anyone who had not seen them (`composeFeedItem`, `checkFeedText` in `feed-compose.ts`, `feed-text.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | What a family member saw cannot be unseen: `feedExposure` lists what was seen under a decision and flags any view after it closed as a breach candidate | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | A visit booking refusal about the person, a sharing decision, a restriction or a location restriction (an outbreak) always reads "We cannot book this visit online. Please call the home to arrange it."; the decision is checked before the person's status, and the refusal code and restriction id are typed staff-only (`checkVisitBooking`); confirmed bookings are re-checked when the person's status, a decision or a restriction changes (`visitBookingsToReview`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Family-only permissions (`PORTAL_ONLY_PERMISSIONS`) are never given to staff roles; a role that mixes portal and staff permissions, or a grant carrying a portal permission, is refused (`checkRoleDefinition`, `checkGrantPermissions`) and ignored if stored; a portal session is resolved from portal roles only, with no grants and no person scope, and a staff session never carries a portal permission (`resolveAccess` in `packages/domain/src/platform/access.ts`); reading the feed needs MFA (`comms.feed.read` touches care records) | Phase 1 (built, slice 12) |
| Design | Database: family members get no `person_assignment`, so `app.person_visible` is false for every person in a family session; feed tables carry a restrictive policy calling `app.family_can_see(person_id, section, care_plan_domain, occurred_at)`, which mirrors the domain rule; family-owned rows are keyed on the caller's contacts (ADR-0061, [comms.md](../specs/comms.md) section 2.1). Built: a family session is resolved from `family_portal_user` links (portal roles only, scope `none`, `app.family`); `app.family_can_see` is a definer function owned by `harbour_family_reader` with a fixed search path; an update taken down shows only to a viewer who saw it; feed rows are append-only; no portal account can hold a person assignment, either way round | Built in Phase 1 (migration 0061) |
| Design | Family device sync streams carry only the sections a decision covers, tested on their own (replication bypasses RLS) | Phase 4 (family app mode) |
| Design | The sharing API applies the slice 12 rules: each check passes the suspension periods (`sharing_decision_suspension`, migration 0025; lifting one keeps what happened during it closed) and the current capacity finding for the sharing decision; a capacity finding on a new decision must name an assessment of the sharing decision; an authority must be held by the contact named, a deputy within their deputyship (`contact.authority_scope`) and the sections their order covers, an appointee benefits only | Built in Phase 1 (people lifecycle API, after slice 12) |
| Design | The web sharing tab (PEOP-013) shows each decision's state in words and an icon, taking "in force" only from the API (a decision closed for a reason its dates do not show reads "Not in force", never "In force"); suspending, lifting and withdrawing need a reason and the version loaded; the check for one recipient lists every section open or closed now with the reason, closed by default. The About me tab makes a share link only under a decision for a share link in force, with a fresh sign-in, never past the decision's end, and shows the code once | Built in Phase 1 (PEOP-003 and PEOP-013 web) |
| Design | A change to the contact who holds a decision's authority that takes it away (no longer that kind of authority, a deputy's order no longer of that kind, the last reference or evidence removed, or the authority now ending before the decision recorded) suspends every decision not yet ended that rests on it, in the same transaction, audited with the reason `authority_changed` and publishing `people.sharing.changed`, so the family feed and share links close at once; a manager reviews it on the sharing tab and lifts or withdraws it (`authorityChangeEffects` in `packages/domain/src/people/sharing.ts`; `PATCH /v1/people/{id}/contacts/{contactId}`). A decision already suspended (for example during a safeguarding enquiry) has the change audited, and lifting any suspension checks the contact still holds the authority as recorded (`authorityHeldNow`), refusing and auditing `authority_changed` if not (#119 review round 2). Changing the email address a family member's portal account is matched by needs `people.sharing.manage` and a fresh sign-in (#119 review M3 and L1) | Built in Phase 1 (PEOP-001 review round 1) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the Registered Manager reviews sharing decisions at each care plan review and suspends sharing when a safeguarding concern involves a family member; staff check a photo shows only the person before choosing it for the feed | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |

## HZ-023 Essential information missing from the hospital transfer pack

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-023 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `people` module (lead engineer); `medicines` module from Phase 2 (reconciliation on return) |
| Source | Master prompt 9.2 (hospital transfer: red bag content generated in one click; return reconciliation); NHS England hospital transfer pathway ("red bag"); [NICE SC1](https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/sc1) (medicines when people transfer between care settings) |

**Description**: a person goes to hospital without their photo, allergies, current medicines, communication needs, resuscitation status or About Me; with a pack that belongs to someone else, is out of date, or shows a missing item as blank or as "none"; without their glasses, hearing aids or dentures; or comes back without the home picking up what the hospital changed (new allergies, a DNACPR decision, a new IDDSI level, changed medicines).

**Causes**: a pack put together by hand under time pressure; sections never recorded; "unknown" printed as blank or "none"; a withdrawn DNACPR printed; another person's data in the pack (HZ-001); stale offline data (HZ-002); belongings not sent; the hospital discharge letter not reconciled on return; names and clinical words printed with characters missing because the PDF's fonts cannot print them (slice 8 review, clinical finding M4); a medicines list from before admission printed as if it were current; return checks raised with no owner or due time; a photo counted as printable that the PDF cannot decode (a 16-bit or interlaced PNG, a truncated or oversized file), or a crafted image that stops the service printing any pack (slice 8 review round 3, security finding M1 and clinical finding L10); the home or hospital name printed with characters missing and not named (round 3, clinical finding L10); return checks recorded by someone who may not complete them (round 3, security finding M2). A crafted photo decoded on every preview, creation and print, inside a database transaction, holds the API's CPU and connections (round 4, security finding S2).

**Effects**: a medicine given that the person is allergic to; time-critical medicines (for example for Parkinson's disease) missed; resuscitation contrary to the person's wishes; the person unable to communicate pain or needs; harm after return when hospital changes are missed.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | One function assembles the pack from the record (`assembleHospitalTransferPack` in `packages/domain/src/people/transfer-pack.ts`); it refuses data from another person's record and an incomplete identity (HZ-001) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Photo, allergies, medicines, communication needs, resuscitation status and About Me are safety-critical: when missing they read "not recorded" on the first page, never blank; "none" must be recorded positively with who and when | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Only active resuscitation records print (HZ-015); allergies print most severe first (HZ-007); time-critical medicines first; every section shows when it was last updated; a pack printed offline shows the last sync time (HZ-002) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (PDF: ADR-0040) |
| Design | The pack is never blocked, because a transfer must not wait, but recording the hand-over of an incomplete pack needs a named acknowledgement and a reason | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Glasses, hearing aids, dentures and walking aids in the property inventory are offered for sending; items left behind are listed; items sent are checked back in on return | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Return checks: discharge letter, medicines (HZ-005), new allergies, resuscitation decisions made in hospital, IDDSI changes (HZ-016), skin | Phase 1 (checks), Phase 2 (eMAR reconciliation) |
| Design | Each return check is a task for the nurse in charge, due `people.return_checks_due_hours` after the return (never in the past for a backdated return), urgent where it carries a hazard, and escalated by the task engine when overdue. The owner, the due time and the escalation are for the CSO (H-85) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | The printed pack embeds Noto Sans, so names and clinical words print as written; a section with a character the font lacks is named on the first page; a photo held but not printable (anything but JPEG or PNG) is named on the first page ([ADR-0040](../decisions/0040-transfer-pack-pdf-writer.md)) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Whether a photo prints is decided once, with the decoder the PDF uses, when it is uploaded (before the upload's transaction) and stored on the document (`image_printable`); an older photo is decided once, outside any transaction, before a pack route opens its own. Only 8-bit, non-interlaced PNGs and 8-bit Huffman-coded grey or colour JPEGs up to 2048 pixels a side print; a PNG may have at most 1024 image chunks and 8 MB of compressed data, its data must inflate to exactly the size its header promises with valid row filters and palette indices, and decoding never throws. The PDF reads and decodes the photo after its transaction ends, and the pack routes allow 30 requests a user a minute, counted in Postgres so the limit holds across API instances (`request_quota`; [care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 7). Anything else counts as missing: page 1 names it and the hand-over needs the acknowledgement. The home and hospital names are checked for characters the font lacks, as the person's name and the sections are ([ADR-0040](../decisions/0040-transfer-pack-pdf-writer.md)) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 (review round 3) |
| Design | Until the eMAR (Phase 2) the pack's medicines come from the pre-admission assessment and are marked on the first page as needing checking against the MAR; an empty list reads "not recorded since admission". Whether that list may be printed at all is for the CSO (H-82) | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | The pre-admission assessment and the welcome checklist require allergies, medicines, communication needs and resuscitation status at admission, so the pack rarely lacks them | Built in Phase 1 slice 8 |
| Design | Go-live waits until every current person has a communication-needs status recorded ([platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rule 26) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the transfer procedure names who prints the pack and checks the red bag, agreed with the local hospital trust | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**. Further reduction is impractical: blocking or delaying a transfer until the record is complete would cause greater harm, and the remaining likelihood depends on the provider completing records, which the controls make visible but cannot force. `[CSO decision - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/people/transfer-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 missing safety-critical information is flagged, never left blank` (each of the six sections; empty lists; a withdrawn-only resuscitation record; positive "none" statements accepted) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/people/transfer-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 needs a named acknowledgement and reason when critical sections are missing` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/people/transfer-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-001 refuses a pack whose sections belong to someone else`, `hazard HZ-015 prints only the active resuscitation record`, `hazard HZ-007 lists the most severe allergies first`, `hazard HZ-016 re-checks allergies, resuscitation decisions and IDDSI levels on return` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 prints not recorded on the first page for each missing critical section` (reads the printed PDF's first page through the font's character map; identity on every page) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/pdf.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 text prints as written`, `hazard HZ-023 the printed transfer pack` (first page names what is missing and what needs checking; characters the font cannot print named) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 marks a medicines list from the pre-admission assessment as needing checking against the MAR`, `hazards HZ-003 and HZ-023 record the return: rounds resume, the checks come up as tasks with an owner and a due time, and the care plan review comes forward` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/mobile` Maestro `hazard-HZ-023-offline-transfer-pack.yaml`: print the pack offline with the last sync time shown | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 previews the pack with every missing safety-critical section flagged`, `hazard HZ-023 previews with the reason and the items to send in the body, never the URL`, `hazard HZ-023 records the hand-over of an incomplete pack only with a named acknowledgement`, `hazard HZ-023 refuses a pack for someone not receiving care`, `hazard HZ-023 communication needs: an empty list is not 'none'` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/people.test.ts`: `communicationSummary (HZ-023)` (the people list and the person header show "Not recorded" as a warning with an icon and words, and "None identified" only for a positive none) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/pdf.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 treats ... as not printable, without decoding it` (a 16-bit PNG, an interlaced PNG, one claiming 20000 x 20000 pixels, one over the pixel limit or 2048 a side, a truncated one, a chunk running past the end, image data that inflates past or stops short of its header, an unknown row filter, palette indices past the palette, no palette, too many image chunks, too much compressed data), `hazard HZ-023 treats a JPEG over the pixel limit, or truncated, as not printable`, `hazard HZ-023 prints a grey JPEG as grey and a colour one as colour, and refuses CMYK and 12-bit JPEGs`, `names the home or the hospital on page 1 when their names have characters it cannot print`; `apps/api/test/rate-limit.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 lets a user make a few packs a minute and asks the rest to wait, without stopping others` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/people-lifecycle.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-001 and HZ-023 count ... photo as missing, so page 1 names it and the hand-over needs an acknowledgement` (16-bit and truncated PNGs), `hazard HZ-023 never sets a return check due in the past for a return recorded more than a day late` (a return with no transfer record still raises the checks) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/onboarding.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 communication needs not recorded` (person readiness) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/rate-limit.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 lets a user make a few packs a minute and asks the rest to wait, without stopping others`; `apps/api/test/device-integrity.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 counts a member's requests across every API instance, in request_quota` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/web/e2e/transfer-pack.spec.ts` (Playwright with axe, light and dark): `hazards HZ-001 and HZ-023 the pack as it would be made now names the person and shows what is missing` (missing safety-critical sections first, as "Not recorded" with an icon), `hazard HZ-023 sends the person with the pack in one form, prints it and records an incomplete hand-over only with a reason` (only the hospital typed; the PDF downloads as a PDF; the hand-over refused without a reason and recorded with one), `hazard HZ-023 records the return, sets the checks on return and keeps the pack to print again`; `apps/web/src/lib/transfer-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-023 lists missing safety-critical sections first, never leaving one out`, `hazard HZ-023 for ...` (the web asks for the acknowledgement exactly when `checkTransferPackHandover` would refuse without one), `hazards HZ-023 and HZ-001 with ...: offered ...` (`sendOffered`: a preview that failed for a rate limit or a fault never hides the send form; only the API's refusal or a pack for someone else does) | Passing (local run; CI e2e-web on the PR) |

## HZ-024 Care plan or risk assessment out of date, or a draft or superseded version followed

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-024 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (care planning, risk assessments), 9.2 (first plan within 7 working days); [care.md](../specs/care.md) rules 1 and 2; Reg 9 and Reg 12 |

**Description**: staff follow a care plan or risk assessment that no longer matches the person's needs, or a draft or superseded version, because the first plan was never written, a review was missed, or a change in needs did not prompt a review.

**Causes**: no first plan after admission; review dates missed; a new screening band, a fall or a new resuscitation decision does not prompt a review; a draft or superseded version shown or used as current; two active plans; tasks generated from a draft; risk assessment reviews missed; rounds that keep the schedule of a version since changed, or rounds made twice when a new version is published, or a round's words taken from an earlier version (slice 5 build); an overdue round of a schedule the plan has since changed stays open showing the earlier plan's words as if current, and a plan change cancels a round someone is part way through (slice 5 review round 2, C-M2); in a record's history, the change the latest review made is left unmarked, so staff miss what changed, or an earlier version (its status shown as "In use", or a question reworded since) is read as the record in use now (fix/care-history-latest-change, clinical safety review round 1).

**Effects**: care against needs that have changed (moving and handling, nutrition, falls, skin), leading to falls, pressure ulcers or malnutrition; a regulatory breach of Regs 9 and 12.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | One active plan and one open draft per person (database partial unique indexes); only an active plan's actions drive tasks; publishing supersedes the previous version and both stay readable with their history | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | The first plan is due within `care_plan.initial_working_days` working days of admission, skipping the nation's bank holidays; plans are reviewed every `care_plan.review_interval_months` months; the plan list shows what is due and whether it is overdue | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | A changed screening band, a fall or a new advance care record brings the review forward to that day and raises the review task at once | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | An hourly job raises one task and one alert to the nurse in charge for each overdue first plan, plan review, risk assessment review and advance care record review, and never two for the same record (HZ-008) | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | Draft and superseded versions are labelled with word and icon, with the active version one tap away | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web: "Draft, not in use" with a pencil, "Superseded, not in use" with a clock turned back, "In use" with a tick; a draft or superseded version opens under a notice with a link to the plan in use) |
| Design | The downtime pack summarises the active care plan only, need by need in the plan's domain order, never a draft or superseded version ([operations.md](../specs/operations.md) rule 1) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the provider's care planning policy names who reviews plans and how changes in need are reported | Phase 1 |
| Design | Only the active plan's task-generating actions drive rounds, one task series each (`planActionTaskSeries`, care.md rule 14): publishing a version and an hourly sweep keep what runs, carry an unchanged action's series to the new version, start what is new and end the rest, cancelling their rounds from then that nobody has started with the reason (a round in progress stays to be finished); one live series per action (a unique index); a care plan series changes only through the plan (the series end route refuses it); the round view shows each round's action as its plan words it with the plan's status, and a round whose action is no longer in the active plan (an overdue one left open to be done) says "From an earlier plan: check the current plan" (`planRoundNotice`) | Built in Phase 1 slice 5 (earlier-plan notice in review round 2, C-M2) |
| Design | On the phone (slice 10 part 2) a round task carries `from_earlier_plan`, the round view's "From an earlier plan: check the current plan" kept on the task by the database (migration 0071): the same rule as the round view (`planRoundNotice`: the plan is not active, or the series no longer runs), worked out in the organisation's view whenever an open task is written, its series changes, or its plan's status changes (at the end of that transaction, so a version published with its rounds carried forward changes none). The API never writes it. The round screen says it with the task (STATUS screens gate item (b); H-303) | Built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2 (data; the screen to follow) |
| Design | On the web home page (INSI-001, `feat/web-dashboards`, docs/specs/insights.md): the reviews tile counts due and overdue reviews from each location's tracker, overdue apart, and leaves out paused ones (the person in hospital); a state the web does not know counts as overdue. Gap: the location tracker does not say when a nation has no review figures, so the tile then reads none due (as `/people/reviews` does); logged for the API | Phase 1 |
| Design | A record's history (web, `/people/[id]/history/[table]/[recordId]` and the personal details on the history tab; the same list shows advance care records, so it also serves [HZ-015](#hz-015-resuscitation-decision-missing-out-of-date-or-not-visible), and it is where record history investigations start, [HZ-017](#hz-017-late-or-retrospective-entry-mistaken-for-a-contemporaneous-record)): every earlier version is labelled "Earlier version: not in use now" with an icon, its status is shown as "Status at the time", and a link opens the record as it is now. Each field an older version had that the next version changed is marked "Changed in the next version"; the newest earlier version is marked "Changed since this version" from the field keys the API says the latest change altered (`latestChanged`, keys only, never the current values; none when the record was saved again while it was read, or a column has since gone). Risk answers show as the template's questions with yes or no only when given under the template version Harbour has now, otherwise as the stored key with "asked in template version …"; MHRA findings as one sentence each; the template by its title and the bands in words; the `status` of a risk assessment, care plan, advance care record and person in words. Still shown as stored: consent and DoLS outcomes, a consent's capacity status, likelihood and severity (as numbers), and other coded fields (2026-10-31 fix and review round 1) | Built in Phase 1 (fix/care-history-latest-change) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

## HZ-025 Bed rail entrapment risk not identified

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-025 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.1 (bed rails, MHRA gaps); [MHRA: bed rails management and safe use](https://www.gov.uk/guidance/bed-rails-management-and-safe-use); [rates.md](../specs/rates.md) `bed_rail.*` |

**Description**: bed rails are used where the gaps between the bars, to the mattress or to the headboard, or the rail height, are outside the MHRA figures, or for a person at risk of entrapment, and the record does not show it.

**Causes**: measurements not recorded; limits held as literals and out of date; "under" read as "at most"; the smaller between-bars figure for a small or atypical body size not applied; a mattress or rail changed without measuring again; the review missed.

**Effects**: entrapment of the head, neck or chest; asphyxia; death.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The bed rails template records each MHRA measurement and checks it against the rate-table figure: gaps strictly under the figure, the rail height at least the figure, and the smaller between-bars figure when the person has a small or atypical body size; findings outside the figures are stored and shown with the assessment | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | The figures live only in the rate table with their source and verification status | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | Risk assessment reviews raise tasks when due (HZ-024) | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: measure at fitting and whenever the bed, mattress or rail changes; the MHRA guidance is cited on the template | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/care/risk-assessment.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-025 bed rails against the MHRA figures in the rate table` | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/care-planning.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-025 lists the templates with the MHRA figures from the rate table` | Passing |

## HZ-026 Restrictive or covert care under consent or authority that is not valid

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-026 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 10.4; [Mental Capacity Act 2005](https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/2005/9) sections 1 to 4, 9, 11, 20 and 37 to 39; [NICE SC1](https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/sc1) 1.15 (covert administration); care.md section 4 rules 9 and 10 |

**Description**: bed rails, covert medicines or other restrictive care are given on the strength of a consent, capacity assessment or best-interests decision that does not meet the Mental Capacity Act: consent from someone without authority (a relative, a property and financial attorney, an unregistered attorney, a deputy outside their order), the person's own consent recorded while they lacked capacity, a "lacks capacity" outcome the two-stage test does not support, an assessment of "everything" rather than one decision, or an incomplete best-interests checklist.

**Causes**: next of kin treated as decision makers; the two kinds of lasting power of attorney confused; capacity assessed once and reused for other decisions; no practicable steps recorded; consent relied on after it expired or was withdrawn, or after the person regained capacity; no IMCA where nobody could be consulted.

**Effects**: covert medication or bed rails without lawful authority (entrapment, medicines given in an unsafe form, distress); the person's own decision overridden; unlawful restriction; regulatory action.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Consent is checked by representative type: only a registered health and welfare attorney or a personal welfare deputy within their order may consent for a person who lacks capacity for the decision; relatives, appointees, IMCAs and advocates are refused with the reason; marketing and AI processing only from the person (`consent.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Bed rails need the restraint conditions (MCA s.11 and s.20); covert medicines need a lack of capacity and a best-interests decision (NICE SC1 1.15) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | `consentStatus` decides at the time of use: expired, withdrawn, refused or superseded consent, and a representative's consent once the person has capacity, are not valid | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The API finds the person's capacity for the decision from their assessments (`capacityFinding`), never from the request, refuses with the domain's words whatever `checkConsentRecord`, `checkCapacityAssessment` and `checkBestInterests` refuse, and shows each decision's status now | Phase 1 (built) |
| Design | Capacity assessments are decision- and time-specific; "lacks capacity" only when stage 1 is met, a stage 2 element is not and the inability is because of the impairment, with practicable steps recorded (`capacity.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The best-interests checklist is complete only when every item is answered, with reasons for "not applicable", a consultation or the reason there was none, and the IMCA prompt (`best-interests.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The web record's consent, capacity and DoLS tab shows, for each decision, whether a valid consent is in place now as the API found it: "Consent in place"; "Refused" as a warning, saying who refused (H-252); "No consent recorded" as a warning with the next step; or "No valid consent" (the stronger warning) with every reason in the catalogue's words; never an unknown status as in place. Beside it, the capacity found for the decision with the date of the assessment ("Assessed on 01/10/2026 as lacking capacity for this decision"), its review date or "No review date set", and an overdue capacity review | Phase 1 (slice 9, built; review round 1) |
| Design | The same tab shows each capacity assessment as a finding with its date, the two-stage answers and evidence, the practicable steps and the finding worded from the two stages; an older assessment of the same decision says a later one replaces its finding and has no overdue review (the domain's `capacityFinding`, H-254). Each best-interests decision shows what its assessment found and warns when that assessment was corrected or the finding now in force is not a lack of capacity (H-255), with the IMCA prompt as a warning. Each consent record says whether it decides its decision now or was replaced, withdrawn or expired (a deciding record withdrawn or expired warns), a refusal as a warning, with a representative's authority (registration, exclusions, the deputy's order and its end, restraint expressly allowed), the restraint conditions and the capacity it was made under with the assessment's date. Corrections show why and strike through the versions they replaced; unknown values warn | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 2, built) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | User guide on consent, capacity and representatives with the Act's wording. Deployer control: the tenant's Mental Capacity Act policy and training | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-026 consent authority by representative type` (`consent.test.ts`) | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-026 whether a valid consent exists at a time` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-026 capacity: lacks capacity only when stage 1 is met and a stage 2 element is not` (`capacity.test.ts`) | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-026 capacity is assumed unless an assessment of that decision says otherwise` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-026 best-interests checklist is complete only when every item is answered` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (care): `hazard HZ-026 IMCA prompt when nobody appropriate can be consulted (MCA s.37 to s.39)` | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/mca.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-026 a consent without authority is refused` (a relative, an attorney while capacity is assumed, a property and financial attorney) | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/mca.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-026 lets a registered welfare attorney agree to bed rails, with the restraint conditions, once the person lacks capacity` (capacity found from the assessments, never the request) | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/mca.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-026 needs a complete best-interests decision before covert medicines`, `prompts for an IMCA when nobody appropriate can be consulted about a move` and `records a withdrawal, keeps the record` | Passing |
| `packages/db/test/mca-and-incidents.test.ts`: append-only capacity assessments and best-interests decisions; a lacks-capacity outcome the two-stage test does not support and a consent claiming an assessment it does not name are refused by the database | Passing |
| `apps/web/src/lib/mca.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-026 shows ... with its reason in the catalogue's words` (each consent status), `says who refused, and never shows an unknown status as consent in place` and `shows the date of the capacity finding, its review date and an overdue review`; `apps/web/e2e/capacity.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-020 hazard HZ-026 the tab shows consent by decision and the DoLS register, every warning state in words` | Passing (slice 9) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/mca.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-026 says where ... stands` (each consent record), `shows a refused consent record as a warning, and its capacity with the assessment's date`, `shows the outcome in words, as a finding rather than an alarm`, `says a later assessment of the same decision replaces an older finding`, `warns when a best-interests decision follows a corrected or overtaken assessment` and `words the finding from the two stages, in the catalogue, never the API's English`; `apps/web/e2e/capacity.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-026 the tab shows the two-stage test, the best-interests checklist and each consent record, with corrections` (both representatives' authority, the restraint conditions, the corrected best-interests decision, the IMCA notice in the warning tone, the replaced finding) | Passing (slice 9, part 2) |

## HZ-027 Head injury after a fall not observed or escalated

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-027 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `safety` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 11.1 (falls protocol); [NICE NG232](https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/ng232/chapter/Recommendations) 1.2.4 and 1.9.11; safety.md section 4 rule 12 |

**Description**: after a fall, a person with a head injury, or on anticoagulant or antiplatelet treatment, is not observed closely enough or urgent medical advice is not sought, so an intracranial bleed is missed.

**Causes**: a head injury not recognised (an unwitnessed fall); anticoagulant status not known at the point of care; no observation schedule created, or one not followed; tasks lost at handover (HZ-018); offline capture delays the tasks (HZ-006); the observation defaults not suited to the setting.

**Effects**: delayed treatment of an intracranial haemorrhage; death or permanent disability.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

| Design | Go-live waits until a working post-fall observation escalation chain exists and the clinical lead has reviewed the post-fall observation settings (`falls.*`) apart from the other defaults (onboarding items `escalation_chains_set` and `clinical_settings_reviewed`, [platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rule 26) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Every fall creates the post-fall checklist; a head injury or anticoagulant or antiplatelet treatment, or either not ruled out, prompts urgent medical advice under the tenant's post-fall policy and creates timed observation tasks (`packages/domain/src/safety/post-fall.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Observation intervals and the monitoring period come from the rate table (`falls.*`), defaulting to NICE NG232 1.9.11, to be confirmed by the CSO (human action H-66); observation times are elapsed time, so a clock change does not move them | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The API creates the checklist through the task engine whenever a fall is recorded or synced, in every nation, with one task per timed observation counted from when the person was found; an answer that rules out both risks cancels the timed observations and starts general monitoring; overdue tasks escalate (HZ-008) | Phase 1 (built) |
| Design | The tasks are created on the device when a fall is recorded offline, before it syncs | Phase 1 (offline slice) |
| Design | The web form asks the falls questions (head injury, anticoagulant or antiplatelet treatment) when a fall is recorded, each needing an answer, "not sure yet" included, so the checklist starts from an answer, never a default. The form has no "when found" time yet, so observations count from when it happened (H-262) | Phase 1 (slice 9) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the tenant's post-fall policy and escalation route linked from the task; staff trained in post-fall observations | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Justification for level 3**: Harbour prompts, schedules and escalates, but recognising deterioration and seeking advice are clinical actions by the staff on shift; further reduction would mean automating a clinical judgement, which the medical-device boundary rules out. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain` (safety): `hazard HZ-027 head injury or anticoagulants shorten the observation schedule` (`post-fall.test.ts`) | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (safety): `hazard HZ-013 open questions, provisional drafts and tasks` (a fall on anticoagulants raises urgent advice and timed observations) | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/incidents.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-027 a fall creates the post-fall tasks with the observation schedule, shortened for a head injury` (one task per timed observation from the rate table) | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/incidents.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-027 ruling out both risks replaces the timed observations with general monitoring` and `counts the observations from when an unwitnessed fall was found` | Passing |
| `packages/domain` (safety): `hazard HZ-027 counts post-fall observations from when the person was found` (`classifier.test.ts`; the found time was dropped before slice 7 stored it) | Passing |
| Maestro `hazard-HZ-027-post-fall-observations.yaml` | Planned, Phase 1 |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/onboarding.test.ts` (go-live clinical items): `hazard HZ-027 a post-fall chain with no steps`, `hazard HZ-027 post-fall observation figures not reviewed by the clinical lead` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/onboarding.test.ts`: `HZ-027 clinical decisions name who reviewed them`; `apps/api/test/onboarding-decisions.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-027 accepts a clinical setting only with the clinical lead or CSO who reviewed it` (refused with no reviewer; the reviewer's member id in the audit event); `apps/web/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts`: a clinical setting refused on the page without its reviewer, then accepted naming them, with axe | Passing (slice 13 web) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/incident-forms.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-027 a fall asks the point-of-care questions, not sure yet included`; `apps/web/e2e/incident-forms.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-027 hazard HZ-090 the carer records a fall from a resident's record, never assumed no` (axe) | Passing (unit; web journeys with axe in CI on every pull request and on `main`; run 38938760784 on `d77b416`) |

## HZ-028 Point-of-care history on a device mistaken for the complete record

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-028 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `packages/sync` and `apps/mobile` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 17.1 and 17.2; [ADR-0011](../decisions/0011-offline-window-and-kiosk-lock.md) (offline window); [care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6 |

**Description**: a carer working offline reads the notes, charts or body-map marks on the phone as the person's whole recent history, but entries older than the tenant's offline window (`mobile.offline_window_days`) are not on the device, so an older open wound, a fluid or weight trend or an earlier refusal is missed.

**Causes**: the window removes entries by the day they happened (ADR-0011), and a body-map mark that is still present (an open pressure ulcer recorded ten days ago) is an entry, not reference data; a tenant choosing a short window; a device that has not yet read the tenant's figure keeps today only; a device that has not synced for days.

**Effects**: an existing wound not checked, or recorded again as new; a falling intake or weight not noticed; a pattern of refusals or falls missed at the point of care.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Today, the person's record and every chart say how many days of entries the device keeps ("Entries from the last 7 days are kept on this device"), a chart even when it has no entries on the device | Phase 1 (built in slice 10 part 1; empty charts after clinical review C9) |
| Design | Reference data is never evicted while the person is in scope: allergies, the care profile (IDDSI levels, SpO2 scale), the active care plan, active risk assessments, every validated-tool result and active advance care records | Phase 1 (built in slice 10 part 1) |
| Design | Present body-map marks and the latest weight and NEWS2 stay on the device whatever their age, through a current-record flag the server maintains and the streams filter on (needs an API and database change) | Phase 1 (slice 10 part 2) |
| Design | The full history stays one tap away online; the tenant chooses the window within ADR-0011's options | Phase 1 |
| Design | The window is minted by the API into the signed sync token from the rate table; the streams read it only from there, so a device cannot widen it (security review S1) | Built in Phase 1 |
| Design | The round on the phone holds only the open tasks due on the days of the offline window (the UTC day of `due_at`, from the token's window); an unfinished task due earlier is on the web round and in each handover as an overdue or unfinished task. The round screen is to say so, with when the phone last synced, and never to read an empty list as nothing due when the streams have no data (STATUS screens gate; the residual is for the CSO, H-301) | Streams built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2 (the tables are published by migration 0052, which `packages/db/test/sync-publication.test.ts` checks for every table a stream reads); the screen to follow |
| Design | The phone does not need the sources of a handover item to say what it is (the item's summary), nor the plan and series to say a round is from an earlier plan (the task's flag), so neither the round nor the handover reads as complete on the phone from data it only partly holds; what the phone holds, from when, and that it may be incomplete is said on both screens (screens gate item (d)) | Built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2 (data; the screens to follow) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Care worker guide: the phone holds the last few days; check the full record online or with the nurse in charge before relying on history. Deployer control: handover covers open wounds and charts with a trend | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/sync/test/care-streams.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 takes entries off the device when their day leaves the window`; `hazard HZ-002 reference data for assigned people is never evicted while the assignment stands` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/sync-summary.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 says how many days of entries the device keeps` | Passing (Phase 1, mobile) |
| `packages/sync`: `hazard HZ-028 present body-map marks stay on the device beyond the window` | Planned, Phase 1 (needs the server-maintained flag) |
| `packages/sync/test/care-streams.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 ignores a window sent by the device: only the token's window counts`, `sends no entries when the token has no window, whatever the device sends`; `apps/api/test/api.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 puts the tenant's offline window from the rate table in the token`, `does not let a script with a normal session widen the offline window` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/sync/test/task-handover-streams.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 takes a task off the device once it is done, skipped, deleted or due before the window`, `hazard HZ-028 ignores connection parameters that try to widen the scope or the window` | Passing (the sync-rules engine); the tables are published by migration 0052, which `packages/db/test/sync-publication.test.ts` checks for every table a stream reads |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/round-view.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 what the round on this phone holds, never read as nothing due (gate item (d))` (the days it holds, when it last synced and that it may be incomplete; unknown days or never synced said, not guessed; an empty round worded as what the phone holds); `apps/mobile/src/components/round-task.render.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 the round's header, rendered (gate item (d))`; Maestro `apps/mobile/.maestro/hazard-HZ-028-round-on-the-phone.yaml` (offline, a task marked done waits to upload and leaves the round once synced) | Passing (unit, Phase 1 slice 10 part 2, round screen); the Maestro flow is written and lints, and runs only against a live API (the mobile gate, phase-1-gate.md) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/round-view.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 what the round on this phone holds` from the token's own `offline_days` (UTC days), worded in UK time: a sync at 23:30 UTC in British Summer Time, and both clock-change Sundays (29/03/2026, 25/10/2026); `apps/mobile/src/sync/offline-days.test.ts` (#123 clinical review, M4; HZ-009) | Passing (round screen, PR #123) |
| `apps/mobile/src/sync/offline-days.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-028 the window is the one the last successful sync used (#123 clinical review re-check, M2)` (a token's days are recorded only once a sync that finished after it completes; a window that cannot be kept removes the old one, so the round says it cannot tell) | Passing (PR #123) |

## HZ-029 An entry recorded twice when a save is tried again

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-029 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `care` module and `apps/web` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Clinical review of the slice 9 web screens (finding M4); master prompt 6 (client UUIDs and idempotent inserts) |

**Description**: a note, chart entry, body-map mark, assessment, activity or visit is recorded twice because a save was tried again after its outcome was unknown, so the record shows two events where there was one.

**Causes**: the connection drops after the server saved the entry but before the reply arrived; the person presses Save again after an error message; a form that makes a new id on every attempt; a record type whose create route takes no client id (advance care records, risk assessments and care plan actions today).

**Effects**: a fluid intake counted twice overstates the day's total and hides a shortfall (dehydration); a repeated NEWS2 or repositioning entry misrepresents the observations or turns given; a duplicated body-map mark or incident note misleads a review; a duplicated advance care record could confuse which decision is in force.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Every append-only entry carries a client UUIDv7 made by the form; the same id is sent with every attempt and a new one is made only after a confirmed save (`useEntryId`, `apps/web/src/lib/entry-id.ts`) | Built in Phase 1 slice 9 (web) |
| Design | The API inserts with the client id and ignores a replay with the same id for the same person, returning the entry already saved; an id used for another person is refused | Built in Phase 1 slices 4a and 4b |
| Design | Mutable records (care plans, risk assessment reviews, closing and withdrawing) carry the version read, so a repeated update is refused as a conflict rather than applied twice | Built in Phase 1 slice 4b |
| Design | Advance care records, risk assessments, new care plan drafts, care plan actions, consents and DoLS entries take a client id on create, as the entry routes do, and the web sends a kept one for the first four; every care create route that takes an id also takes it as the `Idempotency-Key` header (the two must agree). The id is locked for the transaction, so two racing sends record once; an id already used for another person or another kind of record is refused ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 5). Create routes outside care that take none are listed for their owners in care.md section 10 | Built in Phase 1 (care API follow-ups) |
| Design | A save sent again with its client id must carry the details the record holds: a form keeps its id until a save is confirmed, so a reply lost on the way back and a change made before sending again (a CPR decision changed, say) would otherwise return the old record as if the new one were saved. Advance care records, risk assessments, care plan drafts and actions, consents, DoLS entries and escalation policy links compare the details sent with the stored record and refuse a difference with 409 ("This was already saved with different details: reload before changing it."), and nothing is saved; a removed escalation policy link is never returned as live. The refusal has its own code, `already_saved_differently`, so the web says "This was already saved with different details, so your change was not saved. Reload the page, check what was saved, and enter your change again if it is still needed.", never "Someone else changed this record" (HZ-015: a CPR decision changed and resent is not mistaken for a colleague's change); a care plan draft is compared on whether it asked to copy the active plan too (`care_plan.copy_from_active`, #26 review round 3). Handover notes, acknowledgements, capacity assessments and best-interests decisions take the client-id lock too, and an id another record has is refused in words, never a server error (#26 review round 2, C5) | Built in Phase 1 (#26 review round 2) |
| Design | On the phone (slice 10 part 2), one move waits per task and one acknowledgement per handover and member (a second tap writes nothing), each with its own UUIDv7; a task that needs evidence is done by the entry recorded for it, which names the task (`taskId`) so the API completes it, never by a tap; only the entry the task asks for completes it: never a care note, and for a chart's task only that chart; anything else is kept unlinked and reviewed (`isEvidenceFor`, in the API and on the phone; the CSO confirms this default, H-423; #123 clinical review re-check, M3). Every task, evidence ones included, also offers "Not done" with a reason, so a note is never the way to say it was not done (#123 clinical review, H1). The round shows a task whose entry is saved here as "evidence recorded, waiting to upload" (screens gate item (f)); the entry carries the task in a device-only column that its synced copy drops, so the device keeps, in its encrypted bookkeeping and by member and task, which tasks an entry saved here answered (`answered-tasks.ts`): it survives a restart, is cleared when the session is held or ended and rebuilt for the owner when held entries are put back, counts as answered until Harbour refuses that entry, and is forgotten when that refused entry is removed to be recorded again (#123 check, M1). The round says "Sent, not marked done" when Harbour kept the entry but the task stays open, and offers nothing more, not even "Not done"; the record never tags a second entry while the task is answered (#123 clinical review, M1 and its re-check) | Built (#36; the round screen, PR #123) |
| Design | On the phone (slice 10 part 2), a task's move carries its own UUIDv7 and the version seen, and an acknowledgement its own UUIDv7 (the API returns the stored one on a replay). A move whose answer was lost meets the new version (409) when sent again; the phone then reads the task from the task list (about the move's person, or at the task's home, due within a second of the task's due time) and counts the move as sent only when the task shows this move: the status asked for, by the same member of staff, with the same reason for a task not done, at the very next version after the one seen. Anything else is kept as needs attention and never sent again (#37 review round 2). Only an answer it can read decides: no network, a 5xx, any other status or a list it cannot read keeps the move queued, and a 401, a session code on a 403 or `GET /v1/me` saying so ends the session (#37 review round 1, security M1). With no network or a timeout the move stays queued however long it lasts, since nothing behind it can upload either. Reads the server refused, not about the session (a 403 on the task list, a 404, an answer it cannot read), count towards a cap (`TASK_CHECK_MAX_FAILURES`, 5 refused reads) and start a clock (`TASK_CHECK_MAX_MS`, 30 minutes); a 5xx or a 429 starts the clock without counting, so a short outage never parks the move but a fault in the task read alone does after 30 minutes (#37 confirmation check). At the cap the move is kept as needs attention with its data and the words "We could not check whether this was saved. Check the task on the round before recording it again.", so the notes and charts behind it still upload (HZ-006; #37 review round 2, N1). The count is cleared when the session ends | Built in Phase 1 slice 10 part 2 (data layer) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/web/src/lib/entry-id.test.ts`: `uuidv7 (hazard HZ-029) makes RFC 9562 version 7 ids that the API's contracts accept`; `orders ids by time and makes a new one each time` | Passing (Phase 1 slice 9) |
| `packages/db/test/care-record.test.ts`: corrections supersede; replayed device ids are ignored | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/care-planning.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-017 ignores a replayed upload with the same device id` | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 a save sent twice with the same client id is recorded once` (`records an advance care record, a risk assessment and a care plan action once`, with the new draft; `records a consent and a DoLS entry once, with the id in the Idempotency-Key header`; `refuses an id used for another person`) | Passing (care API follow-ups) |
| `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 a save sent again with other details is refused, never shown as saved (#26 review round 2, C5)` (`refuses a ReSPECT plan sent again with a changed CPR decision, and a DNACPR with a changed clinician`; `refuses a consent and a DoLS entry sent again with changed details`; `never returns a removed escalation policy link as live`) | Passing (#26 review round 2) |
| `apps/api/test/care-follow-ups.test.ts`: the ReSPECT refusal carries `code` `already_saved_differently`; `hazard HZ-029 a save sent twice with the same client id is recorded once` (a care plan draft sent again asking to copy the active plan is refused with `already_saved_differently`); `apps/web/src/lib/outcome.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 says a save sent again with other details was not changed, never that someone else changed it`, and `maps %s to plain words` (`already_saved_differently`) | Passing (#26 review round 3) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/round-actions.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 refuses a second move while one is waiting to upload`, `hazard HZ-029 writes the move on the row the carer saw, and nothing on a row that has changed`, `hazard HZ-029 acknowledges the items shown in their compiled order, once while it waits to upload`; `apps/mobile/src/care/notes.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 names the round's task it is recorded for, and the upload sends it`; `apps/mobile/src/sync/care-upload.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 sends the round's task the entry is the evidence for, so the API completes it` | Passing (Phase 1 slice 10 part 2, mobile; no screen yet) |
| `apps/mobile/src/sync/connector.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 counts a move the API already took as sent when its answer was lost`, `hazard HZ-006 hazard HZ-029 never sends a refused move again to a task still open (#37 review round 2)`, `hazard HZ-029 keeps the move queued when the task cannot be read` (no network, a server error, an answer it cannot read, a list without the task's fields, a refusal that leaves the session standing, any other status); `apps/mobile/src/sync/round-upload.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 hazard HZ-006 a move the API says the task changed for (#37 review rounds 1 and 2)` (counts the move as made only when the task shows it; settles the move from the task as it now is, never sending it again; says it could not find a task the list leaves out; reads the task from the list and decides nothing on an answer it cannot read; asks about its person or at its home) | Passing (Phase 1 slice 10 part 2, mobile) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/round-view.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 says evidence saved here waits to upload, and offers nothing more (gate item (f))`; `apps/mobile/src/components/round-task.render.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 offers the entry a task needs, never a tap`, `hazard HZ-029 offers nothing while its evidence or its move waits to upload (gate item (f))`; `apps/mobile/src/care/queries.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 lists the open tasks with their person, the earlier-plan flag and the evidence waiting here` | Passing (Phase 1 slice 10 part 2, round screen) |
| `apps/mobile/src/components/round-task.render.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 offers Not done with a reason on a task that needs evidence, never a note in its place (#123 clinical review, H1)`; `apps/mobile/src/care/answered-tasks.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 a round task is answered by one entry from this device (#123 clinical review, M1)` (stays answered after its entry uploads and the synced copy drops the task); `apps/mobile/src/care/queries.test.ts`: the evidence count drops to 0 when a sync clears `task_id` | Passing (round screen, PR #123) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/answered-tasks.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 a round task is answered by one entry from this device (#123 clinical review, M1)` (none until saved; waiting, then answered after the synced copy drops the task, said as "Sent, not marked done" with nothing offered; kept in the database and the member's own; refused when Harbour refuses that entry and answered again by the next; cleared by the hold's statement); `packages/domain/src/platform/tasks.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 only the entry a task asks for completes it` (a note never, a chart's task only by that chart; #123 clinical review re-check, M3) | Passing (PR #123) |
| `apps/mobile/src/care/queries.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 counts the entries saved here for one task (the record stops tagging after one)` (the person's record tags only the first entry saved for a task from the round, then says one is waiting; clinical review M1), `hazard HZ-006 counts an entry for a task Harbour refused, and never a refused move or bad JSON`; `apps/mobile/src/care/round-view.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-006 ties an entry Harbour refused back to its task, and offers it again` (clinical review M3) | Passing (round screen, clinical review of #50) |
| `apps/api/test/people-enquiries.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-029 returns the first copy when the same request is sent again with its id` (an enquiry; the web enquiry, property and key forms send their own id with every attempt) (PEOP-005 and PEOP-007 web) | Passing (Phase 1) |
