## HZ-040 Held entries deleted to get past the lockout

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-040 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `apps/mobile`, `apps/api` sync routes and task engine (lead engineer) |
| Source | Clinical review C8, round 3 H2, round 4 H1, H2 and M3, round 5 C-H1, C-M1 to C-M4 and lows, round 6 M1 to M3, and round 7 M1 to M5; security reviews S4, S5, round 3 M1 to M3, round 4 M1 to M3, round 5 S-M1, S-M2 and lows, round 6 N1, N2 and M2, and round 7 M1; [care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6 (including Device integrity, the follow-ups from the #15 reviews); linked to HZ-006 and HZ-008 |

**Description**: when a session ends with entries unsent, the device holds them for their owner and refuses anyone else (HZ-006). Notes, observations or body-map marks that never reached the server are lost if the held entries are deleted: by the next member of staff, locked out on a shared device, deleting them to get in, or by the device itself.

**Causes**

- The owner is off shift or has left; staff do not know whose entries they are or that the owner can send them; the lockout comes at the start of a busy shift; deleting is quicker than finding the owner.
- On a shared device, the device passcode alone allowed anyone to delete someone else's held entries (security review round 3, M2; now the owner's or a manager's sign-in is needed).
- The hold's time limit deleting held entries automatically when it ran out (the round 2 design; removed in round 3: the hold now escalates to a manager and nothing is deleted automatically). Assessed as a cause because it removed care records with no person deciding.
- Refused entries' content ageing out with the offline window while the server kept no copy (the security review S7 design; removed in round 3, clinical review M1). Assessed as a cause for the same reason.
- A device clock moved forward, bringing a deadline early, or moved back, hiding it (the clock never moves a hold on now: HZ-010). At the server, a report's times are never later than the server received it (round 5).
- A deletion recorded nowhere but the device (the earlier tombstone had no endpoint), or recorded with the person's words in the keystore outside the encrypted database (security review round 3, M1).
- The server records a deletion that then fails on the device (the round 3 design): the audit trail showed a deletion of entries still held, which could later upload. Round 4 recorded the stages apart; round 4's "failed" was also recorded when the entries were already gone (a sign-out clears the rows before the file), so the record said entries survived that did not (clinical review round 5, C-M1).
- A deletion recorded without its entry ids: a cancelled face or fingerprint prompt led to the passcode and a deletion with no ids, and a lock-screen sign-out after a cold start never opened the database (clinical review round 5, C-M2).
- Entries whose deletion was recorded reach the server later all the same (from another copy, or after a failed deletion), or had already reached it when a response was lost, so the same care is counted twice without anyone seeing it.
- The manager's task for a held device was often invisible to the Registered Manager: it had a location only when the reporter had exactly one live assignment, and a task with no location is visible only to tenant-wide roles (an agency worker's role ends at shift end plus 2 hours); its escalation went to team leaders, who cannot act on it (clinical review round 5, C-H1).
- Any member could close, postpone or open the manager's task for any device id with a report (security review round 5, S-M1).
- A hold's task orphaned when the device's id changed after a deletion, wipe or reset (clinical review round 5, C-M3).
- A hold whose session ended with a 401, `not_a_member` or `mfa_enrolment_required` cannot be reported until someone signs in on that device again (the owner, or another member of the organisation): until then the server knows only the device's last report (clinical reviews round 5, C-M4 and round 6, M3).
- The hold's owner could not close a task another member's sign-in had raised for them, and a deletion's last stage sent from the device's next database named the new device, so a hold the owner had drained or deleted stayed open and escalated (clinical review round 6, M1).
- Raising, closing and raising again with a backdated start made a new task due at once each time, about 60 escalated tasks an hour from one member, and the cap on open tasks dropped real holds silently (security review round 6, N2; clinical review round 6, M1).
- Staff working at more than one location had the task raised at one of them only (clinical review round 6, M2).
- Flagged entries raised one task per entry at every stage of a deletion, due at once and never deduplicated, about 2,000 tasks a day from one carer (security review round 6, N1).
- A report could name anyone in the organisation as the hold's owner: the organisation-wide owner's role routed one overdue task to each of up to 50 locations, the audit trail named an innocent person as the owner, a reporter reopening a task took over its owner's right to close it, and naming a new owner each report escaped the start floor (security review round 7, M1).
- A device lost, stolen or kept by someone who has left could not be stopped: the server could not
  tell it apart from one whose session had expired, so it kept its held entries with nothing to
  end them but its owner (H-68 go-live blocker (a); Device integrity part 2).
- Held entries could leave the device only with their owner's sign-in, so an owner who had left or
  was away left them waiting or deleted (H-68 go-live blocker (b); Device integrity part 6).
- A manager's hand-over could put entries in any member's name, from any device, timed at any
  time and about anyone the manager could see, with the sender only in the audit trail, so care
  could be misattributed to a carer and complete their rounds unseen (#25 review round 2, H1).
- Revocation relied on the device naming itself: a session from the revoked device that left the
  header out, or named a new device id, carried on (#25 review round 2, M1).
- A revoked device's hold can drain only by a hand-over or a recorded deletion, yet its task waited
  for the escalation day like any other hold (#25 review round 2, M4).
- The hand-over's count could come from the sender's own reports (a report of nothing waiting, then
  a large count); and genuine entries were refused: times compared with no clock-drift tolerance
  and by the event time of a back-dated entry, the owner's care measured back from the hand-over
  rather than the entry (an owner whose access ended over a weekend, or while the hold waited for
  days), and an older, smaller report of the owner's taken as the count (#25 review round 3, item 1
  and M1).
- Binding and deletion checks read the audit trail: slow as it grows (the go-live blocker on the
  deletion lookup) and open to an audit event standing in for a binding (Device integrity parts
  1 and 3).
- A reopened task kept its escalation, so a new hold after a proper close never signalled; a cap could stop the signal altogether (one notice a day, capped flagged entries kept out of every later task); flagged fluid entries waited a day while counting towards the fluid total; flagged entries for people at two locations went to one; an escalation with no one to tell was marked done (clinical review round 7, M1 to M5).

**Effects**: missing care records lead to missed or duplicated care (HZ-003, HZ-004), unescalated incidents (HZ-013) and an incomplete legal record, with no trace of what was lost.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

Tests are named in `file`: `test name` form; the Verification table below lists them all.

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The sign-in screen names whose entries are held and how many, and says only that member of staff can send them. Tests: `held-work.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 says whose entries are waiting, in whole sentences (C8)`; `held-view.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 the sign-in screen on held entries (clinical review H2, C8)` | Built in Phase 1 (mobile, clinical review C8) |
| Design | Nothing is deleted automatically. At `mobile.held_entries_max_days` the hold escalates: the last day shows a final warning naming the count and the time, then the sign-in screen says a manager must resolve the entries; a local notification, with no names or counts, fires when the last day starts and at the escalation. A hold without a usable deadline gets now plus the fail-safe day and is saved, so it is never open-ended (security review round 3, M3 and L2; clinical review round 3, H2). Tests: `held-limit.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 a hold escalates to a manager and is never deleted (security review M3)`; `held-work.test.ts`: `the hold marker always has a deadline (L2)` | Built in Phase 1 (mobile) |
| Design | Deleting held entries on a shared device needs the owner's or a manager's sign-in on the device (a manager holds `safety.incident.manage` in the same tenant; when the owner is unknown, only a manager, and only when the entries name the manager's organisation and no other), then device authentication, a coded reason from the list and a confirmation naming the count; the device passcode alone is not enough. There are no words and no "Other" here, because the database the words would be kept in is the one deleted. The signer's session ends straight after. Tests: `held-work.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 $case`, `hazard HZ-040 checks the entries are the manager's organisation's when the owner is unknown (round 4, low)`, `hazard HZ-040 offers no words, and no Other, where the database is deleted (round 4, M3)`; `sign-out.render.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 offers the listed reasons only: no Other and no words, which the deletion would remove (round 4, M3)` | Built in Phase 1 (mobile) |
| Design | A deletion always says which entries it removes, read before anything is deleted (clinical reviews round 4 M3 and round 5 C-M2). A key that is there but could not be read this time (a cancelled prompt) refuses the deletion and asks to try again; entries that could not be read with a key that was read refuse it too. A deletion goes ahead without ids only when nothing on the device could ever be read: no key at all (`key_absent`) or a key kept that is not a valid one (`key_invalid`), and the reason is recorded at the server. A sign-out from the lock screen after a cold start opens the database to read them; a sign-out whose ids cannot be read is refused. Whether a deletion without ids is ever acceptable is a CSO decision (H-144). Tests: `held-work.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 which entries a deletion removes, read before anything is deleted (round 5, C-M2)` (all five cases, including `hazard HZ-040 refuses with HeldIdsUnreadableError when the key was read but the entries could not be`); `api.test.ts`: `records what a deletion left on the device as a security event, and why it has no ids (round 5, C-M1 and C-M2)` | Built in Phase 1 (mobile and API) |
| Design | A deletion is put on record as requested before anything is deleted: a tombstone in the keystore holding only a coded reason, the stage and opaque ids (entry, person, owner, who deleted, kind), and `POST /v1/sync/discards`, an append-only audit event per stage with who sent it, who deleted (unknown stays unknown), whose they were, the coded reason, the ids and the device. A request sent at once carries up to 500 entry ids, never more than the count; a request that has to wait keeps 20 of them in the keystore (each item stays under 2 KB) and sends those (round 5, C-L2). The person's own words are never sent: Harbour records the coded reason. A tombstone that cannot be written refuses the deletion. A member of staff's own entries may be deleted while the request waits to be sent; anyone else's, or entries whose owner is unknown, only once the server has acknowledged it. Waiting stages are sent again at each sync, in order; a record this session may not send (403) is skipped and kept; none is dropped before the server has it, and a full list refuses a new deletion rather than drop one. At the API, anyone but the caller's own entries deleted by the caller needs `safety.incident.manage` (Registered Manager, deputy, nurse in charge); the owner and the deleter must be staff of the organisation; a later stage must match the first stage's kind and owner and come from its sender, the owner or a manager; each person may send 30 records an hour, start 20 new records a day (with or without ids) and list 2,000 new entry ids a day (security reviews S4, round 3 M1 and M2, round 4 M1 to M3, round 5 S-M2, S-L1 and S-L2, and round 6 M2). The tombstone names the device the entries were held on, and every stage sends it, so a stage sent from the device's next database still closes that device's task (clinical review round 6, M1). Tests: `discards.test.ts`, `tombstones.test.ts` and `api.test.ts` `hazard HZ-040 device discards (POST /v1/sync/discards)`, as listed below | Built in Phase 1 (mobile and API) |
| Design | A deletion is checked, and its outcome means one thing (clinical reviews round 4 H1 and round 5 C-M1 and C-L1). The entries are marked never to be uploaded together with the request, before anything is deleted; if that keystore write fails, "failed" is recorded and nothing is deleted. Then the rows are cleared (on sign-out, where the database is open), the file deleted and the key destroyed, each checked. "Failed", superseding the request, is recorded only while the entries can still be read: the file, its key and the rows all remain. The entries then stay held and blocked: when their owner signs in they move to "needs attention" (`held_discard_failed`), the device does not connect until they have, and if they cannot be moved the person is told those entries will never be sent. Otherwise "deleted" is recorded, and any file or key left behind is recorded at the server as a separate security event (`sync.device.residue`). Signing out with unsent entries follows the same steps. What "deletion failed" means for the record is a CSO decision (H-143). Tests: `held-work.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-006 hazard HZ-040 the explicit discard wipes: requested and blocked first, deleted only once the entries cannot be read`, `hazard HZ-040 records failed only while the entries can still be read: the file and its key both remain (round 5, C-M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 records deleted when the entries cannot be read, reporting %s left behind (round 5, C-M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 deletes nothing when the entries cannot be marked never to be uploaded (round 5, C-L1)`, `hazard HZ-040 signing out clears the rows before the file, so a file left behind holds no readable entries (round 5, C-M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 signing out records failed when the rows could not be cleared and the file and key remain (round 5, C-M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 a deletion of %j ends %s (round 5, C-M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 after a deletion that did not finish, nothing is uploaded: every entry moves to needs attention (round 4, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 entries a failed deletion left, which could not be moved (round 5, C-L6)`; `api.test.ts`: `records what a deletion left on the device as a security event, and why it has no ids (round 5, C-M1 and C-M2)` | Built in Phase 1 (mobile and API) |
| Design | An entry uploaded after its own owner's device recorded deleting it (the "deleted" stage, and not later that the deletion failed) is kept, and flagged: an audit event on the entry and a count on one review task for the deletion record, for the Registered Manager at the entries' location, so care recorded again is not counted twice unseen. Only a deletion whose owner is the uploader counts, so no one can mark someone else's entries. An entry a deletion lists that had already reached the server, written by the deletion's owner (a lost response), is flagged the same way when the deletion is recorded as done (clinical reviews round 4 H1 and round 5 C-L2; security reviews round 5 S-M2 and round 6 N1). Each deletion record has one task per kind and location, so each location's managers see only their own people's entries (clinical review round 7, M4), whatever its number of entries, carrying the count in its title and its owner, count and entries (kind and opaque id, up to 50, the rest counted) in `task.details`, which the task routes return (its audit events keep how many were added); a partial unique index on open tasks by kind, source, location and assignee stops a second whatever the locks do, and the transaction-scoped lock on the tenant, kind, record and location stays because it serialises the counts (Device integrity part 4), and one owner's flags are taken in turn under a per-owner lock; an entry flagged before, under an earlier record or on its upload, is not flagged again, and every flagged entry is on a task; each owner's deletions have at most 5 such tasks open at a location, and past that the entries join the oldest of them, the cap is audited and the Registered Managers get the daily notice (round 7, M2; HZ-008). The task is due at once when any entry is an observation, otherwise within 24 hours, and comes forward when an observation joins it (round 7, M3; HZ-008). The lookup is one indexed read of `device_discard_entry`, where each stage of a deletion lists its entry ids, one append-only row each (filled from the existing deletion events by migration 0033), never a scan of the audit trail; a test keeps it fast at 3,000 records. The go-live blocker on this lookup is closed (Device integrity part 3). Tests: `care.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 an entry a device reported deleted, uploaded later (round 4, H1)` (all eleven cases, including `hazard HZ-040 gives each location's managers only their own people's flagged entries (round 7, M4)`, `hazard HZ-008 makes a flagged task due at once when an entry is an observation, and within the day otherwise (round 7, M3)`, `hazard HZ-008 merges an owner's flagged entries past the cap into their oldest open task there, so no cap stops a task (round 6, N1; round 7, M2)`, `flags nothing until the device records the deletion as done (round 6, N1)`, `raises one task per deletion record, however many of its entries arrive (round 6, N1)`, `does not flag again an entry listed under a new deletion record (round 6, N1)`, `stays fast with many deletion records in the tenant (round 5, S-M2)` and `flags an entry a deletion lists that had already reached the server, once the deletion is done (round 5, C-L2; round 6, N1)`); `device-integrity.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 finds an uploaded entry's deletion in device_discard_entry, never by reading the audit trail`, `hazard HZ-040 does not flag an entry whose deletion failed, nor someone else's deletion`, `hazard HZ-040 gives a flagged task its owner, count and entries in its details, returned by the task route` | Built in Phase 1 (API); deletion lists and the open-task index built (Device integrity parts 3 and 4) |
| Design | The device reports its held, unsent, refused and blocked counts, the oldest age and when the hold started, at each sync when they change and as soon as a hold starts: with the session that is ending (any member of the organisation may report, with MFA where their roles call for it), again while the app runs for an hour, and otherwise with the next session in the tenant; a report that could not be built is replaced by one from the marker's count and kept, never taken as sent; once it has gone, or the hour has passed, the ended session is signed out on the server (clinical reviews round 4 H2 and round 5 C-M4; security review round 5, S-L3). The owner's own next sign-in sends a waiting report too. Every report names the hold's owner and the opaque ids of the locations of the owner's sync scope (kept in the hold's marker and the database's owner record), and a waiting report is sent under the id of the device that held the entries (clinical review round 6, M1 and M2). The device sends its id with every sync token request, so the server records who has used it (security review round 7, M1). Tests: `device-report.test.ts`: `says when the hold started only while entries are held`, `hazard HZ-040 counts the entries a failed deletion left, so the manager's task stays open (round 5, C-M3)`, `hazard HZ-040 still reports a hold whose entries could not be counted (round 5, C-M4)`, `hazard HZ-040 a hold's report names its owner, their locations and the device that held it (clinical review round 6, M1 and M2)`; `held-work.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 a hold names where its owner works, so the manager's task goes there (clinical review round 6, M2)`; `connector.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 names the device when it asks for credentials, so the server knows who has used it (security review round 7, M1)`; `api.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-006 records a device's unsent counts without personal data`, `asks for MFA where the member's roles call for it (round 5, S-M1)`. The retry and the sign-out are vault wiring with no automated test (device test plan, H-68) | Built in Phase 1 (mobile and API) |
| Design | While anything waits (held, unsent or blocked), the server keeps the device's task for a Registered Manager, due at the hold's start plus `mobile.held_entries_max_days` (clinical reviews round 5 C-H1, round 6 M1 and M2 and round 7 M1, M2 and M5; security reviews round 5 S-M1, round 6 N2 and round 7 M1). The owner the report names counts only when bound to the device: they have a row for it in `device_binding` (written by a sync token issued to it or a report sent from it, and filled from the audit trail for devices seen before; Device integrity part 1), as the real owner has from syncing before the hold; an audit event never stands in for a binding. A named owner who is not bound is recorded as a claim (`claimedOwnerTenantUserId`) and changes nothing: the task is routed by the reporter's assignments alone, it gives no right to close, the hold's start floor applies whoever is named, and a reopen keeps the recorded owner. Where it goes: the report names the hold's owner (who must be staff of the organisation) and the locations of the device's sync scope; the server keeps those where the reporter, or the owner when bound, has a live or most recent role assignment (any of the organisation's for someone assigned across it), and raises one task at each, closed together; with none left, the reporter's live locations, then their most recent one (an agency worker's role ends with the shift); with no location at all, each Registered Manager in the organisation gets one, assigned to them. Overdue, it escalates only to holders of `safety.incident.manage` at its location. Who closes it: a report with nothing waiting, or recording "deleted" for the device's whole hold (found by the device the deletion names, C-M3), closes every copy when it comes from the hold's owner (named when the task was raised or reopened, even when another member's sign-in sent that report), from whoever raised it, or from a holder of `safety.incident.manage` at one of its locations; anyone else's close is refused and audited. A new hold after a refused close resets the escalation (the due time never later), and so does a reopen of a task that had escalated, under one once-a-day check; a reopen that finds the day's reset used is reset by a later report once it is due, once, so a new hold always signals (clinical review round 8, M2). Bounds: a report's times are never later than the server received it, and a hold's start never earlier than the device's last report for that hold said, nor than the server first saw the device (the earliest first seen in `device_binding`); a later report can bring the due time earlier, never later; a task closed less than a day ago is reopened; each reporter raises tasks for at most 3 devices in a rolling day, counted under a per-reporter lock, and past that each further hold joins the reporter's one capped-holds task, due at the earliest of them and escalating like a device task, so no cap stops a hold's task; the cap is audited and the location's Registered Managers also get one notice a day about the reporter. Overdue with no one at the location who can act, the task is assigned to the longest-serving Registered Manager anywhere (else holder of the permission anywhere), who can then see and complete it, and is never marked escalated with no one told (rounds 7 and 8, M5 and M1). Every location is taken only from the reporter's own organisation-wide role, and the start floor applies whoever is named as owner (security review round 7, L1 and L2). The task is written as the reporter, and its raise, reopen, moves, reset, close, refused close and cap are audited with opaque ids; reports for a device are handled one at a time under a lock. Who receives and may close these tasks is a CSO decision (H-142), as is the merge in place of a stopped task (H-149). Tests: `api.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 raises one task for the Registered Manager at the reporter's location, due when the hold escalates, never moved later, and closed at zero (round 5, C-H1 and S-M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 lets the hold's owner close the task another member's sign-in raised for them (round 6, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 takes a named owner who never used the device as a claim only: routed by the reporter, recorded, with no right to close (security review round 7, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 keeps a hold's start floor on the bound owner, so naming a new owner each report does not escape it (security review round 7, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 keeps the bound owner when a member who never used the device reopens its task (security review round 7, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 routes to every location only from the reporter's own organisation-wide role, never from a named owner's (security review round 7, L1)`, `hazard HZ-040 never takes a hold's start earlier than the device's last report for it said, whoever reports (round 6, N2; security review round 7, L2)`, `hazard HZ-008 signals a hold reopened after the day's reset was used once the reset is due, and only once (clinical review round 8, M2)`, `hazard HZ-040 raises the task at each location of the device's scope where the owner or reporter works, and closes them together (round 6, M2)`, `hazard HZ-040 closes every Registered Manager's copy with one report or one deletion (round 6)`, `hazard HZ-040 lets a manager close a device's task only at its locations (round 6)`, `hazard HZ-040 gives each Registered Manager the task when the reporter's location is not known (round 5, C-H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 finds the location of an agency worker whose role ended with the shift (round 5, C-H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 another member can neither close nor postpone a known device's task (round 5, S-M1)`, `hazard HZ-010 takes a hold's start no later than the server received the report (round 5, S-M1)`, `hazard HZ-008 reopens a device's task closed within the day and resets its escalation at most once a day, so a new hold always signals and raising again signals at most once more (round 6, N2; round 7, M1)`, `hazard HZ-008 merges each reporter's holds past the day's cap into one task, due at the earliest of them, and tells the location's Registered Manager once (round 6, N2; round 7, M2)`, `hazard HZ-008 resets a task's escalation when a new hold follows a refused close, once a day (round 6, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 closes the device's hold task when its held entries are recorded deleted (round 5, C-M3)`, `hazard HZ-040 closes the hold of the device a deletion names, sent from the device's next database (round 6, M1)`; `tasks.test.ts`: `tells holders of the task's permission at its location, not team leaders`, `does not tell a holder of the permission at another location (round 6)`, `hazard HZ-040 a sync task whose location has no one who can act on it (round 7, M5)`; `device-integrity.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 binds the member to the device at each sync token, first seen kept and last seen moved on`, `hazard HZ-040 binds a reporter by their report, judged after it (security review round 7, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 takes the owner as bound from device_binding, never from an audit event (security review round 7, M1)`, `hazard HZ-010 never takes a hold's start earlier than the device's earliest binding`, `hazard HZ-008 raises one device task per location however many reports race` | Built in Phase 1 (API) |
| Design | A manager can hand over another member of staff's held entries ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 6). On the sign-in screen with entries held, "Send N entries" asks a manager (or, on a revoked device, the owner) to sign in on the device, the sign-in being the step-up; after device authentication the device sends its report from that sign-in first (binding the sender to the device and reporting the hold's count), then each held entry, in order, to its own endpoint naming the device that held it, the owner and the hand-over. The API takes it only when the sender is the owner or holds `care.held_entry.hand_over` (owner, Registered Manager, deputy, clinical lead or nurse; step-up and MFA) and has used the device; the owner is staff of the organisation bound to that device; the device's reports show an open hold for that owner, and the hold's entries handed over since the latest report in it not sent by the sender (the owner's own preferred; the sender's own reports never count, so with no other report the hand-over is refused, H-158) stay within the count that report gave; the entry's time (when it was written on the device, else its event time) is, within `sync.clock_drift_flag_minutes`, at or after the owner first used the device, at or before that report and, on a revoked device, at or before the revocation; the person was in the owner's care within the offline window before the entry's own time; and the hand-over id names that owner, device and sender only (#25 review rounds 2 and 3, H1, item 1 and M1). Each entry is admitted in its own transaction (`app.admit_handed_over_entry`), so an entry refused after its admission uses none of the count, recorded in `handed_over_entry` and written as the owner's with the sender kept as "Sent by" (`handed_over_by`, returned with every entry) and audited (`sync.entry.handed_over`); any round it completes is judged on the owner's permission when the care was recorded (HZ-004), and it is flagged against the owner's deletions. A refusal is 422 `handover_refused`; on that or a 404 the device keeps the entry held with the server's reason, never parked as refused, and sends the rest; on any 403 it stops (H2; round 3, M1). An entry refused for what it is moves to needs attention and its copy goes to the server naming the owner, admitted against the hold like the entry (HZ-006). The device first holds anything left in its upload queue, and deletes its database only when no held, needs-attention or queued entry remains, never when one of those cannot be counted (round 3, M4). What the hand-over may do is for the CSO and DPO (H-158). Tests: `device-integrity.test.ts` (API): `hazard HZ-040 a manager hands over a member's held entries: written as the owner's, sent by the manager, recorded`, `hazard HZ-040 needs the hand-over sender who never used the device: the device sends its report as the sender first (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 takes no more entries than the hold reported, however much the refuses an entry timed before the owner first used the device (#25 review round 2, H1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-004 refuses an entry about someone never in the owner's care, though the manager may written before the sign-in is not (#25 review round 3, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-006 takes entries whose owner's care ended more than the offline window before the hand-over, as after a weekend or a hold left to escalate, judged from each entry's time (#25 review round 3, M1)`, `hazard HZ-040 uses none of the hold's count for an entry refused after its admission: both are in one transaction (#25 review round 3)`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-006 counts a handed-over copy of a refused entry against the hold like the entry it is (#25 review round 3)`; `handover.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-006 handing over held entries (Device integrity part 6)` | Built in Phase 1 (API and mobile, Device integrity; #25 review rounds 2 and 3); the H-68 go-live blocker (b) closes when the DPO and CSO accept H-158 |
| Design | A manager can revoke a device ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 2). `GET /v1/devices` lists the devices staff have used, with the entries each last reported waiting (`platform.device.read`), and `POST /v1/devices/{deviceId}/revoke` (`platform.device.revoke`, step-up; owner, Registered Manager and deputy) with a coded reason revokes every binding of the device, audited; a location role sees only devices its staff used, and revokes one only when every member bound to it is its staff. When the device last reported entries waiting, the revocation needs the manager to confirm the count (409 `entries_waiting` otherwise), its hold's task (and any capped-holds task it joined) falls due at once, urgent, and the manager is told: a revoked hold can drain only by a hand-over or a recorded deletion (#25 review round 2, M4). The sessions bound to the device are kept, so the device can tell a revocation from an expired session, but every request from one is refused with 403 `device_revoked` whatever device it names, except its device report, which may raise or hurry the hold's task but never close it; any request naming a device binds its session (#25 review rounds 2 and 3, M1 and M2). A second revocation hurries the hold again, moving only what is not yet due and urgent, and the list and the revocation give when the device last reported (`lastReportedAt`). A new sign-in on the device is refused with 403 `device_revoked` on the sync token and uploads and the device reacts as to a remote wipe: with nothing unsent it deletes its database and key; with entries unsent it keeps them under the hold rules above, marked revoked, and no sign-in resumes syncing, not even the owner's: the entries leave only by a hand-over of those recorded before the revocation (the owner's own or a manager's) or a recorded deletion. The report and deletion routes still take it, so the server keeps hearing what it holds. Whether a revoked device should delete its held entries at once is for the DPO and CSO (H-156). Tests: `device-integrity.test.ts` (API): `hazard HZ-040 revokes a device with a coded reason after a fresh sign-in, once told what it holds: audited, its task due at once and whoever revoked it told; a second revocation changes nothing more`, `hazard HZ-040 hazard HZ-006 keeps a revoked device's sessions and refuses each with device_revoked whatever it names, one that never asked for a sync token too, except its report, which never closes the task (#25 review round 3, M2)`; `hazard HZ-040 hurries a revoked device's hold that joined its reporter's capped-holds task (#25 review round 3, low)`; `held-work.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-040 never lets anyone resume syncing on a revoked device, not even the owner` | Built in Phase 1 (API and mobile, Device integrity; #25 review rounds 2 and 3); the H-68 go-live blocker (a) closes when the DPO and CSO accept H-156 |
| Design | A server check for a device whose last report or sync has gone stale, raising a task for the Registered Manager, would cover holds the server cannot hear of (below). The table it needs now exists (`device_binding`, with first and last seen; Device integrity part 1); the check itself is not built, and whether it blocks go-live is a CSO decision (H-141) | Planned (the table is built) |
| Design | Each stage of a deletion record is one row of `device_discard_record`, unique on the deletion and the stage (the discard replay index): a stage sent twice, however two requests race, is recorded once, and a later stage is checked against the deletion it continues from those rows, read in the system's view, rather than from the audit trail ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6, Device integrity part 8) | Built in Phase 1 (care API follow-ups) |
| Design | The server keeps its own flag on an entry its device recorded deleting (`entry_device_deletion_flag`: `uploaded_after_deletion` or `deleted_after_upload`, one per entry, append-only, written only in the system's view and read like the entry), returned as `deviceDeletion` on every entry item, so the entry itself shows it may be a duplicate or meant to be withdrawn, not only the Registered Manager's task; "already reached Harbour" is one indexed read of it. Keyed by table and id (#26 review round 2, low) | Built in Phase 1 (API, care API follow-ups) |
| Design | Flagged entries are never hidden or left out silently: the web shows the flag on the entry with an icon and words ("Sent after its device deleted it: check for a duplicate"), NEWS2 and chart lists carry it, and the fluid day returns how many flagged entries it counted and their intake and output, shown beside the total ("Includes 2 entries that a device recorded as deleted (300 ml in, 0 ml out): check for duplicates."). Whether they should count towards totals is for the CSO (H-165) (#26 review round 2, C3) | Built in Phase 1 (#26 review round 2) |
| Design | The coded reasons are each organisation's own list (`discard_reason`), seeded with the five the phone offers, changed only by tenant-wide roles, retired rather than deleted (a retired code still taken from a device that recorded it before); a deletion record carrying a code the organisation never had is refused, so every recorded reason is one the organisation defined. Codes it adds travel as `organisationReasonCode` beside `reasonCode` `other`; the phone offers the five until it reads the list (H-146, H-164). Who changes them is read from the roles and grants holding `platform.tenant.manage` across the organisation (S1); an organisation's own code is audited as `other` with the reason's id, so its words never enter the hash-chained trail (#26 review round 2); adding, changing and retiring a reason are audited by its id only, and a reason is changed by its id in the URL (`PUT /v1/discard-reasons/{reasonId}`), never its code; a role mixing portal and staff permissions gives no right to change links or reasons, in the API and in `app.holds_permission_at` (#26 review round 3) | Built in Phase 1 (API, care API follow-ups; #26 review rounds 2 and 3) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Shared-device procedure: before leaving, wait for the banner to show nothing waiting; if locked out by someone else's entries, ask the nurse in charge to contact them rather than deleting; when a hold escalates, a manager resolves it. Deployer control: a shift lead can reach staff who have just left | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**, and not lower until the location routing (C-H1) and the meaning of "failed" (C-M1) are confirmed on a device. The manager hand-over upload, device revocation and the indexed deletion lists landed with Device integrity ([care.md](../specs/care.md) section 6) and leave the rating unchanged until the CSO has seen them on a device (H-156, H-158). The server hears of every deletion and each of its stages with who made it and why, nothing is deleted automatically, a shared device needs the owner or a manager to delete, a failed deletion never uploads, and a held device gives a Registered Manager who can act a task. The gap that remains: a hold whose session ended with a 401, `not_a_member` or `mfa_enrolment_required` is reported only when someone signs in on that device again (the owner's own sign-in sends the report, as does another member's in the organisation); until then the server knows only the last report. When another member's sign-in sends it, the task goes where the owner (bound to the device) or that member works and the owner, its raiser or a manager there may close it. The rating stays Major / Low / 3 with the round 6 and 7 fixes. The rating, and whether clinical entries may ever be deleted automatically, are CSO decisions (human actions H-124, H-126 and H-141).

---

## HZ-050 An inspector evidence pack exposes a third party or a person outside its scope

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-050 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]`; data protection lead consulted |
| Engineering owner | `quality` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 11.2 (evidence library: "inspector evidence pack export (PDF bundle, watermarked, time-limited link)"), 4 (inspector role: read-only, time-boxed, watermarked exports), 5.1 (data minimisation); [quality.md](../specs/quality.md) rules 1 to 10; [ADR-0050](../decisions/0050-evidence-pack-links.md) |

**Description**: an evidence pack sent to an inspector or commissioner contains the name or details of someone it should not: a relative, visitor, witness or other resident named in a record; a person receiving care who is not in the pack's scope; a whistleblower; or identifiers not needed for the assessment; or the pack reaches someone other than its recipient, or stays open after it should have closed.

**Causes**: records selected by location or date only, without the people in scope; free-text notes that name other residents or family members; incident records about several people; witness and visitor fields copied as written; speak-up or employee relations records tagged as evidence; NHS numbers and addresses copied by default; one person both composing and releasing without a second check; a link forwarded to a colleague; a link with no expiry, or one that cannot be withdrawn; a bundle left in storage after the link ends. Added in the slice 11 review: a field's kind taken from the caller rather than the record type, so a typed column is treated as safe; a hand redaction that misses a name or an NHS number; the person who redacted the text releasing it; a new link issued after a lock-out by someone who shaped the pack, or to a different address; a code guessed by alternating with the real recipient, reused, or verified in one browser and used in another; the permission to open packs held by roles that do not need it.

**Effects**: a person's health, whereabouts or safeguarding history disclosed to someone they did not expect (distress, loss of trust, risk from an alleged abuser who learns of a concern); a whistleblower identified; a reportable personal data breach; the provider's relationship with the regulator harmed.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Selection by rule: restricted silos never included; date range in UK time; requested locations and statements only; items about nobody in scope excluded, with the reason kept in the internal manifest (`evidenceItemExclusion` in `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | Minimisation field by field: the kind of every field comes from a typed map per record type (`EVIDENCE_FIELD_KIND_MAP`), never from the caller, and a field the map does not name is free text; every column a person types into is free text; care content only about people in scope; identifiers withheld; third parties and (by default) staff replaced by a role from a closed list; free text withheld until a person has redacted it by hand; every redaction says why in words (`minimiseEvidenceField`, `evidenceFieldKind`; [quality.md](../specs/quality.md) rule 3) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | A hand-redacted copy is withheld again when it still holds a third party's name, a staff name while roles are shown, the name of a person on the record outside the pack's scope, or a run of ten digits (`redactionLeaks`; quality.md rule 4) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | Release, and any new link, by someone who did not shape the pack (not the composer, a hand redactor or any other editor), with step-up; a new link goes to the same recipient only and revokes the old one (`evidencePackContributors`, `checkEvidencePackRelease`, `checkEvidencePackLinkReissue`; quality.md rules 6 and 9) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | One link per recipient: a hashed random token, opened only after a six-digit one-time code from a cryptographic source, stored hashed, used once and valid for at most 10 minutes, sent to the bound address; verification held in an HttpOnly session for that link, never on the link row; a failed-code count that never resets and locks the link for good; expiry at a tenant default up to a maximum; revocable at once; the bundle deleted when the link ends; every page watermarked with the recipient, purpose, time and pack id (`checkEvidencePackCode`, `evidencePackLinkAccess`, `evidencePackWatermark`, `evidencePackBundleDeletable`; quality.md rules 7, 8 and 10; ADR-0050) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11; routes with the API step) |
| Design | `quality.evidence.read` is a care-record permission (MFA) held by the inspector role alone | Phase 1 (built, slice 11) |
| Design | Every open, code attempt and revocation is audited | Phase 1 (built, slice 11 parts 2a and 2c) |
| Design | The public link routes run as a link session the database holds to that link and its pack alone (migration 0058); the token goes only in a body and is looked up by its hash; codes are an HMAC under a server key, at most three open, counted in the same transaction as the attempt and locked for good at five; the session is a signed HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict cookie for one link; the PDF is drawn from the bundle alone, with the watermark on every page, and refused once the bundle is deleted (quality.md section 15) | Phase 1 (built, slice 11 part 2c) |
| Design | The API reads each record added to a pack in the composer's own scope (anything else is not found) and keeps only the values the pack shows; text typed inside a structured value (an observation's drink or override reason) is free text, withheld until redacted by hand; the bundle is deleted at once on revoke or discard and by the five-minute sweep when a link has ended (quality.md section 13) | Phase 1 (built, slice 11 part 2a) |
| Design | Release re-reads every record in the pack and is refused, naming each, when one was corrected or changed since it was added; each entry says how it came to be (a correction, a back-entry from paper with the paper time and outage, the recording time, a wrong device clock); readings carry labels and units; times carry GMT or BST (#60 review round 1; H-221, H-222) | Phase 1 (built, slice 11 part 2a) |
| Design | The database holds the same line (migration 0057): a pack is visible only to holders of compose or release at every one of its locations who can see every person in its scope; its request is fixed once composed; items are append-only and hold outcomes, never values or redacted copies (the shown values live in the bundle, and whoever changes them becomes an editor; deleted when the link ends or the pack is revoked or discarded, never restored); a hand redaction is in the redactor's name and makes them an editor; release and every link only by a member outside the composer, editors, redactors and item writers, holding release at every location; a link goes to the pack's recipient only, expires within the maximum, and a new one replaces the last, revoked first; failed code attempts never go down; a code's lifetime is worked out by the database (at most 10 minutes) and it is used once, only while the link is live | Phase 1 (schema built, slice 11) |
| Design | The web screens (`/quality/evidence-packs`; quality.md section 16) put "What will be shared" first: the recipient and the address the link is bound to, the purpose, dates, locations, the people in scope by name, staff names, the watermark and counts of records, withheld and redacted fields; every field's outcome is an icon and a word with the reason in words. Release and a new link are offered only to a holder who did not shape the pack (a contributor is told why), after the expiry is said in words and the releaser ticks that they have read every record and redaction; step-up is the API's. The link is shown once in the page only, with its token in the URL fragment (never a path, query, log or store), and forgotten when the releaser says they have sent it | Phase 1 (built, `feat/web-evidence-sop`) |
| Design | After the clinical, security and accessibility reviews of the web screens: the link's origin comes from configuration only (`PUBLIC_WEB_URL`), never a request header, so a forged host cannot put another site in front of the token, and release is not offered (with words) when it is unset; each person to choose and each person in scope shows a date of birth and their communication needs, so two people with the same name are told apart; a hand redaction shows the original read-only beside an empty box and refuses a copy identical to it; a care note is never read again across everyone in scope to prefill one (DPIA R13); link stops and days left are counted in UK days, never rounded up; withdrawing asks for a confirmation that it cannot be undone | Phase 1 (built, `feat/web-evidence-sop` review round 1) |
| Design | The public page `/evidence` reads the token from the fragment once, takes it out of the address bar, keeps it only in memory and sends it only in JSON bodies through same-origin route handlers to the API; it forgets the token once the code is verified; the pack is downloaded with the link's own session cookie and streamed without being stored; withdrawn, expired and locked links say so and offer no code (quality.md section 16) | Phase 1 (built, `feat/web-evidence-sop`) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the quality lead reviews the redaction preview before asking a second person to release; the DPO confirms the redaction rules (H-96) | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 fields are minimised and redacted by rule` (third parties' names removed, care content about people outside the pack withheld, free text withheld until a person has redacted it, identifiers withheld, staff by role) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 items outside the request are excluded` (restricted records, dates in UK time, locations, statements, people outside the pack's scope) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 the field-to-kind map decides how every field is treated` (`covers every evidence record type`; `treats every field a person types as free text`; `ignores any kind the caller tries to give a field`) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 hand-redacted text that still names someone is withheld` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 releasing a pack: nobody who shaped it may release it`; `hazard HZ-050 issuing a new link is a release` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 one-time codes` (six digits only, wrong link, malformed, used, expired, wrong code; `never resets the failed-attempt count, even after a correct code`); `hazard HZ-050 the link opens only for its recipient's own session, before expiry and revocation`; `lets a tenant shorten the code lifetime but never lengthen it` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/quality/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `gives opening a released pack to the inspector role alone (least privilege)` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `apps/api/test/evidence-packs.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 composing and minimising an evidence pack` (composed only in the composer's scope; free text withheld, staff as a role and identifiers withheld, with no withheld value in the bundle or the manifest; a record about someone outside the scope excluded; typed text inside an observation's reading treated as free text; a leaking hand redaction refused; release and a new link refused to the composer; the token stored only as its hash and never audited; a revoked pack's link stopped and bundle deleted) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 part 2a) |
| `apps/api/test/evidence-packs.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 a pack says how each entry came to be, and shows the record as it stands` (a correction labelled, its reason withheld; a back-entry from paper labelled with the paper time and outage, its reference withheld; a wrong device clock; release refused after a correction, naming the record); `counts only the chart schema's own values as codes`; step-up on release and re-issue; `no-store` on the token responses | Passing (#60 review round 1) |
| `apps/api/test/evidence-packs.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 release and a new link stop at what the releaser can stand behind` (a new link refused to an editor who is not the composer; release refused with `record_changed` when an incident's version moved on; release refused when the releaser cannot open a record, naming only the pack's own title; an observation's recording time and wrong device clock labelled) | Passing (#60 review round 2) |
| `packages/domain/src/shared/time.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 times in an evidence pack say GMT or BST` (the repeated October hour read both ways) | Passing (#60 review round 1) |
| `apps/api/test/evidence-packs.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 the bundle goes when the link ends` (a discarded draft's bundle deleted; the sweep deletes an expired link's bundle once, audited; an ended link cannot be issued again) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 part 2a) |
| `apps/api/test/evidence-link.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 the rendered pack is watermarked and holds no third party's name`; `hazard HZ-050 the evidence pack link opens only for its recipient's own session` (unknown token, codes as an HMAC, wrong codes counted and never reset, the cookie's flags, the cap on open codes, revocation); `hazard HZ-050 one-time codes lock the link for good` (five wrong codes, a session for one link opens no other) | Passing (slice 11 part 2c) |
| `packages/db/test/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 a link session reaches its own link alone (migration 0058)` | Passing (slice 11 part 2c) |
| `apps/api/test/evidence-link.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 the printed pack` (every page carries the header with the release time in GMT or BST; a correction and a paper back-entry labelled, readings with units, the correction reason withheld; refused once the bundle is gone, audited `bundle_deleted`); the rendered pack states it shows the records as they stood at release (H-222); `packages/db`: a link session reads no audit trail, staff or grants and cannot revoke its link | Passing (#66 review round 1) |
| `apps/api/test/evidence-pack-pdf.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 the evidence pack renders` (from a fixed manifest and bundle, no database: records in time order; each value, or the reason it is withheld or redacted, in words, and a withheld value never printed; a field with no value said, never blank; the watermark relabelled with the composed and released times and the footer on every page of a long pack; an unreleased pack says so; a well-formed PDF whose page tree matches its pages, the same bytes for the same manifest; the title, recipient and each record's heading in order) | Passing (Phase 1 gate; in CI run 38938760784 on `d77b416`, 4 tests; 6 tests run locally on 31/10/2026) |
| `packages/db/test/evidence-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 evidence pack items, release and links in the database` (no values in items; append-only items; hand redactions in the redactor's name; no release by anyone who shaped the pack or of an empty pack; links to the recipient alone, within the maximum, replacing the last; hashed tokens and the token lookup; failed attempts never go down; codes used once; bundles changed only in a draft and deleted for good); `hazard HZ-011 evidence pack isolation and scope` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 schema) |
| `apps/web/e2e/evidence-pack.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 the manager composes a pack, adds a note and redacts it, but cannot release it` (no "everyone": people chosen one by one; free text withheld until redacted; a copy holding a long number refused; the composer not offered release); `hazard HZ-050 the owner, stepped up, reads what will be shared, releases it and sees the link once` (expiry in words before release; the read-through confirmed; the token only in the fragment); `hazard HZ-050 the manager withdraws the pack and its link stops`; axe in light and dark themes. `apps/web/src/lib/evidence-packs.test.ts` (link states, expiry words, who is offered release and a new link) | Passing (`feat/web-evidence-sop`) |
| `apps/web/e2e/evidence-pack.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-050 the inspector opens the link in their own browser with an emailed code and downloads the pack` (a fresh browser with no sign-in; the token out of the address bar at once and in no request URL; a code that does not match refused at the field; the watermarked PDF downloaded with the link's session); `hazard HZ-050 a withdrawn link says so in the inspector's browser and offers no code`; axe in light and dark. `apps/web/src/lib/evidence-link.test.ts` (the token only from the fragment; refusals in the page's terms) | Passing (`feat/web-evidence-sop`, the public page) |

## HZ-051 A letter or email carries another person's details, or wording that was not approved

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-051 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]`; data protection lead consulted |
| Engineering owner | `comms` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 15.1 (templates and letters: "merge-field letters and emails (admission, fee review, incident follow-up, duty of candour) with approval and audit"); regulation 20 of the 2014 Regulations (candour letters); [comms.md](../specs/comms.md) rules 18 to 26; [ADR-0051](../decisions/0051-personal-data-in-template-emails.md) |

**Description**: a letter or email generated from a template merges details that belong to a different person (another resident's name, fees, incident or candour account), goes to another person's contact, leaves a blank where a fact should be, carries health details by email, or uses wording that nobody with authority approved, so a family acts on wrong information or learns about someone else.

**Causes**: values gathered for the wrong person or from a stale screen; a recipient picked from another person's contacts; a missing value merged as an empty string; a template edited after approval; the author approving their own template; an unknown or mistyped merge field rendered as text; special category fields allowed in email; markup in a value breaking an HTML email; a duty of candour letter missing the apology or the account required by regulation 20(4). Added in the slice 11 review: a line break or hidden character in an email subject (header injection, or a subject that reads differently from what it says); an address field merged into a subject; an incident or candour letter set up as an email; the NHS number or date of birth in an email; a candour letter sent to someone who is not the relevant person under regulation 20(7); a first candour letter with the results left blank, or no follow-up once they are known; letter text treated as markup by the PDF renderer. Added in #72's review: a letter sent that differs from what the sender checked (a new version approved or a value changed after the preview); a removed contact or one past their authority's end chosen as recipient; an incident involving several people whose account names another resident; an email sent twice or sent but not recorded when sending and recording are separate steps.

**Effects**: a family told the wrong fee, the wrong account of an incident or the wrong apology; another person's health details disclosed (breach, distress); a candour obligation not met; decisions (for example about a fee or a complaint) taken on wrong information.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Every person-bound merge value must belong to the person the letter is about, and the recipient must be that person's contact, or the letter is refused (`renderTemplate` in `packages/domain/src/comms/templates.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | A missing or blank value refuses the letter; values are type-checked and formatted (dates, pounds and pence); unknown or malformed fields are refused at authoring | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | Only an approved version renders; nobody who wrote or edited a version approves it; approved versions never change, so any change is approved again (`checkTemplateApproval`, `checkTemplateEdit`, `TEMPLATE_WORKFLOW`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | The database holds the same line (migration 0059): versions move only along `TEMPLATE_WORKFLOW`, approval never by the author or an editor, approved versions fixed, a letter only from an approved version on its channel, to the person or one of their own contacts (a composite key), in the sender's scope, append-only | Phase 1 (built, slice 11 schema) |
| Design | No personal data in email subjects and no special category data in email bodies; every value and the template's text escaped for HTML; a duty of candour template must contain the regulation 20(4) contents (`checkTemplateDefinition`; ADR-0051) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | Email subjects refuse C0 and C1 control characters, U+2028 and U+2029 and bidirectional controls, in the template and again after merging, and refuse multi-line fields such as `{{location.address}}`; incident follow-up and duty of candour templates are letters only; the NHS number and date of birth never go in an email body (`hasUnsafeHeaderCharacter`, `LETTER_ONLY_KINDS`, `checkTemplateDefinition`; [comms.md](../specs/comms.md) rule 20) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | A duty of candour letter renders only for the relevant person on the candour record, and a representative only on a regulation 20(7) basis (`checkCandourRelevantPerson`); `candour.results` alone may say the results are to follow, with standard words, and the letter is marked so the follow-up stays due (`resultsToFollowText`; comms.md rule 21; wording and reading for the CSO and a solicitor, H-103) | Phase 1 (domain rules built, slice 11) |
| Design | Letter text is passed to the PDF renderer (Typst) as data, never as markup (comms.md rule 24) | Phase 1 (API step) |
| Design | Before sending, the sender sees the person's name and photo (HZ-001), the recipient and the flagged personal-data fields; the letter, template version and fields used are stored and audited | Phase 1 (API and web steps) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: starter wording reviewed by the provider's adviser; candour letters signed off by the Registered Manager | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/templates.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 refuses to merge details that belong to another person`; `hazard HZ-051 refuses a recipient who is another person's contact`; `hazard HZ-051 refuses a person-bound value with no person`; `hazard HZ-051 needs the person the letter is about` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/templates.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 refuses a letter with a missing value rather than leaving it blank`; `hazard HZ-051 only an approved version can be used` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/templates.test.ts`: approval and separation of duties, fixed approved versions, email data classes, HTML escaping, the regulation 20(4) contents | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/templates.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 email subjects cannot carry hidden characters or several lines` (`refuses a rendered subject whose value smuggles in a line break`); `hazard HZ-051 health details and identifiers stay out of email` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/templates.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 duty of candour letters` (results to follow with and without a date; only in `candour.results`; the recipient must be the relevant person; the regulation 20(7) bases) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11) |
| `packages/db/test/letter-templates.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 template versions in the database` (no email for a letter-only kind; a subject only on an email; approval never by the author or an editor; approved versions fixed; the same words not approved again; superseding and retiring); `hazard HZ-051 letters in the database` (only from an approved version on its channel; only to the person or one of their own contacts; only in the sender's scope; append-only; the retention class tied to the incident grade) | Passing (slice 11, schema: migration 0059) |
| `apps/api/test/letter-templates.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 letter and email templates` (drafts saved with their problems, no personal details in an email subject and no health details in an email, letter-only kinds, approval never by the author or an editor, approved versions fixed, the same words not approved again, retirement); `hazard HZ-051 refuses an approval when an edit lands between its read and its write` (#70 review: every version write matches the version the caller loaded); an incident follow-up as email refused and audited; no template seen across organisations | Passing (slice 11 part 2a) |
| `apps/api/test/letters.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 letters from approved templates` (comms.letter.manage with MFA on every route; values from the record, typed fields only for admission and fees; the preview shows the person; a send that differs from the preview refused (a new version approved in between, a tampered hash); only current contacts; another person's contact and incident refused; an incident involving another person refused, audited; email refused until the worker-based send, audited; duty of candour, a person who has died, out of scope, another organisation and a missing safety.incident.manage refused; a letter stored as a PDF document about the person with field keys only; never content in the list) | Passing (slice 11 part 2b, #72 review round 1) |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-051 a letter's values print as text in the PDF` (as built the ADR-0040 writer takes text only, so nothing typed can run; a dedicated test with markup-like values remains) | Planned |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-051 a letter cannot be made with another person's details` | Covered by `letters.test.ts` (another person's contact, another person's incident) |
| `apps/web/e2e/letter-templates.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 a draft shows each problem at its field, and its author cannot approve it`, `hazard HZ-051 someone else approves it; a new draft is discarded and the version retired, each with a reason`, `hazard HZ-051 an approver edits only behind Edit draft, the form follows the stored words, and stale writes are refused` (axe on every state, including both refusals); `apps/web/src/lib/letters.test.ts` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 web part 1) |
| `apps/web/e2e/letters.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-051 hazard HZ-001 a letter is checked in a preview that shows who it is about, then made`, `hazard HZ-051 a letter changed since the preview is shown again to check, and email is refused in words` (axe on every state) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 web part 2) |

## HZ-060 Health details in an email, SMS or push notification to a family member

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-060 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]`; data protection lead consulted |
| Engineering owner | `comms` module (family notifications); `integrations` (adapters) |
| Source | Master prompt 9.1 (push, email and SMS bodies never carry personal data), 15.1, 15.3 (communications); [comms.md](../specs/comms.md) section 4 rules 13 to 15 |

**Description**: a notification telling a family member about a new update carries something about the person's health or care (a fall, an infection, a score, a hospital visit, the person's name with an event) in an email subject, an SMS or a push notification, where it can be read on a lock screen, a shared phone or tablet, a work inbox, a smartwatch or by the SMS gateway and push services, before or without the family member signing in.

**Causes**: a notification built from the update's text ("Ada had a fall today"); a template with merge fields for the person's name or the update; a template edited by staff without approval; a link that carries the person's id, name or the update in its query; a stale phone number or email address now held by someone else; an urgent-feeling event (a fall, a hospital admission) sent as a notification instead of a phone call; a mobile number shared by several family members.

**Effects**: the person's health or circumstances disclosed to someone who should not know (a safeguarding or financial abuse risk, loss of dignity and trust); a family member learning of a fall or a deterioration from a lock screen without support; a reportable personal data breach.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Content-free by construction: the planner is given the ids of new updates, never their content, so nothing an update says can reach a message (`planFamilyNotification` in `packages/domain/src/comms/notify-plan.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | The words are the latest approved template for the channel, the same for everyone: no placeholders; refused when not approved or withdrawn, when the outbound guard objects (NHS numbers, dates, email addresses, phone numbers, more than 80 characters), when it contains a clinical, event or wellbeing term, or when it names the person in any case or with lookalike letters (`checkFamilyNotificationTemplate`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | The link is the portal's updates page with no id, name, query or fragment (`familyUpdatesLink`); the detail is shown only after sign-in with MFA and under the sharing rules (HZ-022) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Every adapter rechecks the title with `outboundTitleProblem` before sending | Built in Phase 0 |
| Design | Channels are opt-in and verified; a bounce, complaint, unregistered device or opt-out stops the channel; a new address must be verified again (`channels.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | No notification ever carries an event: it holds only the approved template. The feed has no kind for deaths, discharges, falls or hospital admissions; staff-written words that announce one (`FEED_EVENT_TERMS`: a fall, hospital or A&E, an ambulance, a death, a stroke and similar) are refused unless a member of staff holding `comms.feed.publish` confirms with a reason (HZ-061), and nothing is published while the person is in hospital or an incident waits for next-of-kin contact. A held notification is re-checked before it goes and cancelled when nothing is left to tell (`recheckPendingNotification`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: staff tell next of kin about incidents, hospital admissions, deterioration and deaths by phone or in person under the tenant's policy; template approvers are trained to keep templates generic | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/notify-plan.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-060 sends only the approved words and a link with no ids, whatever the updates are` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/notify-plan.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-060 refuses a template with ...` (a score and the person's name, a diagnosis, a prognosis, the surname in lower case, a merge field, an NHS number, a date, a phone number, an email address, a long message, an event, a wellbeing claim) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/feed-text-review.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-060 and HZ-061 events and wellbeing claims need a manager's confirmation`; `notify-plan.test.ts`: `a waiting notification is checked again before it goes` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/notify-plan.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-060 skips a channel whose template is not allowed, and says why`; `hazard HZ-060 keeps every validated tool name out of templates` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `apps/api/test/adapters.test.ts`: `refuses a title that looks like personal data` (the port contract every adapter passes) | Passing (Phase 0) |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-060 the notification worker sends the approved template only` | Planned, Phase 1 (slice 12 API step) |
