## HZ-061 Family reassured by a stale or misleading update

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-061 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `comms` module (family feed) |
| Source | Master prompt 15.1 (family portal feed), 8 (plain English for family surfaces); [comms.md](../specs/comms.md) section 4 rules 3, 4, 7 and 12. HZ-002 covers staff reading stale data on an offline device; this hazard is about families reading the feed |

**Description**: a family member or representative reads the feed as a current, complete picture of how the person is, when it is old, selective or wrong: an update from days ago read as today's, routine activity updates while the person is unwell or in hospital, a cheerful sentence that says more than the record does, or an update later found to be wrong. They do not visit, ask questions or raise a concern, or an attorney does not take part in a decision, because the feed reassured them.

**Causes**: updates without the time they happened; an update written days later shown as new; phrasing that claims wellbeing ("Ada had a lovely day", "settled and happy"); a feed that looks like the whole record; a corrected or withdrawn update left showing, or silently removed after being read; few updates read as "all is well"; events that matter (a fall, a hospital admission, a change in needs) missing from a feed that only carries everyday items. Found in review: staff-written words that announce an event ("had a fall", "went to A&E", "died", "had a stroke") or claim how the person is ("settled and happy"), published before the family has been told by a person; everyday updates published while the person is in hospital, an incident waits for next-of-kin contact or a duty of candour step is open; a care plan update announcing end of life or a medicines change; an accompaniment to an emergency or unplanned appointment, or a visit still under way, shown as routine; a feed that empties without a word after a death or discharge.

**Effects**: concerns about neglect or deterioration raised late; an attorney or deputy not involved when they should be; distress and loss of trust when the family learns more; complaints.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Every entry shows when the thing happened, and when it was written if that was on a later UK day; the feed shows when the newest update happened; newest first (`buildFamilyFeed`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Every feed says: "These are everyday updates. They are not the full care record. The home will phone you about anything important." (`FEED_NOT_FULL_RECORD_STATEMENT`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Approved phrases say what happened, never how the person is, and are tested over every care plan section the feed announces (`FEED_PHRASES`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Staff-written words (shared notes, captions, activity names) that announce an event (`FEED_EVENT_TERMS`) or claim how the person is (`FEED_WELLBEING_TERMS`) are refused unless a member of staff holding `comms.feed.publish` confirms with a reason of at least `FEED_CONFIRMATION_MIN_REASON_LENGTH` characters; the update records who confirmed and why; clinical terms are refused whatever is confirmed (`checkFeedText`). The lists are a backstop behind the staff member's decision to share, not a complete vocabulary | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | Publishing holds: nothing is published for the person while they are in hospital, while an incident waits for their next of kin to be told, or while a duty of candour step is open (`feedPublishingHolds`, reading the candour workflow in `packages/domain/src/safety/candour.ts`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12; safety exposes the next-of-kin state in its schema step) |
| Design | Care plan sections outside a typed allow-list are never announced (`FEED_CARE_PLAN_DOMAINS`: end of life, medicines, skin, continence, cognition, mood, behaviour, pain, breathing, sexuality and relationships); accompaniments are composed only with a recorded purpose and only for planned appointments; a visit still under way is never composed (`ACCOMPANIMENT_PURPOSES`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | After a discharge or death the feed reads exactly as one with no decision in force (the same statement, no entries, no status-specific notice), so the portal never tells a family about a death or discharge before the home does; the domain's paused statement is not shown (#75 and #76 review); a closing message after the next of kin are told is for the CSO (H-110) | Phase 1 (slice 12 part 1) |
| Design | A wrong update is taken down with a reason; a family member who saw it sees that it was taken down, and a corrected update names the one it replaces (`checkFeedWithdrawal`, `FEED_WITHDRAWN_NOTICE`) | Phase 1 (domain rule built, slice 12) |
| Design | The family app mode shows its last sync time (as HZ-002 for staff) | Phase 4 (MOB-004) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the tenant's communication policy says what families are told by phone (incidents, hospital admissions, changes in needs, deterioration, deaths) and by whom; the family guide explains what the feed is and is not | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3** (kept at the initial rating until the CSO reviews the lists, holds and wording, H-110).

**Justification for level 3**: the feed exists to share everyday staff-written news (master prompt 15.1), and no word list can recognise every way of announcing an event or reassuring a family ("had a tumble", "a good day"); a manager may confirm wording that reassures more than the record supports; the publishing holds depend on the incident, candour and lifecycle records being up to date; and families may still read a run of everyday updates as all being well. The controls make the feed's limits visible and keep the commonest events and claims behind a deliberate, recorded decision; removing staff-written words altogether would remove what families value most. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/feed-visibility.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-061 lists open items newest first with when they happened and when they were added`; `hazard HZ-061 tells a viewer who saw an update that it was taken down, without its content`; `hazard HZ-061 does not count a withdrawn notice as the last update` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/feed-compose.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-061 approved phrases say what happened, never how the person is` (every phrase over every announced care plan section: no clinical, event or wellbeing words; the reading-age lint on one sample section); `hazard HZ-061 care plan sections announced in the feed` (every section against the allow-list; never end of life or medicines); `hazard HZ-061 holds every update while the person is in hospital` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/feed-compose-review.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-061 a fall`, `hazard HZ-061 an A&E visit`, `hazard HZ-061 a death`, `hazard HZ-061 a stroke`, `hazard HZ-061 pressure sores` (a clinical term, refused outright), `hazard HZ-061 settled and happy`, an ambulance and the police; `hazard HZ-061 publishes flagged words once a manager confirms, and records who and why`; `hazard HZ-061 refuses a confirmation ...` (without the permission, with a short reason, by nobody); `hazard HZ-061 never lets a confirmation publish a clinical term`; `hazard HZ-061 publishing holds` (in hospital, an incident waiting for next-of-kin contact, an open candour step); `hazard HZ-061 visits and accompaniments` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/feed-text-review.test.ts`: `hazards HZ-060 and HZ-061 events and wellbeing claims need a manager's confirmation` (and ordinary words that are not flagged: falling asleep, happy birthday, hospitality) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `packages/domain/src/comms/feed-visibility.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-061 pauses the feed with a neutral statement when the person is ..., never an empty feed` (discharged, deceased) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12) |
| `apps/api/test/feed-publish.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-061 publishing waits while the family must hear from a person first` (the real holds query: an open incident naming the person with the family not recorded as told holds, and stops holding once they are; a closed incident never holds; an open duty of candour step holds and a terminal one does not; a hospital stay holds) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12 part 1b) |
| `apps/api/test/feed-publish.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-061 refuses a note or activity older than the feed's two weeks, audited` (`FEED_SOURCE_MAX_AGE_DAYS`, 422 `source_too_old`, also a problem in the preview) and `hazard HZ-061 refuses a record corrected between the preview and publishing` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12 part 2a) |
| `apps/web/e2e/family-updates.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-022 hazard HZ-061 an update is written for the family, checked in a preview, published once and taken down` (publishing sends exactly the request previewed, with a new id for each preview; an update already published with other words is said as a problem, never "published") and `hazard HZ-061 words to confirm, a photo without consent, a record corrected after the preview and a hold are said, and nothing is published` (Playwright with axe on every state) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12 part 2a) |
| `apps/web/e2e/family-feed.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-022 hazard HZ-061 the family sees only what a decision opens, a taken-down notice, and a closed feed like no decision` (the statement and the time checked on an empty feed; an update under a decision with the last update time; the taken-down notice in place of an update seen; after the decision is withdrawn the page reads word for word as with no decision; no people list or location switcher in a portal session; the MFA notice and the not-linked page; Playwright with axe on every state) | Passing (Phase 1, slice 12 part 2b) |

## HZ-070 Full tenant export disclosed beyond the organisation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-070 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]`; data protection lead consulted |
| Engineering owner | `platform` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 5.1 (free full export, delivered by expiring link), 5.3 (step-up for data export); [platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rules 10 to 15 |

**Description**: the organisation's full export, which holds every person's record and documents and its staff data, reaches someone it should not: through a forwarded or leaked link, a download by someone other than the person who asked for it, a file kept long after its purpose, or a copy left on a shared or unmanaged computer; or the export holds another organisation's records.

**Causes**: bearer links in emails or chat; links or files that never expire; downloads without signing in again; an owner who has left still able to download; long-lived or cached storage URLs; a stolen session; export files stored indefinitely; secrets or internal keys in the files; an export saved to a personal device; an export job that reads another organisation's rows (a query or role that bypasses row-level security, a missing tenant predicate, a shared storage path), so one organisation receives another's records; a cell that a spreadsheet runs as a formula when the file is opened.

**Effects**: disclosure of special category data for every person, including the whereabouts of people at risk (a safeguarding risk, for example to someone who has fled domestic abuse); a reportable personal data breach; staff data disclosed.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Only holders of `platform.export.create` (the owner) can ask for an export; it is a care-record permission, so MFA is required, and the request needs a sign-in completed with the second step within the step-up window; every request is audited | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Only the person who asked can download it, while they still hold the permission and have stepped up with MFA, while it is ready; the notification carries a pointer to that check, not a bearer link; the API streams the file or mints a storage URL that lasts 5 minutes | Phase 1 (domain rules built; API) |
| Design | The file is deleted `export.link_expiry_hours` after it is ready (tenant default 72, to verify) and never later than the ceiling `export.link_expiry_hours_max` (168, to verify), whatever the tenant sets; a new export is always free, so nothing is kept "in case" | Phase 1 (domain rules built; worker) |
| Design | Every column is classified `exported`, `internal` or `secret` in the catalogue keyed by `schema.table`; only the `public` schema and only exported columns are read, so passwords, sessions, authenticator secrets, secret references, storage keys and sign-in ids never leave; an unclassified table or column is never written and makes the export incomplete; quarantined files are never handed out | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | The export worker runs as `harbour_app` inside `withRequestContext` for the exporting organisation, so forced row-level security applies to every read; the manifest counts any row whose `tenant_id` differs from the exporting organisation's, which fails the export and raises a security alarm | Phase 1 (domain rules built; worker with the API step) |
| Design | Files are stored in the eu-west-2 export bucket with SSE-KMS under the organisation's own prefix (`tenants/<tenant>/exports/<export>/`) | Phase 1 (domain rules built; infrastructure) |
| Design | In CSV files a text cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab, a carriage return or an apostrophe gets a leading apostrophe so a spreadsheet does not run it; `schema.json` documents the guard and JSON Lines keep the exact value | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Every download is audited and visible to the owners and the data protection lead | Phase 1 |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: download only to a managed, encrypted device; delete local copies once used; the export guide says so | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/export.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-070 the export link expires and works only for the requester` (MFA step-up, expiry ceiling; the API streams the archive itself, once, rather than minting a storage URL), `hazard HZ-070 export request: free, MFA step-up, one at a time`, `hazard HZ-070 export plan reads only what may leave` (internal and secret columns, the `auth` schema and unclassified tables never planned), `hazard HZ-070 CSV cells cannot run as spreadsheet formulas`, `stores each export under the organisation's own prefix (HZ-070)`; `hazard HZ-071 an incomplete export is never offered` (a row from another organisation fails the export) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/access.test.ts`: `platform.export.create` touches care records, so MFA is required | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/export-worker.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-070 hazard HZ-071 building the full export` (the worker reads as `harbour_app` in the organisation's own context: the archive holds none of another organisation's ids, no secret or internal column and no `auth` table; the manifest counts foreign rows and fails the export with a security alarm if any) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/exports.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-070 asking for, listing and cancelling an export` (owner only, step-up to ask, one at a time, no other organisation, no storage key in responses), `hazard HZ-070 downloading an export once` (the requester's single fetch recorded before the bytes leave, the archive then deleted, a second fetch refused, an expired export refused); `packages/db/test/tenant-export.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-070 hazard HZ-011 the export record (migration 0062)` (holders and the worker only, one at a time, status forward only, the single download by the requester while ready) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/web/e2e/export.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-070 the owner asks for an export and downloads it once, after the warning` (only the owner, with a passkey step-up; the warning that the file can be downloaded once and holds everyone's records; downloaded once, then no download offered); the manager is offered none of it; `apps/web/src/lib/exports.test.ts` (every state as an icon and a word, refusals where the person can act) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/export-worker.test.ts`: archives past their expiry are deleted and `platform.export.expired` recorded only once the archive is gone; an export left running is failed and its archive deleted; `exports.test.ts`: a downloaded or cancelled export's archive is deleted by the worker from the outbox event | Passing (Phase 1) |

## HZ-071 Full tenant export incomplete or misread when a provider changes system

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-071 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `platform` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 5.1 (full export in a documented schema; DSCR data migration standard); DPA clause 9.1; [data-standards.md](../specs/data-standards.md); [platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rules 10, 11, 14 and 16 |

**Description**: a provider moving to another system relies on Harbour's export, but records are silently missing from it or are misread, so the new system lacks some people's allergies, resuscitation decisions, medicines or care plans.

**Causes**: a new table or column left out of the export; rows dropped by a fault; a truncated file; documents not copied or corrupted; held or deleted records handled inconsistently; codes whose meaning is not documented; destroyed records indistinguishable from missing ones; sample data mixed in; files read at different moments, so one table disagrees with another; a correction and the entry it corrects read as two current facts; a date-only fact (a date of death or discharge) read from its UTC instant, a day early after 23:00 in summer; numbers without units (millilitres or litres, kilograms or stones); an allergy status of unknown read as no allergies; resuscitation records that conflict reduced to one; the receiving system's import mapping errors.

**Effects**: care given without essential information after the move (as HZ-007 and HZ-015), for example an allergen given or a resuscitation decision not known; an incomplete legal record. The export covers every person the organisation cares for, so one misread file can affect many people at once.

**Initial risk**: severity Catastrophic, likelihood Medium, **risk 4**. Re-rated after the clinical review: a fault in a tenant-wide export can harm multiple people, which the plan's severity scale rates Catastrophic, not Major.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The catalogue (`EXPORT_TABLES`) keys every table by `schema.table` and classifies every column, with a description and a unit for every number; tables holding care records describe every column explicitly; `schema.json` is generated from the same plan as the files; a test against the Drizzle schema fails the build when a table or column is unclassified or has gone, and at run time an unclassified one makes the export incomplete | Phase 1 (domain rules and catalogue test built) |
| Design | The manifest records row counts and SHA-256 for every file and document; a missing table or file, a row count that does not match, a current summary without one row per exported person, or a document whose bytes differ from the checksum recorded at upload marks the export incomplete, so it is failed and retried, never offered | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Every file is read in one `REPEATABLE READ` read-only transaction, so all tables are from one moment, recorded in the manifest as `snapshotAt` in UTC and UK time; a "changes since this export" export holds only rows created, changed, recorded, deleted or destroyed at or after an earlier snapshot | Phase 1 (domain rules built; worker with the API step) |
| Design | `schema.json` carries the conventions a reader needs: a correction is a new row naming the row it corrects and the current value is the row nothing supersedes; instants are UTC with the UK offset stated; a date-only fact stored as an instant gets a `<column>_uk_date` column; recurrence times are Europe/London wall-clock times; every number has its unit, and observation values have a schema per chart; allergy status codes with their meaning (`unknown` never means none) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | `current_summary.csv` gives one row per person with the essentials as Harbour shows them, worked out by the same functions as the point-of-care header and the transfer pack: allergy status and allergies most severe first, the resuscitation decision in force (or `conflicting` with every record in force), IDDSI levels and communication needs | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Documented row rules: records under legal hold always in; soft-deleted and sample rows left out and counted; destroyed records listed with their certificate ids; quarantined files keep their rows | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | Field meanings and code lists in [data-standards.md](../specs/data-standards.md), versioned with `EXPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION` (1.1.0) | Phase 1 (built) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1, Phase 2 |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: the receiving provider reconciles row counts against the manifest and checks allergies, resuscitation decisions and medicines person by person before relying on the new system; the export guide gives the steps | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Catastrophic, likelihood Very low, **risk 3**. Until the CSO accepts that the controls bring the likelihood to Very low (H-119), the residual risk is recorded as Catastrophic / Low / **4**, which blocks a production release.

**Justification for level 3**: severity stays Catastrophic, because one bad import still reaches every person. The new controls lower the likelihood: an export that is incomplete, mixes moments or holds an unclassified column is never offered; the meaning of every column, correction, date and unit is in the export itself; and the essentials (allergies, resuscitation, IDDSI, communication) arrive pre-computed, one row per person, so misreading them needs the receiving system to ignore both the schema and the current summary. Level 3 is the lowest the matrix gives a Catastrophic hazard, and further reduction is impractical because the receiving system's import and mapping are outside Harbour's control; that remaining risk is managed by the receiving provider's reconciliation (deployer control). `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/export.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-071 the export catalogue describes every table and column`, `hazard HZ-071 which rows go into the export` (including changes since a snapshot), `hazard HZ-071 an incomplete export is never offered` (missing files, row counts, current summary, document checksums, foreign rows), `hazard HZ-071 schema.json gives every column its meaning` (conventions, units, codes, derived UK dates) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/export.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-071 UK dates and times in the export` (dates of death after 23:00 in summer, the autumn overlap), `hazard HZ-071 observation values carry their units and a schema per chart`, `hazard HZ-071 the current summary is worked out as Harbour shows it` (unknown allergy status, conflicting resuscitation records, IDDSI, communication needs) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/db/src/export-catalogue.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-071 the export catalogue matches the Drizzle schema` (every public table and column classified, nothing stale, every exported column described and every number given a unit, nothing planned outside `public`) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/export-worker.test.ts`: every file is read in one `REPEATABLE READ` read-only snapshot (`snapshotAt` in the manifest), the manifest complete, one current-summary row per exported person, both files for every planned table; `zip-writer.test.ts`: every file reads back byte for byte with its CRC-32, ZIP64 past 65,535 files; the legal-hold table (`held`) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-071 an export round-trips through the import wizard with matching counts` | Planned, Phase 2 (with PLAT-014) |

## HZ-072 Sample data mistaken for, or mixed with, real records

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-072 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `platform` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 9.1 (sample data mode); [platform-completions.md](../specs/platform-completions.md) rules 29 to 32 |

**Description**: while an organisation is getting started, invented sample people or records are taken for real ones, real care is recorded against a sample person, or a sample incident is treated as a real one.

**Causes**: sample people who look real; a sample telephone number or address that belongs to a real person; sample and real people in the same lists; real records arriving by import, integration, enquiry or TUPE transfer while sample data exists; a real note, incident or document linked to a sample person; sample data left in place at go-live; a notification, alert, escalation, handover, downtime pack, print, clock, report or export that does not check the sample flag; a removal that deletes real rows.

**Effects**: a real person's note, observation or administration recorded against a sample person and missing from their record; a wrong-person decision (HZ-001); a false statutory notification; misleading reports.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Sample people are plainly invented: family name "Example", 999-range NHS numbers that pass the check digit, `example.invalid` email addresses, telephone numbers in Ofcom's drama ranges (to verify, H-123) and invented addresses (an "Example" street, no postcode or the ZZ99 pseudo-postcode area) | Phase 1 (domain rules built) |
| Design | A sample person carries a "Sample" badge on every screen, and any print showing sample data carries the watermark "SAMPLE DATA: NOT A REAL PERSON" | Phase 1 (domain rules built; screens and prints) |
| Design | Sample data loads only before any real person exists, and real records wait until it has gone by every creation path (manual entry, enquiry, import, integration, TUPE transfer), so sample and real people never share a list | Phase 1 (domain rules built; for people, enforced in the database by the `person_sample_guard` trigger whichever role writes, one batch at a time, serialised per organisation; migration 0069) |
| Design | A record about sample people is sample; a record linking sample and real records is refused | Phase 1 (domain rules built; a person never changes between real and sample, `sample_flag_fixed`; records about sample people are removed with them, so practice never leaves the organisation stuck; sample flags on those records for the other surfaces still to come) |
| Design | Sample records take part in no statutory notification or clock, return, export, report, bill, message, integration, in-app alert, escalation chain, handover, downtime pack or bulk print (`SAMPLE_DATA_TREATMENT`, `forSurface`); a central outbound guard refuses any email, SMS, push, webhook or partner message about a sample record | Phase 1 (domain rules built; the full export leaves sample people out. Not yet called elsewhere: a sample incident can still draft a statutory notification, which the onboarding page tells the owner to decide not required (H-452); the downtime pack, letters and evidence packs mark a sample person "(Sample)" instead of leaving them out, `apps/api/test/downtime.test.ts`, `letters.test.ts` and `evidence-pack-sample.test.ts` `hazard HZ-072 ...`) |
| Design | Removal is one all-or-nothing step through a narrow security-definer function owned by a no-login role, deleting only the organisation's rows flagged sample in that batch in one transaction; the application role never holds DELETE; "sample data removed" blocks go-live on the onboarding checklist; a banner shows on every screen while sample data exists | Phase 1 (`app.remove_sample_batch`, owned by `harbour_sample_owner`, migration 0069, removing the practice records with the people, an active legal hold stopping it, a fresh step-up; `POST` and `DELETE /v1/onboarding/sample-data`; the checklist item reads the records; sample people left out of the full export; banner and badge with the web work) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | Onboarding guide: what sample data is and when to remove it | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `packages/domain/src/platform/onboarding.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-072 sample data is flagged, never mixed with real people, and removable in one step` (invented names, numbers, telephone numbers and addresses; every surface's treatment; badge and watermark; the outbound guard; every creation path refused while sample data exists; all-or-nothing removal) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `packages/db/test/sample-data.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-072 sample data is never mixed with real people (migration 0069)` (loads only with the permission and in the loader's name; refuses sample people beside real ones and real people beside sample ones, from any role; the flag never changes; removal only through the function, whole batch or nothing, audited, leaving every real row and the other organisation's; practice records (allergy, contact, note, chart, released hold) removed with the people and counted; an active hold or a record outside the people's own stops it; the append-only triggers still refuse every other delete; soft-deleted people still count; REPEATABLE READ refused) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/sample-data.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-072 sample data mode (rules 29 to 32)` (permission; refused while real people exist; once only; a real person waits; a note, a chart, an allergy, a document and a released hold practised on a sample person; step-up; removed in one step with them, the document's stored file deleted from storage; a real person may then be added; audited, refusals too) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api`: `hazard HZ-072 a sample incident drafts no statutory notification` | Planned: not true today (the classifier does not look at the sample flag); suppressing a statutory draft is a clinical-safety decision for the CSO (H-452). Until then the onboarding page says a draft may appear and is to be decided not required |
| `apps/api/test/downtime.test.ts`, `letters.test.ts`, `evidence-pack-sample.test.ts`; `packages/domain/src/operations/downtime-pack.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-072 ...` (a sample person is marked "(Sample)" on the downtime pack, a letter's check and PDF, and an evidence pack) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/web/e2e/sample-data.spec.ts` (Playwright with axe on every state): load after a confirmation; the banner on every screen; the "Sample" badge, an icon and a word, on each sample person in the people list and on their record; "Sample data is removed" blocking go-live while a batch is loaded; removal confirmed, refused in words without a fresh step-up, then done; `apps/web/src/components/people/PersonHeaderCompact.test.tsx` and `src/lib/people.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-072` badge and name marker | Passing (Phase 1) |
| Maestro `hazard-HZ-072-sample-banner.yaml`: the sample data banner on every screen | Planned, Phase 1 (mobile sync paused; the people streams carry `is_sample`) |

## HZ-073 Safety work or essential information hidden from the person who must act because of their access scope

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-073 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `platform` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | [ADR-0071](../decisions/0071-data-scope-is-per-permission.md) review rounds 1 to 5 (clinical safety reviews of #27, C-M3, C-M4, C2-M1 to C2-M4, C3-M1 to C3-M4, C4-M1 and C5-M1 to C5-M3); related to HZ-011 (the same scope, read the other way), HZ-014 (urgent results), HZ-018 (handover) and HZ-021 (essential information) |

**Description**: a task, alert, handover item or synced record that someone must act on is routed to, or meant for, a member of staff whose access scope does not let them see it, so the work waits unseen while everyone assumes it is in hand. Data scope is per permission (ADR-0071), so a member can hold the permission to act at a home without the permission to open the person's record there, or the reverse.

**Causes**: a task about a person assigned (by a manager, or automatically by an escalation fallback) to someone who cannot open that person's record, since being a task's assignee does not show a task about a person (`app.task_visible`); a notice sent to the nurse in charge, or to the holders of a permission, who cannot open the task's person or reach its location with `people.person.read`; a role bundle the organisation edits so that a routing role (the clinical lead, team leader or Registered Manager) loses `people.person.read`, or the work's own permission, at the same place; a sync stream family narrower than the role's work on the device (a coordinator with the care reference but no recent entries); a handover item about nobody that a reader's scope for `care.handover.manage` does not show; a grant or role with no location that no longer reaches anywhere after the organisation-wide mark (review round 1); a notice filtered by a permission the recipient does not need to be aware of, so a senior carer in charge at a home with no nurse is not told (review round 2, C2-M1); a role holder who has left, or whose role has ended, counted as the one told, so no fallback runs (C2-M4); a screen or phone that offers a form the API will refuse, so care is written into a refusal instead of being handed on (a record opened by break-glass, a coordinator's phone; C2-M2 and C2-M3), on any tab of the record (a NEWS2 or MUST entered on a read-only record stores no score and raises no alert; C3-M1), or that withholds one the API would take (a coordinator who is also a carer; C3-M2); an escalation, or an escalation chain step, marked done although no one was told, because its order stopped short or its only recipient has never signed in (C3-M3, C3-M4); an escalation marked done when only the assignee, who has already let the task go overdue, was told (C4-M1); a gap audited but seen by no one outside the audit (C4-M1); an alarm the runbook names but no infrastructure defines (C5-M1); a worker run that stops at the first organisation whose part fails, so the rest are never escalated (C5-M2); an overdue statutory notification skipped silently when no active member of staff can submit it (C5-M3); a sync task's own assignee or a break-glass opener counted as the one told (review round 5); a stored downtime pack hidden from the location's holders once someone on it has moved away, so the latest pack the nurse in charge can open is older or none (#32 review round 1, clinical M1; HZ-021, H-200).

**Effects**: an urgent review, overdue round, deterioration response, ReSPECT review or hold on a device not acted on (HZ-014, HZ-003, HZ-006); a handover item missed (HZ-018); essential information missing at the point of care (HZ-021).

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | A task about a person is given only to someone who can see the person with both `people.person.read` and the task's permission, whoever assigns it (`mayBeGiven`, the assign and create routes), and the escalation's holders and fallback route it only to such a person (a Registered Manager at another home who cannot open the record is skipped); with no one, the gap is audited (`sync.escalation_unrouted`) rather than the task marked escalated | Built in Phase 1 (fix/platform-scope-per-permission, review round 1) |
| Design | A holder told about a task must also reach the task's place with `people.person.read`, so the task is in their list | Built in Phase 1 (review round 1) |
| Design | One definition of the nurse in charge (`nurseInCharge`: the clinical leads and team leaders whose roles reach the home, at every home, with no rota checked, else its Registered Manager, each with a role in force and active) serves urgent result alerts (HZ-014, HZ-016), care plan and risk review notices and refused-entry reviews, each filtered only by what the recipient can see (`nurseInChargeSeeing`), never by a permission to act they do not need for awareness. A refused entry's urgent review goes to the nurse in charge whose `people.person.read` and `care.note.read` reach the person, and to the holders of `care.plan.write` who can see the person, to act (those who hold it at the home itself, and only with none of them those who hold it across the organisation, so an owner is not told about every home; HZ-008); with neither, the fallback; with no one at all, it is audited as unrouted (`care.refused_entry.unrouted`) | Built in Phase 1 (review rounds 2 and 3, C2-M1) |
| Design | Everyone told or given work by role holds that role now: an assignment in force, a role neither archived nor deleted, where the role reaches the location; everyone told about something is active (an invited member who has never signed in, a suspended one or one who has left is never the one told; review round 3), and a member suspended or gone holds nothing at all; so a team leader who has left is never the only one told, and the Registered Manager fallback runs (overdue escalation, Registered Manager lookups, escalation chains, break-glass alerts, the fallbacks). A fallback about no one prefers a manager, else a holder, whose role reaches the location. A new round keeps its series' assignee only while they could still be given it | Built in Phase 1 (review rounds 2 and 3, C2-M4 and lows) |
| Design | An overdue task goes down its order until someone other than its assignee is told: the clinical leads and team leaders whose roles reach its home; else a Registered Manager whose role reaches it (there or across the organisation); else the holders of `care.plan.write` who can see the person (for a task about no one, who reach the location). A task about a person with no location takes the person's, and each tier keeps only those who can see the person. With no one other than the assignee, the assignee is still told, but the task is not marked escalated, the gap is audited (`task.escalation_unrouted`, coded `assignee_only`, `no_one_sees_person` or `no_one_reaches_location`; on the first occurrence, then at most once a day) and an hourly run tries again (review round 4, C4-M1). An escalation chain step whose named member and role holders are none goes to a Registered Manager whose role reaches the home, else the owner; one that can reach no one is audited (`platform.escalation.unrouted`, `no_recipient`), never passed over silently. A break-glass alert no one active can be sent is audited (`people.break_glass_alert.unrouted`). Neither a sync task's own assignee nor the person who opened a record by break-glass counts as told: with no one else, the sync task's assignee is still told, the task stays due and `sync.escalation_unrouted` is audited (`assignee_only`), and the break-glass gap is audited (`opener_only`). The unrouted audit is written at most once a day per task, action and reason, and the hourly run retries every unrouted task, oldest first, in pages. The minute run, the hourly retry and the daily report each go on to the next organisation when one organisation's part fails, audited as `platform.job.tenant_failed` (review round 5, C5-M2 and lows) | Built in Phase 1 (review rounds 3 to 5, C3-M3, C3-M4, C4-M1 and C5-M2) |
| Design | The operator sees every gap: once a day the worker writes one `escalation.unrouted.daily` log line per organisation with any unrouted audit event in the last 25 hours (opaque tenant id and counts per kind, overdue statutory notifications no active member of staff can submit included, `safety.notification.unrouted`), and a CloudWatch alarm defined in Terraform (`infra/modules/observability`: a metric filter on the worker's log group, `{ $.event = "escalation.unrouted.daily" }`, any line in a day) sends it to the alerts topic; a second alarm fires on any failed worker job or failed organisation's part of a sweep (`worker.job.failed`). The operator tells the organisation the same working day ([on-call runbook](../runbooks/on-call.md)). The alarms are live once the Terraform is applied (H-171). Whether the organisation needs its own signal in the product is H-170 | Built in Phase 1 (review rounds 4 and 5, C4-M1 and C5-M1); live once applied (H-171) |
| Design | No form is offered where the API would refuse the entry, and none withheld where it would take it: the person's record lists the permissions reaching the person without break-glass (`access.reaching`), and every tab of the record on the web (notes, charts, body map, activities and visits, assessments, the care plan, risk assessments, ReSPECT and advance care) offers its form only where its write permission is listed, saying in words when the record is open for reading only (break-glass, or roles that reach the person for reading only); the phone offers recording only where the sync token's `entries_write_*` claims say it may, deciding per person for the clients assigned to the user (`entries_write_person_ids`; above 100 people the token names none and the phone applies the location rule, failing closed where the home syncs for reading only), and to no one while no scope is known (before its first token, or when where it may record could not be kept, removed or read), saying so and that a sync fixes it, never guessing from the user's permissions (review round 5); the charts tab says entries cannot be recorded, never that the record cannot be, above chart settings the viewer can still change (review round 5) | Built in Phase 1 (review rounds 2 to 5, C2-M2, C2-M3, C3-M1 and C3-M2) |
| Design | An urgent result alert goes only to someone whose `people.person.read` reaches the person, and in the care plan tier whose `care.plan.write` reaches the person too (HZ-014) | Built in Phase 1 (#26, review round 1) |
| Design | Every role key the services assign work to by name (`assigneeRoleKey`) and the Registered Manager fallbacks hold `people.person.read` and the work's permission in their built-in bundles; the roles told as the nurse in charge hold `people.person.read`, and for each review a nurse in charge is told about, at least one of them (the clinical lead) holds its permission, while the senior carer may not, so the holders of it are told too (a table-driven test reads the routing sites from the code) | Built in Phase 1 (review rounds 1 and 2) |
| Design | Each family of sync streams follows its own read permissions (the person only where the care reference syncs too, so the phone never shows a resuscitation decision or IDDSI level as not recorded when it is merely absent) and the release note says what each built-in role syncs; devices are synced before the deployment that narrows scope | Built in Phase 1 (review rounds 1 and 2) |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: review role bundles before go-live, with the pre-deployment access report ([runbook](../runbooks/data-scope-per-permission-access-report.md)), so that each person who must act on safety work holds the permission to open the record at the same place (DCB0160 template, HZ-011 row); cover at another home is a role assignment at that home until grants can be managed | Phase 1 |
| Design | On the web home page (INSI-001, `feat/web-dashboards`, docs/specs/insights.md): a tile is hidden only when the API refuses the reader the permission outright; a location outside one permission's scope (`not_found`) is a failure for that location ("Some not loaded" or "Could not load"), so safety work never disappears because of scope; with more than three locations and none chosen, the per-location tiles say "Choose a location" rather than a count | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3** (an engineering proposal; the CSO rates it and accepts it, H-169).

**Justification for level 3**: the organisation designs its own role bundles and decides who covers where; Harbour routes only to people who can see the work, tells whoever can act and audits a gap, but a bundle that leaves no one at a home able to see or act cannot be put right by software. Further reduction rests on the deployer control below. `[CSO acceptance - human action]`

| `apps/api/test/scope-per-permission.test.ts`: `refuses to give a task about a person to a care worker not assigned to them`; `skips a Registered Manager at another home in the fallback for a task about a person` | Passing (Phase 1) |

## HZ-090 An incident form records an assumed answer, or asks a notification question in other words

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-090 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `web` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 11.1; safety.md sections 4 and 5; #42 review round 1 (`emergencyServicesCalled` and `gpContacted` default to false in the API) |

**Description**: the web incident forms send an answer nobody gave (whether the emergency services were called, whether the GP was contacted, whether the person died, a falls question), or show a classifier question in words that differ from the rule it feeds, so the answer is given to a different question.

**Causes**: a yes or no question pre-set to "no", or the API's default used when the form leaves a field out; the catalogue's wording drifting from the domain's question set (which quotes the regulations); an option or question the catalogue does not know left out of the form.

**Effects**: a notification not drafted (HZ-013) or a post-fall checklist started on a wrong answer (HZ-027); a record that says nobody was called when nobody was asked.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The emergency services, GP and (for an incident about a person) death questions are yes or no with nothing chosen; the form refuses until each is answered, tied to the field, and its hint says Harbour never assumes no | Phase 1 (slice 9) |
| Design | When it happened is chosen every time, with nothing chosen at first: "just now" (taken on the server when the form arrives, never from the browser's clock) or "earlier" with the date and time, so post-fall observations and statutory clocks start at the time someone gave (#55 review round 1) | Phase 1 (slice 9) |
| Design | The falls questions on a fall need an answer each, "not sure yet" included; the incident page's questions are sent only when answered (a blank one stays open, never sent as no) | Phase 1 (slice 9) |
| Design | The catalogue's words for every question, its guidance and every option repeat the domain's question set word for word (a unit test compares them); a question or option the catalogue does not know is shown in the API's English, marked as English, never left out | Phase 1 (slice 9) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | The CSO reviews the wording of the questions and the yes or no prompts (H-261) | Before first real tenant |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2** (engineering proposal; the CSO rates it, H-261).

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/web/src/lib/incident-forms.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-090 has catalogue words for every question, its guidance and every option`, `hazard HZ-090 shows a question or option the catalogue does not know in the API's English, marked`, `hazard HZ-090 never assumes the time: just now or earlier is chosen every time`, `hazard HZ-090 never assumes no: emergency services and the GP are asked every time`, `hazard HZ-090 asks whether the person died when it is about someone`, `hazard HZ-090 reading an answer: not sure is an answer, blank is not` | Passing |
| `apps/web/e2e/incident-forms.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-027 hazard HZ-090 the carer records a fall from a resident's record, never assumed no` (the refusal summary names each unanswered question) | Passing (web journeys with axe in CI on every pull request and on `main`; run 38932679711 on `5b3d238`) |

## HZ-091 An incident is closed, or a notification recorded as submitted or not required, on a wrong or stale page

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-091 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `web` (lead engineer) |
| Source | Master prompt 11.1 and 11.2; safety.md sections 3 and 5 (rule 11); STATUS item 19 (part 4b, `feat/web-incident-review-submit`); related to HZ-013 and HZ-090 |

**Description**: a manager's web form ends the tracking of a statutory duty wrongly: an incident closed by the person who raised it or with a notification still open, a notification recorded as submitted when it was not (or with a time nobody gave), "not required" decided on a page that no longer shows the notification as it is, or a prepared form marked ready with required content missing.

**Causes**: a form offered to someone the API would refuse, so they believe it is done; a page drawn before another manager's change (or before the person's own earlier attempt, saved after a lost connection) sent again; a submission time pre-set to now; the required content keyed so an answer lands under the wrong line; a refusal shown in codes or blaming someone else, so it is ignored; the step-up sign-in losing what was typed, so it is entered again in haste.

**Effects**: the overdue alerts stop for a notification the regulator never received (HZ-013); an incident closed without independent review (rule 11); an audit trail that records a decision nobody made.

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Each form only where the API would take it: review and close only for `safety.incident.manage` on an incident seen whole and not closed, close never to the person who raised it (`availableTransitions` with the domain's `INCIDENT_WORKFLOW`); prepare, submit and "not required" only where the permission reaches the person it is about (`access.reaching`, ADR-0071); "record as submitted" only on a ready notification | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | While a notification is a draft or ready or a question is open, the incident page lists what to do first, each notification linked, instead of a close form; the API's refusals (open notifications, questions, the candour record, a step the workflow does not allow, a finished notification, the submission evidence) are recognised and said in the catalogue's words, tied to their fields | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | Every write carries the version the page was drawn from, so a stale page is refused; the refusal says the record changed since the page opened, perhaps through the person's own earlier attempt, never that someone else changed it, and to reload | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | "Not required" on a notification Harbour found needed (neither provisional nor no longer indicated) says so beside the form, takes a reason of at least 20 characters and a ticked confirmation; a provisional draft keeps the short reason (#58 review round 1; the CSO confirms, H-265) | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | When it was submitted is chosen every time ("just now" taken on the server, or earlier with the date and time, never in the future) and the regulator's reference is required; "not required" needs a reason of at least 3 characters, kept and audited; both need a fresh sign-in, and the step-up notice says what was typed is not kept | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | Each answer in the prepared form is stored under the line of required content it answers, so it is found again; a notification is marked ready only with every line answered | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | After each form the page opens again with focus on its heading and says what was done, with an icon and words | Phase 1 (slice 9, part 4b) |
| Design | The approvals page (`/approvals`, PLAT-011) offers closing an incident, or sending it back to open, only when the API marks the incident as waiting for the reader's approval. That is never for the person who raised it, who is told in words that someone else must approve. It then uses the incident page's own rules (`incidentManageView`), forms and server actions with the version the page read: what must be done first links to the incident, and a stale page is refused as on the incident page. Every other workflow's step is taken on the record's own page | Phase 1 (built 31/10/2026) |
| Test | See Verification | Phase 1 |
| Training and user documentation | The CSO reviews the forms' wording and whether a submission may be recorded without the regulator's reference (H-265, H-266) | Before first real tenant |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2** (engineering proposal; the CSO rates it, H-265).

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/web/src/lib/incident-review.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-013 hazard HZ-091 never offers close to the manager who raised it`, `hazard HZ-091 never says someone else changed it after a conflict, which may be your own retry`, `hazard HZ-091 announces what was done only while the status still matches`, `hazard HZ-013 hazard HZ-091 needs a longer reason and the confirmation ticked`, and `hazard HZ-091 readSubmitForm (HZ-090: no time assumed)` (nothing chosen; `hazard HZ-091 allows no time after now, as the API allows none`; `hazard HZ-091 refuses a time skipped when the clocks went forward in March`; `hazard HZ-091 asks which time in the hour the clocks went back in October`); the `notificationManageView (ADR-0071)` table | Passing |
| `apps/web/src/lib/refusals.test.ts`: `closing an incident and the notifications (HZ-013)` (every refusal run in the domain and recognised); `apps/web/src/components/safety/ManageForms.test.tsx`: `hazard HZ-091 says to sign in again and links back to the page, accessibly`, `hazard HZ-091 says the separation of duties refusal in words a manager can act on, accessibly` and `hazard HZ-091 shows the step-up notice for a step-up refusal, and nothing on success` (axe; the journey cannot reach these two states) | Passing |
| `apps/web/e2e/incident-review.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-013 hazard HZ-091 the nurse who raised an incident is never offered close`, `hazard HZ-013 hazard HZ-091 the manager is refused in words on a stale page, then closes it`, `hazard HZ-013 hazard HZ-091 the manager prepares a death notification and records it as submitted` and `hazard HZ-013 hazard HZ-091 what to do first, then not required on a stale page is refused` (what to do first with the draft linked, empty, error, refused and done, each checked with axe) | Passing (web journeys with axe in CI on every pull request and on `main`; run 38932679711 on `5b3d238`) |
| `apps/api/test/workflows.test.ts`: the person who raised an incident is never offered the approval and is told so; another manager is; an incident at a home the reader's role does not reach, and another organisation's workflow, are never listed. `apps/web/src/lib/approvals.test.ts` (who sees the page, the groups, every state and deadline as an icon and words, an unknown one as a warning, links to each record's own page, closing offered only for an incident waiting for the reader). `apps/web/e2e/approvals.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-013 hazard HZ-091 another manager closes one here and sends the other back` (the raiser offered no close, refused in words without a reason, closed, sent back; axe in the light and dark themes) | Passing (local, 31/10/2026) |

## HZ-080 A revised statement of purpose not sent to CQC in time

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-080 |
| Status | Open (whether it is in DCB0129 scope at all is for the CSO, H-224) |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `quality` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | [quality.md](../specs/quality.md) rule 15 and section 14; CQC (Registration) Regulations 2009 regulation 12(3); #61 review round 1 |

**Description**: an approved revision of the statement of purpose is not sent to CQC within
`cqc.statement_of_purpose.revision_notice_days` (28) UK days, because nobody was told, the alert
did not say which version or by when, or the due date moved with a clock change.

**Causes**: no alert when the due date passes; an alert to someone who cannot record the
submission; an alert in vague words; the due date worked out as 28 times 24 hours across a clock
change; the alert sent again and again, or not at all after a failed run.

**Effects**: a regulatory breach for the provider; people choosing a service from an out-of-date
statement (for example a location or registered manager that has changed). No direct harm to a
person receiving care.

**Initial risk**: severity Minor, likelihood Medium, **risk 2**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The database sets the due date on approval in UK wall-clock days (migration 0057); the hourly job tells every active holder of `safety.notification.submit` once, urgently, through the overdue chain, naming the version and the due date, and audits the gap daily when nobody can be told; recording the submission acknowledges the alerts | Phase 1 (built, slice 11 part 2b) |
| Training and user documentation | The words of the alert, a "due soon" reminder and who receives it (H-219) | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Minor, likelihood Low, **risk 1**.

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/api/test/statement-of-purpose.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-080 tells the holders of safety.notification.submit once, naming the version and the due date, when the revision is overdue`; `pins the 28 UK days across the October clock change`; the due date at approval matches 28 UK days to the minute | Passing (#61 review round 1) |
| `apps/web/e2e/statement-of-purpose.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-080 the manager drafts a revision with its changes and prompts, and cannot approve it`; `hazard HZ-080 the owner approves the revision, sees the CQC clock and records it as sent` (the clock as an icon and a word with the due date from the API, never a literal; the submission recorded with step-up); axe in light and dark themes. `apps/web/src/lib/statement-of-purpose.test.ts` (`hazard HZ-080 puts every revision still to reach CQC first, the earliest due at the top`) | Passing (`feat/web-evidence-sop`) |

## HZ-092 Paper records confirmed as reviewed at reconciliation without being found

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-092 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `operations` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | Slice 11 web (`feat/web-downtime`): the reconciliation screen on `GET /v1/outages/{id}/reconciliation`, which returns entry ids, people and counts only; related to HZ-017 (back-entry), HZ-004 (the same care recorded twice) and HZ-021 |

**Description**: at reconciliation, the manager must review each possible duplicate group and each back-entry with a time outside the outage and no reason. The API gives only the entries' ids, so the screen lists them by a short reference. A manager ticks them as checked without finding them in each person's record, and the outage is reconciled with the same fluid intake counted twice, or a paper record entered against the wrong time.

**Causes**: no link from a reference to the entry and its person; many entries to check after a long outage; time pressure; a reference mistyped or not found in the record's history.

**Effects**: a fluid or food chart double-counted, or care shown at the wrong time, after an outage (HZ-017, HZ-004); decisions taken on that chart.

**Initial risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Each group and each entry is a separate box to tick, with what it is in words ("possibly the same care recorded twice", "a time outside the outage and no reason"), how many entries and their references; the outage cannot be reconciled until every box is ticked and the manager says what was checked, and the API refuses the same again | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web) |
| Design | Someone who entered or sent any paper record from the outage is told another manager who can manage outages, such as the Registered Manager, must reconcile it, and offered no form (H-203) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web) |
| Design | Every record list shows a paper record's reference (the last 8 characters of its id, the current version's for a corrected note) beside "Entered from a paper record", the same reference the reconciliation lists; the reconciliation says to look in the care record of each person at the location, and that an entry recorded in Harbour at the time has no reference and is the one for the same person at about the same time (#62 review round 1) | Built in Phase 1 (slice 11 web) |
| Design | The reconciliation names the person, the entry's kind and time, and links each entry to its place in the record, so it can be opened and kept or corrected from the screen | Planned (Lane A: the API returns ids only; STATUS item 21; the CSO decides whether reconciling goes live before it, H-267) |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: reconcile with the paper forms in hand, find each listed entry in the person's record history before ticking it (DCB0160 template) | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Considerable, likelihood Low, **risk 2** (an engineering proposal; the CSO rates it, H-267).

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/web/src/lib/downtime.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-092 reconciling an outage` (`hazard HZ-092 lists each possible duplicate group and each back-entry outside the window`; `hazard HZ-092 sends the reviewed ids, once each, only when every item is ticked`; `hazard HZ-092 gives the short reference the record shows beside the paper label`); `apps/web/src/components/care/EntryBadges.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-092 shows the reference the outage's reconciliation lists, beside the paper label` | Passing (Phase 1, slice 11 web) |
| `apps/web/e2e/downtime.spec.ts`: `hazard HZ-092 the nurse finds and checks each possible duplicate and late paper record before reconciling` (a duplicate pair and a record after the true end entered by the manager; the reference on the person's notes; each box by its full name; one left unticked is refused with focus on the summary; axe) | Passing (web journeys with axe in CI on every pull request and on `main`; run 38932679711 on `5b3d238`) |
| Playwright: each entry opened from the reconciliation screen, with the person, kind and time | Planned (with the API change above) |

## HZ-110 A server-side sign-out clears a carer's offline records during a shift

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-110 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `platform` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | The admin sign-out and the idle timeout (platform.md, Identity; branch `feat/platform-session-controls`, #40: `apps/api/src/routes/member-sessions.ts`, `apps/api/src/http/context.ts`); related to HZ-006 (silent sync failure, loss of offline records), HZ-021 (essential information), HZ-040 (held entries) and HZ-111 (the browser side of the idle timeout) |

**Description**: the server ends a Care mode session (a manager signing the member out, or an idle timeout if it applied to the phone), and at its next contact the phone clears what it had synced, keeping only unsent entries (HZ-006). The carer loses the care plan, allergies and other reference data on the device until they sign in again, which may be impossible without signal, and entries wait longer to reach the record.

**Causes**: a server idle timeout applied to the mobile app's session, so a shift without signal ends in a sign-out; a manager signing out a member mid-shift without knowing their phone holds entries or that it will lose its records; signing out the wrong member, one at another home, or one who is working for another organisation at the time; held entries on the phone of someone who has left, never sent because they never sign in again.

**Effects**: essential information missing at the point of care (HZ-021); entries reaching the record late, or never (HZ-006).

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The server's idle timeout never applies to the mobile app's session (a request with its bearer token); the device's own auto-lock covers it (ADR-0011). The idle timeout is off altogether unless the environment turns it on (HZ-111) | Built in Phase 1 (`feat/platform-session-controls`, `apps/api/src/http/context.ts`) |
| Design | The admin sign-out needs `platform.user.manage`, a fresh sign-in and a coded reason; it is audited before the sessions end, and every refusal is audited by opaque id; a location role reaches only members whose roles and grants are all at its homes | Built in Phase 1 (`feat/platform-session-controls`, `apps/api/src/routes/member-sessions.ts`) |
| Design | A member who also works for another organisation in Harbour is never signed out (409 `member_of_other_organisation`, in plain words), because their sessions are their sign-in's, not one organisation's | Built in Phase 1 (`feat/platform-session-controls`, #40 review M1) |
| Design | When the member's devices last reported entries waiting, the sign-out is refused until the manager confirms (409 `entries_waiting`), saying that signing out removes the care record from their phone until they sign in again and that unsent entries stay on the phone until then | Built in Phase 1 (`feat/platform-session-controls`, #40 review M2) |
| Design | For a member signed out because they have left or been suspended, the holds on their devices fall due at once and the manager is told, as after a device revocation (`hurryRevokedHold`) | Built in Phase 1 (`feat/platform-session-controls`, #40 review M2) |
| Design | A device whose session the API has ended keeps unsent entries, encrypted, for their owner to send after signing in again (HZ-006, `held-work.ts`) | Built in Phase 1 (#15) |
| Design | The sign-out confirmation says how old the devices' last report is | Planned (STATUS item 18) |
| Training and user documentation | Deployer control: sign a carer out mid-shift only for a lost or stolen device, a departure, a suspension or suspected misuse, and tell them to sign in again before going on with care (DCB0160 template) | Phase 1 |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2** (an engineering proposal; the CSO rates it, H-351).

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/api/test/flags-and-sessions.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-110 hazard HZ-111 idle timeouts per role, when turned on`: `never signs out the mobile app's session, which the device lock covers` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/flags-and-sessions.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-110 a manager signs a member of staff out of every device`: `hazard HZ-110 asks before signing out a member whose phone holds entries, then signs out every session and chases the hold`; `hazard HZ-110 never signs out someone who also works for another organisation`; `hazard HZ-110 reaches only members wholly at the manager's homes, and audits a refusal`; `hazard HZ-110 needs platform.user.manage and a fresh sign-in` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/mobile/src/app-state/held-work.test.ts`: `when the API ends the session (401 or 403)` (unsent entries kept, the rest cleared) | Passing (Phase 1) |

## HZ-111 A browser idle timeout loses an unsaved care entry

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-111 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `platform` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | #40 clinical review round 1 (High); the idle timeout in `apps/api/src/http/context.ts` and the web's sign-in redirect (`apps/web/src/lib/problems.ts`); related to HZ-110 and HZ-006 |

**Description**: a nurse half-way through a note, observation or assessment on the web leaves the tab for longer than the idle timeout. On return, the person record header's refresh on focus (`HeaderFreshness`), or the form's own submit, meets 401, the page goes to sign-in, and what was typed is lost; the care is not recorded, or is recorded later from memory.

**Causes**: an idle timeout enforced by the server with no warning before it expires; a sign-in that unmounts the form; refreshes on focus that reach the server before the person can act.

**Effects**: a care entry, observation or escalation not recorded or recorded late and inaccurately (HZ-006, HZ-014).

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | The idle timeout is off unless the environment turns it on (`SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT`), and production refuses to start with it on, so it introduces no loss until the controls below exist and the CSO and DPO have decided (H-353) | Built in Phase 1 (`feat/platform-session-controls`, `apps/api/src/config.ts`) |
| Design | A warning before expiry, as WCAG 2.2 success criterion 2.2.1 asks, with "Stay signed in", a request the person makes that counts as use | Planned (before the timeout is turned on) |
| Design | Signing in again in place without unmounting the form, so what was typed survives; no care text is written to browser storage | Planned (before the timeout is turned on) |
| Design | Background refreshes never count as use, while the idle check still applies to them | Planned (before the timeout is turned on; platform.md prerequisites) |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Very low, **risk 2** (an engineering proposal; the CSO accepts it, or blocks the timeout until entries are kept, H-353).

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/api/test/flags-and-sessions.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-111 the idle timeout is off unless the environment turns it on` (`never signs out an idle browser session with the default configuration`); `apps/api/test/config.test.ts`: `refuses the browser idle timeout (HZ-111) in production` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| Playwright: a form half-filled, the warning shown before expiry, "Stay signed in" keeps the session; after expiry, signing in again in place keeps the typed text and stores none in the browser | Planned |

---

## HZ-112 Care records opened without a second factor, or staff shut out of them when the rule tightens

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| ID | HZ-112 |
| Status | Open |
| Clinical owner | `[CSO name - human action]` |
| Engineering owner | `platform` module (lead engineer) |
| Source | #43 review M5; `fix/platform-mfa-session-factor` (`apps/api/src/auth/session-factor.ts`, `apps/api/src/auth/better-auth.ts`, `apps/api/src/http/context.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/problems.ts`); ADR-0072; related to HZ-006 (offline records), HZ-110 and HZ-111 |

**Description**: MFA counted when a member merely had a passkey or authenticator app enrolled, so a stolen or guessed password alone opened care records for a passkey holder. Fixing it changes who can open records: every session from before the deploy is refused care records until the person signs in again with their passkey or authenticator code, and a nurse refused mid-shift who is sent to a page that cannot help (the enrolment page for a factor they already have) cannot reach a care plan, medicines information or an escalation note when they need it.

**Causes**: MFA judged from the account's enrolment rather than the session; a deploy that tightens the rule while staff are signed in; a refusal that sends people to the wrong next step; the mobile app not yet knowing the new refusal code.

**Effects**: personal and clinical information disclosed to someone holding only a password; or care delayed while staff cannot open the record (HZ-006, HZ-014).

**Initial risk**: severity Major, likelihood Medium, **risk 3**.

**Controls**

| Type | Control | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Design | Once a member has a factor, adding, showing or removing one (`/two-factor/enable`, `disable`, `get-totp-uri`, backup codes, passkey registration and deletion) needs a session that used one, so a password alone cannot enrol the attacker's own factor; only a first enrolment goes ahead. Wrong codes on an existing session are limited to five (#57 security review round 1) | Built in Phase 1 (`apps/api/src/auth/better-auth.ts`) |
| Design | MFA counts for care records (and the operator console) only when the session signed in, or confirmed a code on the session, with a passkey, an authenticator code or a backup code; a password-only or trusted-device session does not count, and a session with no record fails closed (ADR-0072) | Built in Phase 1 (`fix/platform-mfa-session-factor`) |
| Design | A person with a factor enrolled is told `mfa_verification_required` and the web sends them straight to sign in with it (`/sign-in?reason=mfa`, keeping where they were going), not to the enrolment page; anything that is not a care record keeps working | Built in Phase 1 (`apps/web/src/lib/problems.ts`, sign-in page) |
| Process | The release note says every signed-in person will be asked to sign in again with their passkey or code before opening care records; deploy outside shift handover and tell staff beforehand (H-358) | Before the first real tenant |
| Design | The mobile app reads `mfa_verification_required` as a session code (signs in again, keeping unsent entries under the hold rules) rather than as withdrawn access | Built (`fix/mobile-mfa-verification-session`, `apps/mobile/src/sync/session-signals.ts`): on the sync token and on any upload, so a care entry is never parked as refused for it |
| Design | Step-up (controlled drugs, residents' money, export, break-glass, statutory notifications, downtime pack, evidence pack release and every other step-up permission) counts from the second factor the session last used, within `STEP_UP_WINDOW_SECONDS`, never from a password sign-in; a holder with no factor enrolled is told `mfa_enrolment_required`; confirming a code on the session refreshes it; freshness is judged on the database's clock; the code that completes a first enrolment records no factor, so a password holder cannot enrol a factor and step up in one session (ADR-0072 amendment; #63 security review) | Built in Phase 1 (`fix/platform-step-up-factor`) |
| Process | Owners make sure every member holding a step-up permission has a passkey or authenticator app before go-live, and staff are told step-up actions now ask for it (H-359) | Before the first real tenant |

**Target residual risk**: severity Major, likelihood Low, **risk 3** until the mobile control is built, then Very low, **risk 2** (an engineering proposal; the CSO decides, H-358).

**Verification**

| Test | Status |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/api/test/mfa-session-factor.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-112` cases (a passkey holder's password-only session is refused with `mfa_verification_required`; a passkey session is allowed; removing the passkey refuses again; a wrong code records nothing; a code confirmed when enrolling, on an existing session and at a new sign-in is allowed; a session with no record is refused) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/web/src/lib/problems.test.ts` (`mfa_verification_required` goes to `/sign-in?reason=mfa` with the next path) and `apps/web/src/lib/outcome.test.ts` | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/api/test/mfa-session-factor.test.ts`: a password-only session cannot enrol TOTP or a passkey or delete the passkey; a session that used its passkey may add one; a session is refused after five wrong codes; `apps/web/src/lib/problems.test.ts` (`factorChangeRedirect`) | Passing (Phase 1) |
| `apps/mobile/src/sync/session-signals.test.ts`: `classifyForbidden` reads `mfa_verification_required` as a session code; `apps/mobile/src/sync/connector.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-006 treats a 403 mfa_verification_required from the sync token as an ended session`, `hazard HZ-006 ends the session on a 403 mfa_verification_required instead of parking the entry as refused` | Passing |
| `apps/api/test/step-up-factor.test.ts`: `hazard HZ-112` table over every step-up permission (password-only, factor inside and outside the window, none enrolled), each step-up route over HTTP, an in-session code refreshing step-up, a backup code and the five-code limit under step-up, no factor enrolled, About me links, residents' money at exit and task links over HTTP, and no API or domain source giving step-up a sign-in time; `mfa-session-factor.test.ts`: a first enrolment's code records no factor; `apps/web/src/components/safety/ManageForms.test.tsx` (the step-up notice asks for the passkey or authenticator code) | Passing (Phase 1) |

## Candidate hazards for the first CSO workshop

Not yet in the log (no IDs allocated). The CSO decides at the Phase 1 SWIFT workshop whether each becomes a hazard `[CSO decision - human action]`:

- Data import mapping errors beyond allergies and medicines (care-plan content, contacts, legal representatives).
- AI module output mistaken for clinical advice (Phase 6; scope lock in [medical-device-boundary.md](../compliance/medical-device-boundary.md)).
- Missed domiciliary visit not escalated (Phase 4).
